Minimal model program for normal pairs along log canonical locus
Kenta Hashizume

Abstract
. Let ( X , Δ ) (X,\Delta) be a normal pair with a projective morphism X → Z X\to Z and let A A be a relatively ample ℝ \mathbb{R} -divisor on X X . We establish the minimal model theory for ( X , Δ + A ) / Z (X,\Delta+A)/Z under assumptions that the non-nef locus of K X + Δ + A K_{X}+\Delta+A over Z Z does not intersect the non-lc locus of ( X , Δ ) (X,\Delta) and that the restriction of K X + Δ + A K_{X}+\Delta+A to the non-lc locus of ( X , Δ ) (X,\Delta) is semi-ample over Z Z .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
14E30

1.
Introduction

Throughout this paper, we work over the complex number field
\mathbb{C}
.

The minimal model theory is a fundamental tool to construct a variety with good geometrical properties.
The theory is indispensable to recent development of birational geometry, especially, partial resolutions of singularities, boundedness and finiteness of various invariants, and moduli problems.
For inductive arguments on the dimension of varieties, we usually deal with pairs of a variety (or a scheme) and a linear combination of divisors.
The minimal model theory is discussed in the category of log canonical (lc, for short) pairs.
It is expected that the full general statements of the minimal model theory hold for all lc pairs, and remarkable progress has been made (
[
BCHM10
]
,
[
F11
]
,
[
B12
]
,
[
HX13
]
,
[
HH20
]
).

There are research works on the minimal model theory in the framework beyond lc pairs.
Semi-log canonical pairs appear in the compactification of moduli of lc pairs and inductive arguments to study the abundance conjecture for lc pairs.
Partial results of minimal model theory for semi-log canonical pairs hold (
[
F14
]
,
[
AK17
]
).
On the other hand, generalized pairs, introduced by Birkar–Zhang
[
BZ16
]
, appear as the structures of base varieties of lc-trivial fibrations and normal pairs whose log canonical divisor is anti-nef.
The minimal model theory for generalized lc pairs is developed (
[
HL21
]
,
[
LT22
]
), and relation between the minimal model theory for generalized lc pairs and that for lc pairs is known (
[
LP20
]
).
Unfortunately, there are examples showing that the minimal model theory cannot be established in full generality for pairs
beyond lc pairs.
For example, we cannot construct a step of a minimal model program (MMP, for short) for semi-log canonical pairs in general (
[
F14
]
,
[
AK17
]
), and the abundance conjecture for generalized lc pairs does not hold in general.

In this paper, we discuss the minimal model theory for normal pairs that are not necessarily lc.
As shown in Section
6
, the existence of a step of an MMP, the existence of a minimal model, and the abundance conjecture do not always hold for normal pairs in general.
For a projective normal pair
(X,\Delta)
, to construct a step of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, we need the cone and contraction theorem and the existence of a relative lc model with respect to a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-negative extremal contraction.
The cone and contraction theorem for normal pairs (more generally, quasi-log schemes) have been established by Fujino
[
F17
]
, and the existence of a relative lc model with respect to a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-negative extremal contraction is a local problem (cf.
[
KM98
, Corollary 6.7]
) and known for lc pairs by Birkar
[
B12
]
and Hacon–Xu
[
HX13
]
.
Hence, if any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-negative curve does not intersect the non-lc locus of
(X,\Delta)
, then we can construct a step
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP.
If, furthermore, any
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime})
-negative curve is disjoint from the non-lc locus of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
, then we can construct a next step
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP.
By repeating this discussion, if
K_{X}+\Delta
has a special positivity around the non-lc locus of
(X,\Delta)
, we can run a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP.

The special positivity condition in the previous paragraph is satisfied if the non-nef locus (see Definition
2.9
) of the log canonical divisor is disjoint from the non-lc locus.
The non-nef locus of the log canonical divisor contains all curves whose intersection number with the log canonical divisor is negative (Theorem
2.12
), and any MMP does not modify the complement of the non-nef locus of the log canonical divisor.
For a projective normal pair
(X,\Delta)
, assuming that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
does not intersect the non-lc locus of
(X,\Delta)
, then we can always construct a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP (Corollary
3.11
).
Then it is natural to consider the termination of the
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP.
More specifically, we may consider the following question.

Question 1.1
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties. Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective. Suppose that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
over
Z
(Definition
2.9
) does not intersect the non-lc locus of
(X,\Delta)
.
Then, is there a finite sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z

(X,\Delta)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{m},\Delta_{m})

such that
K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}
is nef over
Z
?

This question is natural since we can give an affirmative answer under the assumption on the termination of all MMP for klt pairs (see Remark
3.14
).

The goal of this paper is to study the termination of the MMP for a normal pairs.
The following theorem is the main result of this paper.

Theorem 1.2

(cf. Theorem
5.3
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta+A
over
Z
does not intersect the non-lc locus
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
of
(X,\Delta)
. Suppose in addition that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is a finite
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-linear combination of
\pi|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
-globally generated invertible sheaves on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.
We put
(X_{1},B_{1}):=(X,\Delta+A)
.
Then there exists a diagram

\textstyle{(X_{1},B_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},B_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{m},B_{m})}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

over
Z
, where all varieties are normal and projective over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

For each
1\leq i<m
, the diagram
(X_{i},B_{i})\overset{\varphi_{i}}{\longrightarrow}W_{i}\overset{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}{\longleftarrow}(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})
is a usual step of a
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP over
Z
, in other words,
\varphi_{i}
is birational,
\rho(X_{i}/W_{i})=1
,
\varphi^{\prime}_{i}
is small birational, and
-(K_{X_{i}}+B_{i})
and
K_{X_{i+1}}+B_{i+1}
are ample over
W_{i}
,

•

(X_{1},B_{1})\dashrightarrow(X_{m},B_{m})
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}
is semi-ample over
Z
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

We note that the scheme structure of
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
in Theorem
1.2
is not defined to be the reduced scheme structure.
For the definition of
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
as a closed subscheme of
X
, see Definition
2.14
.
We also note that the definition of MMP in this paper is slightly different from the non-
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial MMP in
[
F17
, 4.9.1]
or
[
K21
]
.
For details, see Definition
3.4
and Remark
3.5
.
This is the reason why we explicitly state the properties of the diagram in Theorem
1.2
.

When the non-lc locus of
(X,\Delta)
in Theorem
1.2
is empty, the the statement follows from
[
HH20
, Theorem 1.5 and Theorem 1.7]
.
Thus, Theorem
1.2
is a generalization of
[
HH20
]
and
[
BCHM10
]
.
However, we use the results in
[
HH20
]
and
[
BCHM10
]
in the proof.
In the proof, we use the idea in
[
BCHM10
, Section 5 and Section 6]
.
Therefore, the semi-ampleness of
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
over
Z
is necessary for the termination of the
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP as well as the abundance for the minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
.

We close the introduction with corollaries.

Corollary 1.3

(= Corollary
5.5
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta+A
over
Z
does not intersect
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
and that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is a finite
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-linear combination of
\pi|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
-globally generated invertible sheaves on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.
Then the stable base locus
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
of
K_{X}+\Delta+A
over
Z
is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.

Corollary 1.4

(= Corollary
5.6
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.
We put
(X_{1},B_{1}):=(X,\Delta+A)
.
Then there exists a diagram

\textstyle{(X_{1},B_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},B_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{m},B_{m})}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

over
Z
, where all varieties are normal and projective over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

For each
1\leq i<m
, the diagram
(X_{i},B_{i})\overset{\varphi_{i}}{\longrightarrow}W_{i}\overset{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}{\longleftarrow}(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})
is a usual step of a
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP over
Z
(cf. Theorem
1.2
),

•

(X_{1},B_{1})\dashrightarrow(X_{m},B_{m})
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}
is semi-ample over
Z
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Corollary 1.5

(= Corollary
5.7
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
over
Z
does not intersect
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
.
Then there exists a diagram

\textstyle{(X,\Delta)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

over
Z
, where all varieties are normal and projective over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\overset{\varphi_{i}}{\longrightarrow}W_{i}\overset{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}{\longleftarrow}(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
is a usual step of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
(cf. Theorem
1.2
),

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

For applications of Corollary
1.5
, see Corollary
5.8
and Corollary
5.9
.

Corollary 1.6

(= Corollary
5.10
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
\Delta
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta+A
over
Z
does not intersect
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
and that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then the sheaf of graded
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra

\underset{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}{\bigoplus}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(\lfloor m(K_{X}+\Delta+A)\rfloor)

is finitely generated.

The contents of this paper are as follows: In Section
2
, we collect some definitions and basic results used in this paper.
In Section
3
, we prove fundamental results to run an MMP for normal pairs.
In Section
4
, we study the minimal model theory for normal pairs such that the non-nef locus of the log canonical divisor is disjoint from the non-klt locus.
In Section
5
, we prove the main result and corollaries.
In Section
6
, we collect some examples.

.

2.
Preliminaries

Throughout this paper, a
scheme
means a separated scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
.
A
variety
means an integral scheme, that is, an irreducible reduced separated scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
.

Divisors and morphisms
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism from a normal variety to a variety.
We will use the standard definitions of
\pi
-nef
\mathbb{R}
-divisor,
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor,
\pi
-semi-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor, and
\pi
-pseudo-effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor.
The set of
\mathbb{R}
-divisors on
X
is denoted by
{\rm WDiv}_{\mathbb{R}}(X)
.
For a prime divisor
P
over
X
, the image of
P
on
X
is denoted by
c_{X}(P)
.

A
contraction

f\colon X\to Y
is a projective morphism of varieties such that
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Y}
.
For a variety
X
and an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D
on
X
, a
log resolution of

(X,D)
is a projective birational morphism
g\colon W\to X
from a smooth variety
W
such that the exceptional locus
{\rm Ex}(g)
of
g
is pure codimension one and
{\rm Ex}(g)\cup{\rm Supp}\,g_{*}^{-1}D
is a simple normal crossing divisor.

A birational map
\phi\colon X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
of varieties is called a
birational contraction
if
\phi^{-1}
does not contract any divisor.
We say that
\phi
is
small
if
\phi
and
\phi^{-1}
are birational contractions.

Definition 2.1

(
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on scheme, relative ampleness, relative semi-ampleness)
.

Let
X
be a (not necessarily reduced or irreducible) scheme and let
{\rm Pic}(X)
be the Picard group of
X
.
A
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle on
X
is an element of
{\rm Pic}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism to a scheme
Z
.
A
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
is
\pi
-ample
or
ample over
Z
if
\mathcal{L}
is a finite
\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
-linear combination of
\pi
-ample invertible sheaves on
X
.
We say that a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
is
\pi
-semi-ample
or
semi-ample over
Z
if we can write
\mathcal{L}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}q_{i}\mathcal{L}_{i}
as an element of
{\rm Pic}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
such that
q_{1},\,\cdots,\,q_{k}
are positive rational numbers and
\mathcal{L}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\mathcal{L}_{k}
are globally generated over
Z
, in other words, the natural morphism

\pi^{*}\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}_{i}\to\mathcal{L}_{i}

is surjective for all
1\leq i\leq k
.

Similarly, we define an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
X
to be an element of
{\rm Pic}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
, and we say that an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
is
\pi
-ample
or
ample over
Z
(resp.
\pi
-semi-ample
or
semi-ample over
Z
) if
\mathcal{L}
is a finite
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-linear combination of invertible sheaves on
X
that are
\pi
-ample (resp. globally generated over
Z
).

Definition 2.2
.

Let
a
be a real number.
We define the
round up

\lceil a_{i}\rceil
to be the smallest integer not less than
a_{i}
, and we define the
round down

\lfloor a_{i}\rfloor
to be the largest integer not greater than
a_{i}
.
It is easy to see that
\lfloor a\rfloor=-\lceil-a\rceil
.

Let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on a variety, and let
D=\sum_{i}d_{i}D_{i}
be the decomposition of
D
into prime divisors.
We define

\begin{split}\lceil D\rceil:=\sum_{i}\lceil d_{i}\rceil D_{i},\qquad\lfloor D\rfloor:=\sum_{i}\lfloor d_{i}\rfloor D_{i},\qquad{\rm and}\qquad\{D\}:=D-\lfloor D\rfloor.\end{split}

We also define

D^{<1}:=\sum_{d_{i}<1}d_{i}D_{i},\quad D^{=1}:=\sum_{d_{i}=1}D_{i},\quad D^{>1}:=\sum_{d_{i}>1}d_{i}D_{i},\quad{\rm and}\quad D^{\geq 1}:=\sum_{d_{i}\geq 1}d_{i}D_{i}.

By definition, we have
\lfloor D\rfloor=-\lceil-D\rceil
.

2.1.
\mathbb{R}
-linear system

In this subsection, we define the relative
\mathbb{R}
-linear system and the relative stable base locus, and we prove some basic results.
Afterwards, we define the relative diminished base locus.
Almost all results in this subsection are analogue of the results proved in
[
BCHM10
, Subsection 3.5]
.

Definition 2.3

(Relative
\mathbb{R}
-linear system, relative stable base locus)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal varieties.
Let
D
be a (not necessarily
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier)
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
.
Then the
\mathbb{Q}
-linear system of
D
over
Z
, denoted by
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}
, is defined by

|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}:=\{E\geq 0\,|\,E\sim_{\mathbb{Q},\,Z}D\}.

The
stable base locus of
D
over
Z
, denoted by
{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}
, is defined by

{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}:=\bigcap_{E\in|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}}{\rm Supp}\,E.

If
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}
is empty, then we set
{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}=X
by convention.

For an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D^{\prime}
on
X
, the
\mathbb{R}
-linear system of
D^{\prime}
over
Z
, denoted by
|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, is defined by

|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}:=\{E^{\prime}\geq 0\,|\,E^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}D^{\prime}\}.

and the
stable base locus of
D^{\prime}
over
Z
, denoted by
{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, is defined by

{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}:=\bigcap_{E^{\prime}\in|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}}{\rm Supp}\,E^{\prime}.

If
|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
is empty, then we set
{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}=X
by convention.

We say that an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D^{\prime\prime}
on
X
is
movable over
Z
if the codimension of
{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
in
X
is greater than or equal to two.

Lemma 2.4

(cf.
[
BCHM10
, Lemma 3.5.6]
)
.

Let
X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal varieties and let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
which is movable over
Z
.
Then there exist positive real numbers
r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{l}
and effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
M_{1},\cdots,\,M_{l}
on
X
such that
D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}M_{i}
and
{\rm Bs}|M_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.
In particular,
M_{i}
is movable over
Z
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.

Proof.

The argument is similar to
[
BCHM10
, Proof of Lemma 3.5.6]
.
We fix elements
D_{1},\cdots,\,D_{p}
of
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}{\rm Supp}\,D_{i}={\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We prove Lemma
2.4
by induction on the sum of the numbers of components of the divisors
D_{1},\cdots,\,D_{p}
.
For each
2\leq i\leq p
, we consider the set

\mathcal{W}_{i}:=\left\{(E_{1},E_{i})\in{\rm WDiv}_{\mathbb{R}}(X)\times{\rm WDiv}_{\mathbb{R}}(X)\,\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}\text{$E_{1}\geq 0$, $E_{i}\geq 0$, $E_{1}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}E_{i}$,}\\
\text{${\rm Supp}\,E_{1}={\rm Supp}\,D_{1}$, and}\\
\text{${\rm Supp}\,E_{i}={\rm Supp}\,D_{i}$}\end{array}\right\}.

By the argument from convex geometry, we can find a rational polytope
\mathcal{C}_{i}\subset\mathcal{W}_{i}
that contains
(D_{1},D_{i})
.
Then there are effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
N_{1},\cdots,\,N_{p}
on
X
such that
(N_{1},N_{i})\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
for every
2\leq i\leq p
.
Then

{\rm Supp}\,N_{i}={\rm Supp}\,D_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq p
, and it follows that

N_{1}\sim_{\mathbb{Q},\,Z}N_{i}
for all
2\leq i\leq p
.
Since we have

{\rm Bs}|N_{1}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}{\rm Supp}\,N_{i}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}{\rm Supp}\,D_{i}={\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}},

it follows that
N_{1}
is movable over
Z
and
{\rm Bs}|N_{1}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We set

M_{1}:=N_{1}\quad{\rm and}\quad r_{1}:={\rm sup}\{t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$D_{i}-tN_{i}\geq 0$ for every $1\leq i\leq p$}\}.

Putting
D^{\prime}_{i}=D_{i}-r_{1}N_{i}
for each
1\leq i\leq p
and
D^{\prime}:=D-r_{1}M_{1}
, then the sum of the numbers of components of
D^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\,D^{\prime}_{p}
is strictly less than the sum of the numbers of components of
D_{1},\cdots,\,D_{p}
.
By construction, the relation
D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}D^{\prime}_{i}
holds for every
1\leq i\leq p
, and

{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}{\rm Supp}\,D^{\prime}_{i}\subset\bigcap_{i=1}^{p}{\rm Supp}\,D_{i}={\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

Thus
D^{\prime}
is movable over
Z
.
We apply the induction hypothesis to
D^{\prime}
.
Then we can find positive real numbers
r^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{q}
and effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
N^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\,N^{\prime}_{q}
on
X
such that
D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\sum_{j=1}^{q}r^{\prime}_{j}N^{\prime}_{j}
and
{\rm Bs}|N^{\prime}_{j}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
for every
1\leq j\leq q
.
Since we have
D^{\prime}=D-r_{1}N_{1}
and
{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, by renaming
r^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{q}
and
N^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\,N^{\prime}_{q}
we obtain positive real numbers
r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{l}
and effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
M_{1},\cdots,\,M_{l}
on
X
satisfying the conditions of Lemma
2.4
.
∎

Lemma 2.5

(cf.
[
BCHM10
, Proposition 3.5.4]
)
.

Let
X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal varieties and let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
.
Then there exist effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
M
and
F
on
X
satisfying the following.

•

D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}M+F
,

•

every component of
F
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and

•

we may write
M=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}M_{i}
for some positive real numbers
r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{l}
and effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
M_{1},\cdots,\,M_{l}
on
X
such that every
M_{i}
is movable over
Z
and
{\rm Bs}|M_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.

Proof.

Let
D_{1},\cdots,\,D_{k}
be elements of
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Supp}\,D_{i}={\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
For any prime divisor
P
on
X
, we define
r_{P}:=\underset{1\leq i\leq k}{\rm min}\,{\rm coeff}_{P}(D_{i})
, and we set

F:=\sum_{P}r_{P}P,

where
P
runs over prime divisors on
X
.
By construction,
F
is well defined as an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
and every component of
F
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We put

D^{\prime}:=D-F.

Then
D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}D_{i}-F
for all
1\leq i\leq k
, and construction of
F
implies that any prime divisor is not contained in
\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Supp}\,(D_{i}-F)
.
Hence,
D^{\prime}
is movable over
Z
.
We also have

{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Supp}\,(D_{i}-F)\subset\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Supp}\,D_{i}={\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

By applying Lemma
2.4
to
D^{\prime}
, we get positive real numbers
r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{l}
and effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
M_{1},\cdots,\,M_{l}
on
X
such that

•

D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}M_{i}
, and

•

M_{i}
is movable over
Z
and
{\rm Bs}|M_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.

Putting
M=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}M_{i}
, then
M
and
F
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
2.5
.
∎

Theorem 2.6
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties and let
\Delta
be a reduced divisor on
X
.
Let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
such that
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
.
Then there exist a log resolution
f\colon Y\to X
of
(X,\Delta)
and effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
M_{Y}
and
F_{Y}
on
Y
satisfying the following.

•

f^{*}D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}M_{Y}+F_{Y}
,

•

every component of
F_{Y}
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

M_{Y}
is semi-ample over
Z
, and

•

{\rm Supp}(F_{Y}+f_{*}^{-1}\Delta)\cup{\rm Ex}(f)
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
Y
.

Proof.

Replacing
X
by a resolution, we may assume that
X
is smooth.
By Lemma
2.5
, there exist effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
M
and
F
on
X
satisfying the following.

•

D\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}M+F
,

•

every component of
F
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and

•

we may write
M=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}M_{i}
for some positive real numbers
r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{l}
and effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
M_{1},\cdots,\,M_{l}
on
X
such that every
M_{i}
is movable over
Z
and
{\rm Bs}|M_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.

We take a positive integer
k
such that
kM_{i}
is Cartier for every
1\leq i\leq l
.
Since
Z
is quasi-projective, choosing
k
sufficiently large and divisible, we may assume

{\rm Bs}|M_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}={\rm Supp}\bigl({\rm Coker}(\pi^{*}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(kM_{i})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-kM_{i})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X})\bigr)

as Zariski closed subsets of
X
.
Define
\mathcal{I}_{i}:={\rm Im}(\pi^{*}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(kM_{i})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-kM_{i})\to\mathcal{O}_{X})
for each
1\leq i\leq l
.
We take a log resolution
f\colon Y\to X
of
(X,\Delta+F)
such that
\mathcal{I}_{i}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{Y}
is an invertible sheaf on
Y
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.
We can write
\mathcal{I}_{i}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{Y}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-E_{i})
for some effective Cartier divisor
E_{i}
on
Y
.
Since
{\rm Bs}|M_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{Q}}\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, every component of
E_{i}
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
By taking some blow-ups if necessary, we may assume that
{\rm Supp}(f_{*}^{-1}(\Delta+F)+\sum_{i=1}^{l}E_{i})\cup{\rm Ex}(f)
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
Y
.
Now we put

M_{Y}:=f^{*}M-\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}E_{i}\quad{\rm and}\quad F_{Y}:=f^{*}F+\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}E_{i}.

Then
M_{Y}=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}\bigl(f^{*}(kM_{i})-E_{i}\bigr)
.
Hence
M_{Y}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
By construction,
f\colon Y\to X
,
M_{Y}
, and
F_{Y}
satisfy the conditions of Theorem
2.6
.
∎

Finally, we define the relative diminished base locus.
We note that we only use the diminished base locus in the projective case as in
[
BBP13
]
,
[
TX23
]
. However, we define the relative version of the diminished base locus for the future use.

Definition 2.7

(Relative diminished base locus, cf.
[
BBP13
]
,
[
TX23
]
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism from a normal quasi-projective variety
X
to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
.
Let
D
be a
\pi
-pseudo-effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.
Then the
diminished base locus of
D
over
Z
, which we denote by
\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D/Z)
, is defined to be

\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D/Z):=\bigcup_{\epsilon>0}{\rm Bs}|D+\epsilon A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}

for a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
A
on
X
.
Note that
\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D/Z)
does not depend on
A
.
When
Z
is a point, we denote the diminished base locus over
Z
by
\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D)
.

2.2.
Non-nef locus

The goal of this subsection is to define the relative non-nef locus and prove some properties used in this paper.

Definition 2.8

(Relative asymptotic vanishing order,
[
N04
, III, §4.a]
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism from a normal quasi-projective variety
X
to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
.
Let
D
be a
\pi
-pseudo-effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.
For a prime divisor
P
over
X
, we define the
asymptotic vanishing order of
P
over
Z
, denoted by
\sigma_{P}(D/Z)
, as follows (
[
N04
, III, §4.a]
):
We take a resolution of
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
of
X
on which
P
appears as a prime divisor.
We put

m_{f^{*}D}:={\rm max}\left\{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\,\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}(\pi\circ f)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lfloor f^{*}D\rfloor-mP)\hookrightarrow(\pi\circ f)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lfloor f^{*}D\rfloor)\\
\text{is an isomorphism}\end{array}\right\}

if
(\pi\circ f)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lfloor f^{*}D\rfloor)
is not the zero sheaf.
When
D
is big over
Z
, then

\sigma_{P}(f^{*}D;X^{\prime}/Z)_{\mathbb{Z}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}+\infty&((\pi\circ f)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lfloor f^{*}D\rfloor)=0)\\
m_{f^{*}D}+{\rm coeff}_{P}(\{f^{*}D\})&((\pi\circ f)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lfloor f^{*}D\rfloor)\neq 0)\end{array}\right.

and
\sigma_{P}(D/Z)
is defined to be

\sigma_{P}(D/Z):=\underset{m\to\infty}{\rm lim}\frac{1}{m}\sigma_{P}(f^{*}D;X^{\prime}/X)_{\mathbb{Z}}.

Note that
\sigma_{P}(D/Z)
is independent of the resolution
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
.
If
D
is not necessarily
\pi
-big, then

\sigma_{P}(D/Z)=\underset{\epsilon\to 0+}{\rm lim}\sigma_{P}(D+\epsilon A/Z)

for a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
A
on
X
.
Note that
\sigma_{P}(D/Z)
does not depend on
A
, and we may have
\sigma_{P}(D/Z)=+\infty
for some
P
.

Definition 2.9

(Relative non-nef locus, cf.
[
N04
, III, 2.6 Definition]
)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism from a normal quasi-projective variety
X
to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
.
Let
D
be a
\pi
-pseudo-effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.
We define the
non-nef locus of
D
over
Z
, denoted by
{\rm NNef}(D/Z)
, to be

{\rm NNef}(D/Z):=\underset{\sigma_{P}(D/Z)>0}{\bigcup}c_{X}(P),

where
P
runs over prime divisors over
X
and
\sigma_{P}(D/Z)
is the asymptotic vanishing order of
P
over
Z
in Definition
2.8
.

Remark 2.10
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
P
, and
D
be as in Definition
2.8
.
Then

\sigma_{P}(D+H/Z)\leq\sigma_{P}(D/Z)

for every
\pi
-semi-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
H
on
X
.
This implies that

{\rm NNef}(D/Z)=\bigcup_{\epsilon>0}{\rm NNef}(D+\epsilon H/Z).

Remark 2.11

(
[
BBP13
]
,
[
TX23
]
)
.

By definition, we have

{\rm NNef}(D/Z)\subset\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D/Z)\subset{\rm Bs}|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}

for every
\pi\colon X\to Z
and
D
as in Definition
2.9
.
Furthermore, we have

{\rm NNef}(D/Z)=f({\rm NNef}(f^{*}D/Z))

for any projective birational morphism
f\colon Y\to X
from a normal variety
Y
.

In this paper we use the following results without mentioning explicitly.

Theorem 2.12
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
D
be a
\pi
-pseudo-effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.
Let
C
be a curve on
X
such that
\pi(C)
is a point and
C\not\subset{\rm NNef}(D/Z)
.
Then
(D\,\cdot\,C)\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a resolution of
X
such that
f^{-1}(C)
is pure codimension one.
Let
A
be an ample Cartier divisor on
Y
.
Since
C\not\subset{\rm NNef}(D/Z)
, by the same argument as in
[
N04
, Proof of III, 1.7 Lemma]
, for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
we see that
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D+\frac{1}{k}A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
does not contain any irreducible component of
f^{-1}(C)
mapping onto
C
.
We can find a curve
C^{\prime}\subset Y
such that
f(C^{\prime})=C
and
C^{\prime}\not\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D+\frac{1}{k}A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Then
(f^{*}D+\frac{1}{k}A)\cdot C^{\prime}\geq 0
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, and therefore

(f^{*}D\,\cdot\,C^{\prime})\geq 0
.
Thus
(D\,\cdot\,C)\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties, and let
D
be a
\pi
-pseudo-effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.
Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a projective surjective morphism from a normal variety
Y
.
Then we have the inclusion
{\rm NNef}(D/Z)\supset f({\rm NNef}(f^{*}D/Z))
.

Proof.

By Remark
2.10
, it is enough to prove
{\rm NNef}(D+A/Z)\supset f({\rm NNef}(f^{*}(D+A/Z))
for any
\pi
-ample divisor
A
on
X
.
In particular, we may assume that
D
is
\pi
-big.

We will prove
X\setminus{\rm NNef}(D/Z)\subset X\setminus f({\rm NNef}(f^{*}D/Z))
.
We fix
x\in X\setminus{\rm NNef}(D/Z)
.
We pick
y\in f^{-1}(x)
and an arbitrary prime divisor
P
over
Y
such that
c_{Y}(P)\ni y
.
We will prove that
\sigma_{P}(f^{*}D/Z)=0
.
Let
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a resolution of
X
such that
g^{-1}(f(c_{Y}(P)))
is pure codimension one in
X
, and let
h\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a resolution of
Y
such that
P
appears as a prime divisor on
Y^{\prime}
and the map
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a morphism.
Since
D
is
\pi
-big and
Z
is quasi-projective, by Definition
2.8
, the equality

\sigma_{P}(f^{*}D/Z)={\rm inf}\left\{{\rm coeff}_{P}(D^{\prime})\,\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}\text{$D^{\prime}\geq 0$ and $\mathbb{R}$-Cartier,}\,D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}h^{*}f^{*}D+h^{*}f^{*}\pi^{*}H_{Z}\\
\text{for some $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $H_{Z}$ on $Z$}\end{array}\right\}.

holds.
Let
g^{-1}(f(c_{Y}(P)))=\sum_{i=1}^{l}Q_{i}
be the prime decomposition.
As above, we have

\sigma_{Q_{i}}(D/Z)={\rm inf}\left\{{\rm coeff}_{P}(E^{\prime})\,\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}\text{$E^{\prime}\geq 0$ and $\mathbb{R}$-Cartier,}\,E^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}g^{*}D+g^{*}\pi^{*}H^{\prime}_{Z}\\
\text{for some $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor $H^{\prime}_{Z}$ on $Z$}\end{array}\right\}

for all
i
.
Since
x\in X\setminus{\rm NNef}(D/Z)
, we have
\sigma_{Q_{i}}(D/Z)=0
for all
i
such that
g(Q_{i})\ni x
.
For any
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, by taking a general effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
E_{\epsilon}
on
X^{\prime}
such that
E^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}g^{*}D+g^{*}\pi^{*}H_{Z}
for some
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor
H_{Z}
on
Z
, we have
{\rm coeff}_{Q_{i}}(E_{\epsilon})\leq\epsilon
for all
i
such that
g(Q_{i})\ni x
.
By construction,
P
is a component of
f^{\prime*}(\sum_{j}Q_{j})
, where
j
runs over the indices such that
g(Q_{j})\ni x
.
Therefore, for any
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we can find
D_{\epsilon}\geq 0
such that
D_{\epsilon}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}h^{*}f^{*}D+h^{*}f^{*}\pi^{*}H_{Z}
for some
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor
H_{Z}
on
Z
and
{\rm coeff}_{P}(D_{\epsilon})\leq\epsilon
.
This implies
\sigma_{P}(f^{*}D/Z)=0
.
Therefore,
y\not\in{\rm NNef}(f^{*}D/Z)
for any
y\in f^{-1}(x)
.
Then
f^{-1}(y)\cap{\rm NNef}(f^{*}D/Z)=\emptyset
.
Hence
x\in X\setminus f({\rm NNef}(f^{*}D/Z))
.
We finish the proof.
∎

2.3.
Singularities of pairs

In this subsection, we collect some definitions and basic results on singularities of pairs.

A
sub normal pair

(X,\Delta)
consists of a normal variety
X
and an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta
on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier.
A
normal pair
is a sub normal pair
(X,\Delta)
such that
\Delta
is effective.
We use these terms to explicitly state the normality of
X
.
For a sub normal pair
(X,\Delta)
and a prime divisor
P
over
X
, the
discrepancy of
P
with respect to
(X,\Delta)
is denoted by
a(P,X,\Delta)
.
A normal pair
(X,\Delta)
is
Kawamata log terminal
(
klt
, for short) if
a(P,X,\Delta)>-1
for all prime divisors
P
over
X
.
A normal pair
(X,\Delta)
is
log canonical
(
lc
, for short) if
a(P,X,\Delta)\geq-1
for all prime divisors
P
over
X
.
A normal pair
(X,\Delta)
is
divisorially log terminal
(
dlt
, for short) if
(X,\Delta)
is lc and there exists a log resolution
f\colon Y\to X
of
(X,\Delta)
such that every
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E
on
Y
satisfies
a(E,X,\Delta)>-1
.
When
(X,\Delta)
is an lc pair, an
lc center
of
(X,\Delta)
is
c_{X}(P)
for some prime divisor
P
over
X
such that
a(P,X,\Delta)=-1
.
We freely use properties of lc centers of dlt pairs in
[
F07a
, Proposition 3.9.2]
and
[
K13
, Theorem 4.16]
.

Definition 2.14

(Non-lc locus, non-klt locus)
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair.
We define the
non-lc locus
and the
non-klt locus
of
(X,\Delta)
, denoted by
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
and
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
respectively, to be closed subschemes of
X
by the following construction:
We take a log resolution
f\colon Y\to X
of
(X,\Delta)
.
We may write
K_{Y}+\Gamma=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)
with an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma
on
Y
.
Then the natural isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}\to f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)

defines ideal sheaves

\begin{split}&\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}:=f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)=f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor+\Gamma^{=1}),\quad{\rm and}\\
&\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}:=f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Gamma\rfloor)=f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor-\Gamma^{=1})\end{split}

of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
These ideal sheaves are independent of the log resolution
f\colon Y\to X
(
[
FST11
]
).
Then
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
and
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
are closed subschemes of
X
defined by
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
and
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
, respectively.

{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
and
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
sometimes mean the support of the non-lc locus and the non-klt locus of
(X,\Delta)
respectively if there is no risk of confusion.

Definition 2.15

(Lc center)
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair.
A subset
S\subset X
is called an
lc center
of
(X,\Delta)
if
S\not\subset{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
and there exists a prime divisor
P
over
X
such that
S=c_{X}(P)
and
a(P,X,\Delta)=-1
.
Unless otherwise stated, the scheme structure of an lc center is the naturally induced reduced scheme structure.

Theorem 2.16

(Dlt blow-up,
[
F21
, Theorem 3.9]
)
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
X
is quasi-projective.
Then there exists a projective birational morphism
f\colon Y\to X
from a normal quasi-projective variety
Y
with the following properties.

•

Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,

•

a(E,X,\Delta)\leq-1
for every
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E
on
Y
, and

•

if we define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma
on
Y
by

K_{Y}+\Gamma=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta),

then
(Y,\Gamma^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
is dlt.

We call the morphism
f\colon(Y,\Gamma)\to(X,\Delta)
a dlt blow-up.

We will prove some results on the defining ideal sheaf of the non-lc locus of a normal pair.

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
(Y,\Delta)
be a sub normal pair such that
K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier.
Let
g\colon W\to Y
be a projective birational morphism from a normal variety
W
such that all
g
-exceptional prime divisors
P
on
W
satisfy
a(P,Y,\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1})>-1
.
We define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma
on
W
by

K_{W}+\Gamma=g^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta).

We put

D=\lceil-(\Delta^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor\qquad{\rm and}\qquad G=\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor.

Then
D
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor and
G-\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor
is effective and
g
-exceptional.

Proof.

We have

\Delta=\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}+\lfloor\Delta^{<1}\rfloor+\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor=\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}-D.

Since
K_{Y}+\Delta
and
K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}
are
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier,
D
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.

We will prove that
G-\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor
is effective and
g
-exceptional.
Clearly we have
g_{*}\Gamma=\Delta
,
g_{*}\{\Gamma\}=\{\Delta\}
, and
g_{*}\Gamma^{=1}=\Delta^{=1}
.
Thus
g_{*}G=D
.
It is also clear that
g_{*}^{-1}D
is a Weil divisor, and therefore
\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor-g^{*}D
is
g
-exceptional.
From these facts, it follows that
G-\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor
is
g
-exceptional.
We also have

\begin{split}K_{W}+\{\Gamma\}+\Gamma^{=1}+\lfloor\Gamma^{<1}\rfloor+\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor=g^{*}(K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1})+g^{*}(\lfloor\Delta^{<1}\rfloor+\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor).\end{split}

By the definitions of
D
and
G
, we obtain

G-g^{*}D=K_{W}+\{\Gamma\}+\Gamma^{=1}-g^{*}(K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}).

Since all
g
-exceptional prime divisors
P
on
W
satisfy
a(P,Y,\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1})>-1
, we have
\lceil(K_{W}+\{\Gamma\}+\Gamma^{=1}-g^{*}(K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}))\rceil\geq 0
.
Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}\lceil G\rceil+\lceil-g^{*}D\rceil\geq\lceil(G-g^{*}D)\rceil=\lceil(K_{W}+\{\Gamma\}+\Gamma^{=1}-g^{*}(K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}))\rceil\geq 0.\end{split}

Since
G
is a Weil divisor, it follows that
G-\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor\geq 0
.

By the above arguments,
D
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and
G-\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor
is effective and
g
-exceptional.
Thus, Lemma
2.17
holds.
∎

Corollary 2.18
.

Let
(Y,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is a dlt pair.
Let
g\colon W\to Y
be a projective birational morphism.
We define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma
on
W
by
K_{W}+\Gamma=g^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta).

Suppose that
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma)
is log smooth.
Then the natural isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\longrightarrow g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)

as subsheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cong g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)
.

Proof.

As in
[
FST11
, Lemma 5.2]
, the sheaf
g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)
does not depend on
g\colon W\to Y
.
From this fact, we may freely replace
g
without loss of generality.

Since the problem is local, we may shrink
Y
, and therefore we may assume that
Y
is quasi-projective.
Since
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is dlt, there is a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorialization
h\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
.
The quasi-projectivity of
Y
was used to construct this
h\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
.
Put
\Delta^{\prime}=\phi_{*}^{-1}\Delta
.
Since
h
is small,
h
is an isomorphism over the smooth locus of
Y
(see, for example,
[
KM98
, Corollary 2.63]
).
Thus, the dlt property (
[
KM98
, Definition 2.37]
) of
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is preserved after we replace
(Y,\Delta)
by
(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
.
Since
h
is a small projective birational morphism, we have

h_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(-\lfloor\Delta^{\prime>1}\rfloor)=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor).

From these facts, we may replace
(Y,\Delta)
and
g\colon W\to Y
by
(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
and some log resolution of
(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
, respectively.
Therefore, we may assume that
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Since
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt,
K_{Y}+\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor and there is a log resolution
g^{\prime}\colon W^{\prime}\to Y
of
(Y,\Delta)
such that all
g^{\prime}
-exceptional prime divisors
P
on
W^{\prime}
satisfy
a(P,Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})>-1
.
It is easy to check the relation
\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1}\leq\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}
, and thus all
g^{\prime}
-exceptional prime divisors
P
on
W^{\prime}
satisfy
a(P,Y,\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1})>-1
.
By replacing
g
by
g^{\prime}
, we may assume that all
g
-exceptional prime divisors
P
on
W
satisfy
a(P,Y,\{\Delta\}+\Delta^{=1})>-1
.
We put

D=-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor\qquad{\rm and}\qquad G=\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor.

By Lemma
2.17
,
D
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and
G-\lfloor g^{*}D\rfloor
is effective and
g
-exceptional.
Then

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(D)\cong g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(G)=g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)

by the standard argument of divisorial sheaves.
∎

Theorem 2.19
.

Let
(Y,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is a dlt pair.
Let
S
be an lc center of
(Y,\Delta)
.
We define
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
G_{S}
and
B_{S}
on
S
by

\begin{split}&G_{S}=(\Delta^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1})|_{S}=(K_{Y}+\Delta-(K_{Y}+\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}))|_{S},\qquad{\rm and}\\
&K_{S}+B_{S}=(K_{Y}+\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})|_{S},\end{split}

respectively.
We put
\Delta_{S}=B_{S}+G_{S}
.
Then
\Delta_{S}
satisfies the following properties.

•

(S,\Delta_{S})
is a pair satisfying
K_{S}+\Delta_{S}=(K_{Y}+\Delta)|_{S}
,

•

\Delta^{<1}_{S}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{S}=B_{S}
, in particular,
(S,\Delta^{<1}_{S}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{S})
is a dlt pair, and

•

the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{S}=\mathcal{O}_{S}
induces a morphism

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}(-\lfloor\Delta_{S}^{>1}\rfloor).

Proof.

We prove the theorem by induction on
{\rm dim}\,Y
.
The theorem is clear if
{\rm dim}\,Y=1
.
From now on, we assume that
{\rm dim}\,Y>1
.
Since
S
is not contained in
{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1}
, it follows that
S
is an lc center of
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
.
Then we can find a component
T
of
\Delta^{=1}
containing
S
.
Since
T
is not contained in
{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1}
, the divisor

G_{T}:=(\Delta^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1})|_{T}=(K_{Y}+\Delta-(K_{Y}+\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}))|_{T}

is well defined as an effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
T
.
Let
(T,B_{T})
be a dlt pair defined by adjunction
K_{T}+B_{T}=(K_{Y}+\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})|_{T}
.
We set

\Delta_{T}:=B_{T}+G_{T}.

We will prove that
\Delta_{T}
satisfies all the properties of Theorem
2.19
.
We have

K_{T}+\Delta_{T}=(K_{Y}+\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})|_{T}+(\Delta^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1})|_{T}=(K_{Y}+\Delta)|_{T}.

Hence
K_{T}+\Delta_{T}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and we see that
(T,\Delta_{T})
is a pair.
Therefore
\Delta_{T}
satisfies the first property of Theorem
2.19
.
We may write

\Delta^{\geq 1}=T+\sum_{i}D_{i}+\sum_{j}(1+\gamma_{j})D^{\prime}_{j}

for some prime divisors
D_{i}
and
D^{\prime}_{j}
and positive real numbers
\gamma_{j}
.
By the dlt property of
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
, we see that
(Y,\Delta)
is log smooth at the generic point of
T\cap D_{i}
and
T\cap D_{j}
for all
i
and
j
.
By considering the locus on which
(Y,\Delta)
is log smooth, we see that any component
P
of
G_{T}=(\Delta^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1})|_{T}
is an irreducible component of
T\cap D^{\prime}_{j}
for some
j
.
This fact implies that any component
P
of
G_{T}
satisfies

{\rm coeff}_{P}(\Delta_{T})=1+\gamma_{j}>1,\quad{\rm coeff}_{P}(B_{T})=1,\quad{\rm and}\quad{\rm coeff}_{P}(\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T})=1.

Therefore, any component
P
of
G_{T}
satisfies

{\rm coeff}_{P}(B_{T})={\rm coeff}_{P}(\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T}).

Since
\Delta_{T}=B_{T}+G_{T}
, if a prime divisor
Q
is not a component of
G_{T}
, then we have

{\rm coeff}_{Q}(\Delta_{T})={\rm coeff}_{Q}(B_{T})\leq 1,

where the inequality follows from the dlt property of
(T,B_{T})
.
This shows that
Q
is not a component of
\Delta_{T}^{>1}
.
Hence we have
{\rm coeff}_{Q}(\Delta_{T})={\rm coeff}_{Q}(\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T}).

Then

{\rm coeff}_{Q}(B_{T})={\rm coeff}_{Q}(\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T}).

By the discussion, we obtain

B_{T}=\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T},

which is the second property of Theorem
2.19
.

In this paragraph, we will prove the third property of Theorem
2.19
for
T
and
\Delta_{T}^{>1}
.
Since
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is dlt, there is a log resolution
g\colon W\to Y
of
(Y,\Delta)
such that all
g
-exceptional prime divisor
E
on
W
satisfy
a(E,Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})>-1
.
We put
T^{\prime}=g_{*}^{-1}T
and
g_{T^{\prime}}=g|_{T^{\prime}}\colon T^{\prime}\to T
, and we define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma
on
W
by

K_{W}+T^{\prime}+\Gamma=g^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta).

By the first property of Theorem
2.19
for
(T,\Delta_{T})
, we have
K_{T^{\prime}}+\Gamma|_{T^{\prime}}=g_{T^{\prime}}^{*}(K_{T}+\Delta_{T})
.
By the exact sequence

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor-T^{\prime})&\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)\\
&\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})|_{T^{\prime}}\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}|_{T^{\prime}}\rfloor)\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

we get the exact sequence

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor-T^{\prime})&\longrightarrow g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)\\
&\longrightarrow g_{T^{\prime}*}\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})|_{T^{\prime}}\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}|_{T^{\prime}}\rfloor).\end{split}

By Corollary
2.18
, the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\cong g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor)
.
This isomorphism and
T^{\prime}=g_{*}^{-1}T
imply that
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor-T)\to g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor-T^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.
Now
(T^{\prime},\Gamma|_{T^{\prime}})
is log smooth by construction, and
(T,\Delta^{<1}_{T}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T})
is dlt by the second property of Theorem
2.19
for
T
.
Since
g_{T^{\prime}}\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is birational and
K_{T^{\prime}}+\Gamma|_{T^{\prime}}=g_{T^{\prime}}^{*}(K_{T}+\Delta_{T})
, Corollary
2.18
implies that the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{T}\to g_{T^{\prime}*}\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})|_{T^{\prime}}\rceil)
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{T}(-\lfloor\Delta_{T}^{>1}\rfloor)\cong g_{T^{\prime}*}\mathcal{O}_{T^{\prime}}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})|_{T^{\prime}}\rceil-\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}|_{T^{\prime}}\rfloor)
.
By these isomorphisms, we get the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor-T)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{T}(-\lfloor\Delta_{T}^{>1}\rfloor)

such that the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\to\mathcal{O}_{T}(-\lfloor\Delta_{T}^{>1}\rfloor)
is induced by
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{T}
.
This shows that
T
and
\Delta_{T}^{>1}
satisfy the third property of Theorem
2.19
.

The arguments in the previous paragraphs show that
\Delta_{T}
satisfies all the properties of Theorem
2.19
.
We recall that
T
is a component of
\Delta^{=1}
containing
S
.
Therefore
S
is an lc center of
(T,\Delta_{T})
.
By the second property of Theorem
2.19
for
\Delta_{T}
, we obtain

B_{T}=\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad G_{T}=\Delta_{T}^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta_{T}^{>1}.

Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&G_{S}=\bigl((\Delta^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1})|_{T}\bigr)|_{S}=(\Delta_{T}^{>1}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta_{T}^{>1})|_{S},\quad{\rm and}\\
&K_{S}+B_{S}=\bigl((K_{Y}+\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})|_{T}\bigr)|_{S}=(K_{T}+\Delta_{T}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{T})|_{S}.\\
\end{split}

By the induction hypothesis of Theorem
2.19
, the divisor
\Delta_{S}
satisfies the first and the second properties of Theorem
2.19
, and moreover we obtain the morphism

\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{T}(-\lfloor\Delta_{T}^{>1}\rfloor)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{S}(-\lfloor\Delta_{S}^{>1}\rfloor)

induced by
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{T}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
.
From this,
\Delta_{S}
satisfies all properties of Theorem
2.19
.
We finish the proof.
∎

2.4.
Quasi-log scheme

The goal of this subsection is to define a
quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
(Definition
2.23
) and prove some results used in this paper.

Definition 2.20

(Quasi-log scheme,
[
F17
, Definition 6.2.2]
)
.

A
quasi-log scheme
is a scheme
X
endowed with an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor (or an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle)
\omega
on
X
, a closed subscheme
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)\subsetneq X
, and a finite collection
\{C\}
of reduced and irreducible subschemes of
X
such that there is a proper morphism
f\colon(Y,B_{Y})\to X
from a globally embedded simple normal crossing pair satisfying the following properties:

•

f^{*}\omega\sim_{\mathbb{R}}K_{Y}+B_{Y}
,

•

the natural map
\mathcal{O}_{X}\to f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lceil-(B_{Y}^{<1})\rceil)
induces an isomorphism

\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\lceil-(B_{Y}^{<1})\rceil-\lfloor B_{Y}^{>1}\rfloor),

where
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
is the defining ideal sheaf of
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
, and

•

the collection of reduced and irreducible subschemes

\{C\}
coincides with the images
of the strata of
(Y,B_{Y})
that are not included in
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
.

We simply write
[X,\omega]
to denote the above data

\left(X,\omega,f\colon(Y,B_{Y})\to X\right)

if there is no risk of confusion.
An element of
\{C\}
is called a
qlc center of
[X,\omega]
.

The non-qklt locus of
X
, denoted by
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
, is the union of
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
and all qlc centers of
[X,\omega]
.
We note that
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
has the scheme structure naturally induced by
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
and all qlc centers of
[X,\omega]
(cf.
[
F17
, Notation 6.3.10]
).

Theorem 2.21
.

Let
[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme and
\pi\colon X\to Z
a projective morphism to a scheme.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
.
Suppose that
\omega+A
is
\pi
-nef and
(\omega+A)|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then
\omega+A
is
\pi
-semi-ample.

Proof.

We may assume that
Z
is affine.
By using
[
F21
, Lemma 4.25]
and the argument from convex geometry, we can find positive real numbers
r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{m}
,
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisors
\omega_{1},\,\cdots,\,\omega_{m}
on
X
, and
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
A_{1},\,\cdots,\,A_{m}
on
X
such that

•

\sum_{i=1}^{m}r_{i}=1
,
\sum_{i=1}^{m}r_{i}\omega_{i}=\omega
, and
\sum_{i=1}^{m}r_{i}A_{i}=A
,

•

for every
1\leq i\leq m
, there is the structure of a quasi-log scheme
[X,\omega_{i}]
that has the same non-qlc locus as
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
, and

•

all
(\omega_{i}+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
are semi-ample over
Z
.

For each
1\leq i\leq m
, the cone theorem
[
F17
, Theorem 6.7.4]
implies that we may write

\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)=\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)_{(\omega_{i}+A_{i})\geq 0}+\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)^{(i)}_{-\infty}+\sum_{j=1}^{p_{i}}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{i,j}]

for some
p_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and curves
C_{i,j}\subset X
contained in a fiber of
\pi
, where

\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)^{(i)}_{-\infty}:={\rm Im}(\overline{\rm NE}({\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega_{i})/Z)\to\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z))\qquad(\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{fujino-book}{}{}, Definition 6.7.1]}}).

Since
(\omega_{i}+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
is semi-ample over
Z
, we have
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)^{(i)}_{-\infty}\subset\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)_{(\omega_{i}+A_{i})\geq 0}
for any
1\leq i\leq m
.
For each
1\leq i\leq m
and
1\leq j\leq p_{i}
, we consider the set

\mathcal{H}_{i,j}:=\left\{(t_{1},\cdots,\,t_{m})\in(\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})^{m}\,\middle|\,\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}=1,\,\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{m}t_{k}(\omega_{k}+A_{k})\Bigr)\cdot C_{i,j}\geq 0\right\}.

Since all
\omega_{k}+A_{k}
are
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and all
\mathcal{H}_{i,j}
contain
(r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{m})
, we see that
\bigcap_{i,j}\mathcal{H}_{i,j}
is a rational polytope that contains
(r_{1},\cdots,\,r_{m})
.
By the standard argument from convex geometry, we can find positive real numbers
r^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{n}
,
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisors
\omega^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\omega^{\prime}_{n}
on
X
, and
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
A^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,A^{\prime}_{n}
on
X
such that

•

\sum_{l=1}^{n}r^{\prime}_{l}=1
,
\sum_{l=1}^{n}r^{\prime}_{l}\omega^{\prime}_{l}=\omega
, and
\sum_{l=1}^{n}r^{\prime}_{l}A^{\prime}_{l}=A
,

•

every
\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l}
is a convex linear combination of
\omega_{1}+A_{1},\,\cdots,\,\omega_{m}+A_{m}
,

•

for every
1\leq l\leq n
, there is the structure of a quasi-log scheme
[X,\omega^{\prime}_{l}]
that has the same non-qlc locus as
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
,

•

all
(\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
are semi-ample over
Z
, and

•

(\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l})\cdot C_{i,j}\geq 0
for all
l
,
i
, and
j
.

Then
\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l}
is
\pi
-nef for all
l
.
Indeed, if
\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l}
is not
\pi
-nef for some
l
, then there exists an
(\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l})
-negative extremal ray
R
of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
.
By construction, there is an index
i
such that
R
is an
(\omega_{i}+A_{i})
-negative extremal ray of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
.
Then
R
is generated by
C_{i,j}
for some
j
.
However, it contradicts the condition
(\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l})\cdot C_{i,j}\geq 0
.
Therefore,
\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l}
is
\pi
-nef for all
l
.
By the base point free theorem
[
F17
, Theorem 6.5.1]
, every
\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l}
is
\pi
-semi-ample.
Therefore,
\omega+A=\sum_{l=1}^{n}r^{\prime}_{l}(\omega^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l})
is also
\pi
-semi-ample.
∎

In this paper, we use the notion of quasi-log scheme induced by normal pair.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a contraction of normal varieties and let
(Y,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
f({\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta))\subsetneq X
and
K_{Y}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R}}f^{*}\omega
for some
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
\omega
on
X
.
Let
g\colon W\to Y
be a log resolution of
(Y,\Delta)
.
We define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma
on
W
by

K_{W}+\Gamma=g^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta).

Let
(X,\omega,f\circ g\colon(W,\Gamma)\to X)
be the structure of a quasi-log scheme.
Then the structure does not depend on the choice of
g\colon W\to Y
, in other words,
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
and the set of qlc centers does not depend on
g
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of
[
F17
, Proposition 6.3.1]
.
∎

Definition 2.23

(Quasi-log scheme induced by normal pair)
.

A
quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
, which we denote by
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
in this paper, consists of a normal pair
(Y,\Delta)
, a contraction
f\colon Y\to X
of normal varieties, and the structure of a quasi-log scheme
[X,\omega]
on
X
which is defined with a log resolution of
(Y,\Delta)
.
By definition, it follows that

•

f({\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta))\subsetneq X
, and

•

K_{Y}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R}}f^{*}\omega
.

By Lemma
2.22
, the structure of
[X,\omega]
does not depend on the choice of a log resolution of
(Y,\Delta)
.

In the case of
X=Y
and
\omega=K_{Y}+\Delta
, we may identify
[Y,K_{Y}+\Delta]
with the normal pair
(Y,\Delta)
.
In this situation, it follows that
{\rm Nqlc}(Y,K_{Y}+\Delta)={\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)
and
{\rm Nqklt}(Y,K_{Y}+\Delta)={\rm Nklt}(Y,\Delta)
as closed subschemes of
Y
.

By definition,
(Y,\Delta)
and
f\colon Y\to X
in a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
also form an lc-trivial fibration.
However, we will often focus on the structure of the quasi-log scheme of
[X,\omega]
and use
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
and
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
.
Hence, we regard
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
as a quasi-log scheme rather than an lc-trivial fibration in this paper.

Remark 2.24
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
(Y,\Delta)
.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(Y,\Delta)}
and
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)}
) be the defining ideal sheaves of the non-klt locus and non-lc locus of
(Y,\Delta)
, respectively.
By definition,
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
and
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
are the closed subschemes defined by
f_{*}\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(Y,\Delta)}
and
f_{*}\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)}
, respectively.
In particular, we have
f({\rm Nklt}(Y,\Delta))={\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
and
f({\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta))={\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
set-theoretically.

Theorem 2.25
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair such that
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is a dlt pair.
Let
S
be an lc center of
(Y,\Delta)
such that
f(S)\not\subset{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
.
Let
(S,\Delta_{S})
be a pair defined by adjunction
K_{S}+\Delta_{S}=(K_{Y}+\Delta)|_{S}
.
Let
T
be the normalization of
f(S)
and let
\omega_{T}
be the pullback of
\omega
to
T
.
Then there exists the structure of a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
f_{S}\colon(S,\Delta_{S})\to[T,\omega_{T}]
as in Definition
2.23
such that the natural morphism
\tau\colon T\to X
induces a morphism
{\rm Nqlc}(T,\omega_{T})\to{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
of closed subschemes and the image of any qlc center of
[T,\omega_{T}]
by
\tau
is a qlc center of
[X,\omega]
.

Proof.

Let
\tau\colon T\to X
and
f_{S}\colon T\to Y
be the natural morphisms.
To define the structure of a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
f_{S}\colon(S,\Delta_{S})\to[T,\omega_{T}]
, we need to check
f_{S}({\rm Nlc}(S,\Delta_{S}))\neq T
.
By Theorem
2.19
, the pair
(S,\Delta_{S}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta_{S}^{\geq 1})
is a dlt pair and
the natural morphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
induces a morphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)\to\mathcal{O}_{S}(-\lfloor\Delta_{S}^{>1}\rfloor)
.
By Corollary
2.18
, the support of
{\rm Nlc}(S,\Delta_{S})
is contained in
{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)\cap S
.
Then
f({\rm Nlc}(S,\Delta_{S}))\subset{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)\cap f(S)
.
This fact and the hypothesis
f(S)\not\subset{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
imply
f_{S}({\rm Nlc}(S,\Delta_{S}))\neq T
.
Therefore, we may define the structure of a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
f_{S}\colon(S,\Delta_{S})\to[T,\omega_{T}]
.

Since
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is a dlt pair and
S
is an lc center of
(Y,\Delta)
, any lc center of
(S,\Delta_{S})
is an lc center of
(Y,\Delta)
contained in
S
.
From this, the image
\tau(C)
of any qlc center
C
of
[T,\omega_{T}]
is a qlc center of
[X,\omega]
.

By Corollary
2.18
, the defining ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{X}
of
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
is
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}\rfloor)
, and the defining ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{T}
of
{\rm Nqlc}(T,\omega_{T})
is
f_{S*}\mathcal{O}_{S}(-\lfloor\Delta^{>1}_{S}\rfloor)
.
By Theorem
2.19
, the natural morphism
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}
induces a morphism
\mathcal{I}_{X}\to\tau_{*}\mathcal{I}_{T}
.
By the following diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{*}\mathcal{I}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau_{*}\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nqlc}(T,\omega_{T})}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

there exists a natural morphism
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}\to\tau_{*}\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nqlc}(T,\omega_{T})}
.
This induces the desired morphism
{\rm Nqlc}(T,\omega_{T})\to{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
as a morphism between closed subschemes.
∎

Lemma 2.26
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
, and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
.
Then there exists a normal pair
(X,G)
such that the relation
K_{X}+G\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\omega+A
holds,
{\rm Nklt}(X,G)
is a closed subscheme of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
, and the support of
{\rm Nklt}(X,G)
is equal to the support of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
.

Proof.

We quickly recall the construction of the discriminant
\mathbb{R}
-b-divisor
\boldsymbol{\rm B}
and the moduli
\mathbb{R}
-b-divisor
\boldsymbol{\rm M}
.
Let
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a projective birational morphism from a normal variety
X^{\prime}
, and let
h\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a resolution of
Y
such that the induced map
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a morphism.
We can write
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=h^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta)
with an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta^{\prime}
on
Y^{\prime}
.
For any prime divisor
Q
on
X^{\prime}
, after shrinking
X^{\prime}
around the generic point of
Q
we define
t_{Q}
to be the lc threshold of
f^{\prime*}Q
with respect to
(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
.
Then the trace
\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}
of the discriminant
\mathbb{R}
-b-divisor on
X^{\prime}
is

\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}:=\sum_{Q}(1-t_{Q})Q,

where
Q
runs over prime divisors on
X^{\prime}
.
The trace
\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}
of the moduli
\mathbb{R}
-b-divisor on
X^{\prime}
is defined by
\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}:=g^{*}\omega-K_{X^{\prime}}-\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}
.
By
[
FH23
, Corollary 5.2]
(see also
[
A04
]
for the case of
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors), there exists a log resolution
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
of
(X,\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X})
such that
\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}
is nef over
Z
and for any projective birational morphism
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
from a normal variety
X^{\prime\prime}
, we have
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime\prime}}=g^{\prime*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}})
and
\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime\prime}}=g^{\prime*}\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}
.

In this paragraph we follow
[
F21
, Proof of Theorem 1.10]
.
We take a log resolution
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
of
(X,\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X})
such that
\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}
is nef over
Z
and for any projective birational morphism
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
from a normal variety
X^{\prime\prime}
, we have
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime\prime}}=g^{\prime*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}})
and
\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime\prime}}=g^{\prime*}\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}
.
Since
\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}
is effective, the natural isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}\to g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
defines an ideal sheaf
g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(-\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor)\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
We take a member
H^{\prime}\in|g^{*}A+\boldsymbol{\rm M}_{X^{\prime}}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor=\lfloor(\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}+H^{\prime})\rfloor
.
By replacing
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and taking
H^{\prime}
appropriately, we may assume that
g
is a log resolution of
(X,\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X}+g_{*}H^{\prime})
.
By construction of
\boldsymbol{\rm B}
, the support of
{\rm Nklt}(X,\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X}+g_{*}H^{\prime})
is equal to the support of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
.

We put

G:=g_{*}(\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}+H^{\prime}),

and we will prove that
(X,G)
is the desired normal pair.
By construction, we have
K_{X}+G\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\omega+A
and the support of
{\rm Nklt}(X,G)
is equal to the support of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
.
Hence, it is sufficient to prove that
{\rm Nklt}(X,G)
is a closed subscheme of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}
and
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}
be the defining ideal sheaves of
{\rm Nklt}(X,G)
and
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
, respectively.
We will prove
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}\subset\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}
as subsheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Now we have
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}=g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(-\lfloor(\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}+H^{\prime})\rfloor)=g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(-\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor)
by construction of
H^{\prime}
.
We take a log resolution
h\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
of
(Y,\Delta)
such that the induced map
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a morphism and
(Y,{\rm Supp}\,h^{-1}_{*}\Delta\cup{\rm Ex}(h)\cup{\rm Supp}\,f^{\prime*}\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}})
is log smooth.
Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
Y
satisfying
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=h^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta)
.
For any open subset
U\subset X
, the definitions of
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}
and
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}
show

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}(U)=&\{\phi\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\,|\,({\rm div}(h^{*}f^{*}\phi)-\lfloor\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor)|_{(f\circ h)^{-1}(U)}\geq 0\},\qquad{\rm and}\\
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}(U)=&\{\phi\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\,|\,({\rm div}(g^{*}\phi)-\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor)|_{g^{-1}(U)}\geq 0\},\end{split}

For each prime divisor
Q
on
X^{\prime}
, we can write
f^{\prime*}Q=\sum m_{j}P_{j}
for some positive integers
m_{j}
and prime divisors
P_{j}
on
Y
.
Then the lc threshold of
f^{\prime*}Q
with respect to
(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
over the generic point of
Q
is
{\rm min}\{\frac{1-{\rm coeff}_{P_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime})}{m_{j}}\,|\,f^{\prime}(P_{j})=Q\}.

We note that this invariant can be negative because
(Y,\Delta)
is not necessarily lc.
Hence, there exists an index
l
such that
f^{\prime}(P_{l})=Q
and

{\rm coeff}_{Q}(\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}})=1-\frac{1-{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}(\Delta^{\prime})}{m_{l}}.

We pick
\phi\in\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}(U)
, and we regard
\phi
as a rational function on
X
.
For any prime divisor
Q
on
X
intersecting
g^{-1}(U)
, we define
b_{Q}:={\rm coeff}_{Q}({\rm div}(g^{*}\phi))
.
With the above notation, we have
{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}({\rm div}(h^{*}f^{*}\phi))=b_{Q}m_{l}
.
Therefore,
\phi\in\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}(U)
implies

b_{Q}m_{l}-{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}(\lfloor\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor)\geq 0
.
Hence, we have

b_{Q}\geq\frac{{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}(\lfloor\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor)}{m_{l}}>\frac{{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}(\Delta^{\prime})-1}{m_{l}}.

Then

b_{Q}-{\rm coeff}_{Q}(\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor)>\frac{{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}(\Delta^{\prime})-1}{m_{l}}-\left(1-\frac{1-{\rm coeff}_{P_{l}}(\Delta^{\prime})}{m_{l}}\right)=-1.

Since
b_{Q}
is an integer, we see that
b_{Q}-{\rm coeff}_{Q}(\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor)\geq 0
.
Since
Q
is arbitrary, we have

({\rm div}(g^{*}\phi)-\lfloor\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{X^{\prime}}\rfloor)|_{g^{-1}(U)}\geq 0.

This shows
\phi\in\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}(U)
, and therefore
\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}\subset\mathcal{I}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}
as subsheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Then there exists a natural surjective morphism
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}\to\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X,G)}
.
This shows that
{\rm Nklt}(X,G)
is a closed subscheme of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
.
∎

Theorem 2.27
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair.
Fix
X^{\prime}
a union of
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
and (possibly empty) some qlc centers of
[X,\omega]
.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism to a scheme
Z
, and let
L
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
X
such that

•

L
is Cartier on a neighborhood of
X^{\prime}
,

•

f^{*}L
is a Weil divisor on
Y
, and

•

L-\omega
is
\pi
-nef and
\pi
-log big with respect to
[X,\omega]
.

Then
R^{i}\pi_{*}(\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L))=0
for every
i>0
, where
\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}
is the defining ideal sheaf of
X^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
Z
is affine.
Let
U\supset X^{\prime}
be an open subset of
X
on which
L
is Cartier.
Let
g\colon W\to Y
be a log resolution of
(Y,\Delta)
, and we define
\Gamma
by

K_{W}+\Gamma=g^{*}(K_{Y}+\Delta).

Then
g^{*}f^{*}L=\lfloor g^{*}f^{*}L\rfloor+\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}
and
\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}
is an effective
g
-exceptional
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor such that
\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}|_{(f\circ g)^{-1}(U)}=0
.
By taking some blow-ups on
W
if necessary, we may assume that the union of all strata of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
mapping into
X^{\prime}
is
{\rm Supp}\,(\Gamma^{>1}+S)
for some union
S
of components of
\Gamma^{=1}
.
We put

T:=S+\lfloor\Gamma^{>1}\rfloor\qquad{\rm and}\qquad B:=\Gamma^{=1}+\{\Gamma\}-S.

Then
B
is an snc boundary
\mathbb{R}
-divisor, and
B^{=1}
and
T
have no common components.
Moreover,
\Gamma=T+B-\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil
and
\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}=(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(-T+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)
.
We have

\begin{split}\lfloor g^{*}f^{*}L\rfloor=&g^{*}f^{*}(L-\omega)+g^{*}f^{*}\omega-\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}\\
\sim_{\mathbb{R}}&g^{*}f^{*}(L-\omega)+K_{W}+\Gamma-\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}\\
\sim_{\mathbb{R}}&g^{*}f^{*}(L-\omega)+K_{W}+T+B-\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}.\end{split}

We take a reduced divisor
E
on
W
such that if we define

B^{\prime}:=B-\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}+E,

then
B^{\prime}
is a boundary
\mathbb{R}
-divisor and
B^{\prime=1}\leq B^{=1}
.
More precisely, for each prime divisor
P
on
W
, the coefficient of
P
in
E
is defined by

{\rm coeff}_{P}(E)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{0\qquad({\rm coeff}_{P}(B-\{g^{*}f^{*}L\})\geq 0)}\\
{1\qquad({\rm coeff}_{P}(B-\{g^{*}f^{*}L\})<0)}\end{array}\right.

We note that
E
is
g
-exceptional and
E|_{(f\circ g)^{-1}(U)}=0
because any component of
E
is a component of
\{g^{*}f^{*}L\}
.
By the above relation, we have

(
\spadesuit
)

\lfloor g^{*}f^{*}L\rfloor+E+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-T\sim_{\mathbb{R}}g^{*}f^{*}(L-\omega)+K_{W}+B^{\prime}.

We put

A:=\lfloor g^{*}f^{*}L\rfloor+E+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil\sim_{\mathbb{R}}g^{*}f^{*}(L-\omega)+K_{W}+T+B^{\prime},

and we consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{W}(A-T)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{W}(A)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{T}(A|_{T})\longrightarrow 0.

Now
T
is an snc Weil divisor, and the fact
B^{\prime=1}\leq B^{=1}
implies that
B^{\prime=1}
and
T
have no common components.
By this fact and the log smoothness of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma)
, any stratum of
(W,B^{\prime=1})
is not contained in
{\rm Supp}\,T
.
By these facts, we can check that the image of any lc center of
(W,B^{\prime})
by
f\circ g
is not contained in
X^{\prime}
.
Indeed, if there is an lc center
C
of
(W,B^{\prime})
mapping into
X^{\prime}
, then
C
is a stratum of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
by the relation
B^{\prime=1}\leq B^{=1}\leq{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1}
.
Then
C\subset{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime=1}\cap{\rm Supp}\,T
because the union of all strata of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
mapping into
X^{\prime}
is
{\rm Supp}\,T
, which follows from the definitions of
T
and
S
.
This contradicts the log smoothness of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma)
and the fact that
B^{\prime=1}
and
T
have no common components.
Therefore, the image of any lc center of
(W,B^{\prime})
is not mapped into
X^{\prime}
.
By the torsion-free theorem
[
F17
, Theorem 5.6.2 (i)]
, the connecting morphism

(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(A|_{T})\to R^{1}(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A-T)
is the zero morphism.
Therefore,

0\longrightarrow(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A-T)\longrightarrow(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A)\longrightarrow(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(A|_{T})\longrightarrow 0

is exact.

Now we have the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}\longrightarrow 0.

We recall that
L|_{U}
is Cartier and
U\supset X^{\prime}
.
From this, we have the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L|_{X^{\prime}})\longrightarrow 0.

We recall that
f^{*}L
is a Weil divisor on
Y
, which is the hypothesis of Theorem
2.27
, and
A=\lfloor g^{*}f^{*}L\rfloor+E+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil
such that
E+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil
is an effective
g
-exceptional divisor.
Thus, we have

(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A)\cong f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(f^{*}L)\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)

via the natural morphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\to(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A)
(cf.
[
N04
, II, 2.11. Lemma]
).

Recall that
\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}=(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(-T+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)
.
Since
U\supset X^{\prime}=(f\circ g)({\rm Supp}\,T)
, we have
{\rm Supp}\,T\subset(f\circ g)^{-1}(U)
.
By recalling the fact
E|_{(f\circ g)^{-1}(U)}=0
, we have

\begin{split}(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(A|_{T})=&(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}\bigl((\lfloor g^{*}f^{*}L\rfloor+E+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)|_{T}\bigr)\\
\cong&(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}\bigl(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil|_{T}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L).\end{split}

Note that this isomorphism is induced by the restriction of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\to(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A)
to
X^{\prime}
.
By the definitions of
T
and
B
, we also have

\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-T\sim_{\mathbb{R}}-g^{*}f^{*}\omega+K_{W}+\Gamma+\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-T\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,X}K_{W}+B.

We can check that the image of any lc center of
(W,B)
by
f\circ g
is not contained in
X^{\prime}
.
Indeed, if there is an lc center
C^{\prime}
of
(W,B)
mapping into
X^{\prime}
, then
C^{\prime}
is a stratum of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
by the relation
B^{=1}\leq{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1}
.
Then
C^{\prime}\subset{\rm Supp}\,B^{=1}\cap{\rm Supp}\,T
since the union of all strata of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
mapping into
X^{\prime}
is
{\rm Supp}\,T
.
This contradicts the log smoothness of
(W,{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma)
and the fact that
B^{=1}
and
T
have no common components.
Thus, the image of any lc center of
(W,B)
is not mapped into
X^{\prime}
.
By the torsion-free theorem
[
F17
, Theorem 5.6.2 (i)]
and applying
(f\circ g)_{*}
to the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil-T)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{W}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{T}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil|_{T})\longrightarrow 0,

we have the exact sequence

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil|_{T})\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

This shows
(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil|_{T})=\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
.
Therefore we have

(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(A|_{T})\cong(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}\bigl(\lceil-(\Gamma^{<1})\rceil|_{T}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\cong\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\cong\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L|_{X^{\prime}}).

Here, the final isomorphism follows from the Cartier property of
L
around
X^{\prime}
.

From the above argument, we obtain the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A-T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}(A|_{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L|_{X^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{0.}

From this, we have
(f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A-T)\cong\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)
.
By (

♠

‣
2.4
