) and the definition of
A
, we have
A-T\sim_{\mathbb{R}}g^{*}f^{*}(L-\omega)+K_{W}+B^{\prime}.

By construction of
B^{\prime}
, any lc center of
B^{\prime}
is an lc center of
\Gamma
.
Since
L-\omega
is
\pi
-nef and
\pi
-log big with respect to
[X,\omega]
, which is the hypothesis of Theorem
2.27
, the vanishing theorem for simple normal crossing varieties
[
F17
, Theorem 5.7.3 (ii)]
implies

R^{i}\pi_{*}(\mathcal{I}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(L))\cong R^{i}\pi_{*}\bigl((f\circ g)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(A-T)\bigr)=0

for all
i>0
.
This is what we wanted to prove.
∎

3.
Running minimal model program

In this section, we define a minimal model and a good minimal model for normal pairs, and we study how to construct a sequence of an MMP for normal pairs whose non-nef locus is disjoint from the non-lc locus.
Corollary
3.11
and Corollary
3.12
are the main results of this section.

3.1.
Minimal model

In this subsection, we define minimal models for normal pairs and prove some basic results.

Definition 3.1

(Minimal model)
.

Let
X\to Z
be a projective morphism from a normal quasi-projective variety
X
to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
, and let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair.
Let
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
be a normal pair with a projective morphism
X^{\prime}\to Z
, and let
\phi\colon X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
be a birational map over
Z
.
We say that
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a
minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
if

•

for any prime divisor
P
on
X
, we have

a(P,X,\Delta)\leq a(P,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}),

and the strict inequality holds if
P
is
\phi
-exceptional,

•

for any prime divisor
P^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
, we have

{\rm coeff}_{P^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})=-a(P^{\prime},X,\Delta),

and the inequality
a(P^{\prime},X,\Delta)\leq-1
holds if
P^{\prime}
is
\phi^{-1}
-exceptional, and

•

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
is nef over
Z
.

We say that a minimal model
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
is a
good minimal model
if
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
A
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial minimal model
(resp.
a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model
) of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
is a minimal model (resp. a good minimal model)
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
such that
X^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Even if
(X,\Delta)
is lc, the above definition of minimal model is different from the definition of log minimal model in the sense of Birkar–Shokurov because
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is not necessarily
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt.
However, if
(X,\Delta)
is an lc pair then
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is also an lc pair and any
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt model
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a log minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
in the sense of Birkar–Shokurov.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties and
(X,\Delta)
a normal pair.
Let
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
be a normal pair with a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
such that
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)
and any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E
on
X^{\prime}
satisfies
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq-1
.
If a normal pair
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
is a minimal model (resp. a good minimal model) of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
over
Z
, then
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
is a minimal model (resp. a good minimal model) of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
.

Proof.

We will prove the case of minimal model because the case of good minimal model can be proved similarly.

Let
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
be a minimal model of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
over
Z
.
For any prime divisor
P
on
X
, we have

a(P,X,\Delta)=a(P,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\leq a(P,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}).

Moreover, if
P
is exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
then
f_{*}^{-1}P
is exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence

a(P,X,\Delta)<a(P,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}).

Therefore, the first condition of a minimal model in Definition
3.1
holds.
For any prime divisor
Q^{\prime\prime}
on
X^{\prime\prime}
, we have

{\rm coeff}_{Q^{\prime\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime})=-a(Q^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})=-a(Q^{\prime\prime},X,\Delta).

Suppose that
Q^{\prime\prime}
is exceptional over
X
.
If
Q^{\prime\prime}
is not exceptional over
X^{\prime}
, then the birational transform
Q^{\prime}
of
Q^{\prime\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
is
f
-exceptional, and therefore

a(Q^{\prime\prime},X,\Delta)=a(Q^{\prime},X,\Delta)\leq-1.

If
Q^{\prime\prime}
is exceptional over
X^{\prime}
, then

a(Q^{\prime\prime},X,\Delta)=a(Q^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\leq-1.

In any case, we have
a(Q^{\prime\prime},X,\Delta)\leq-1
.
Thus the second condition of a minimal model in Definition
3.1
holds.
By definition,
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is nef over
Z
, and thus the third condition of a minimal model in Definition
3.1
holds.
Therefore,
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
is a minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
.
From this, we see that Lemma
3.2
holds.
∎

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties and let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
.
Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a projective birational morphism from a normal variety
Y
such that any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
P
on
Y
satisfies
a(P,X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
P\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We write

K_{Y}+\Gamma=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\Gamma
and
E
on
Y
having no common components.
Let
B
be an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
Y
such that
{\rm Supp}\,B\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Suppose that
(Y,\Gamma+B)
is a pair, in other words,
K_{Y}+\Gamma+B
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier.
Let
\phi\colon Y\dashrightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a birational contraction over
Z
, where
Y^{\prime}
is a normal variety and projective over
Z
, such that
\phi_{*}(K_{Y}+\Gamma+B)
is semi-ample over
Z
and
\phi
only contracts some prime divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|K_{Y}+\Gamma+B/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then
\phi
exactly contracts all prime divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and
(Y^{\prime},\phi_{*}\Gamma)
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
.

Proof.

We have

{\rm Bs}|K_{Y}+\Gamma+B/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\rm Bs}|K_{Y}+\Gamma-E/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cup{\rm Supp}\,(B+E).

Since
\Gamma\geq 0
and
E\geq 0
have no common components, any component
\tilde{E}
of
E
satisfies
a(\tilde{E},X,\Delta)>0
.
Then
{\rm Supp}\,E\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
by the assumption of
f\colon Y\to X
.
Since
{\rm Supp}\,B\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain

(
*
)

\begin{split}{\rm Supp}\,(B+E)\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.\end{split}

From this fact and the relation
K_{Y}+\Gamma-E=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)
, we have

{\rm Bs}|K_{Y}+\Gamma+B/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\rm Bs}|K_{Y}+\Gamma-E/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cup{\rm Supp}\,(B+E)\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

Therefore, any prime divisor contracted by
\phi
is contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We put
\Gamma^{\prime}=\phi_{*}\Gamma
and
B^{\prime}=\phi_{*}B
.
Let
g\colon W\to Y
and
g^{\prime}\colon W\to Y^{\prime}
be a common resolution of
\phi
.
We may write

(
**
)

\begin{split}g^{*}f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+F=g^{\prime*}(K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}+B^{\prime})+F^{\prime}\end{split}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
F
and
F^{\prime}
on
W
that have no common components.
Then
F^{\prime}
is
g^{\prime}
-exceptional since
g^{\prime}_{*}(F-F^{\prime})=B^{\prime}+\phi_{*}E
and
g^{\prime}_{*}F
and
g^{\prime}_{*}F^{\prime}
have no common components.

In this paragraph, we will prove that
F=0
.
We assume that
F
is
(f\circ g)
-exceptional.
Then the semi-ampleness of
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}+B^{\prime}
over
Z
implies

\begin{split}{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|g^{*}(K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}+B^{\prime})+F^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}=&{\rm Bs}|g^{*}f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+F/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\\
=&{\rm Bs}|g^{*}f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cup{\rm Supp}\,F.\end{split}

From this relation and the fact that
F
and
F^{\prime}
have no common components, we have
F=0
.
Therefore, it is sufficient to prove that
F
is
(f\circ g)
-exceptional.
By definition, we have
g^{\prime}_{*}F=B^{\prime}+\phi_{*}E
.
Hence every component of
g_{*}F
is
\phi
-exceptional or a component of
B+E
.
By the inclusion (

∗

‣
3.1
) and the fact that
\phi
only contracts divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we have
{\rm Supp}\,g_{*}F\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Thus

{\rm Supp}\,(f\circ g)_{*}F\subset{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

From now on, we suppose that
(f\circ g)_{*}F\neq 0
and we will get a contradiction.
By (

∗
⁣
∗

‣
3.1
) and the semi-ampleness of
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}+B^{\prime}
over
Z
, any component of
(f\circ g)_{*}F
is not an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+(f\circ g)_{*}F/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Note that
K_{X}+\Delta+(f\circ g)_{*}F
may not be
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier.
We pick
D\in{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and let
D=\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}D_{i}
be the prime decomposition, where
a_{1},\cdots,\,a_{l}
are positive real numbers.
Since every component of
{\rm Supp}\,(f\circ g)_{*}F
is contained in
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we can write
(f\circ g)_{*}F=\sum_{i=1}^{l}b_{i}D_{i}
for some nonnegative real numbers
b_{1},\cdots,\,b_{l}
.
Relabeling the indices, we may assume
\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\geq\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}
for all
2\leq i\leq l
.
Then
b_{1}>0
because otherwise the relation
\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\geq\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}
implies
b_{i}=0
for all
2\leq i\leq l
, which shows
(f\circ g)_{*}F=0
.
Moreover

\begin{split}\left(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\right)D=\left(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}D_{i}=&D+b_{1}D_{1}+\sum_{i=2}^{l}\frac{a_{i}b_{1}}{a_{1}}D_{i}\\
=&D+(f\circ g)_{*}F+\sum_{i=2}^{l}a_{i}\left(\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}}-\frac{b_{i}}{a_{i}}\right)D_{i}.\end{split}

Using
D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}K_{X}+\Delta
and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm Bs}|(1+\frac{b_{1}}{a_{1}})(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain

{\rm Supp}\,(f\circ g)_{*}F\subset{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+(f\circ g)_{*}F/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cup\bigcup_{i=2}^{l}{\rm Supp}\,D_{i}.

Recalling the fact that any component of
(f\circ g)_{*}F
is not an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+(f\circ g)_{*}F/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we see that the right hand side does not contain
{\rm Supp}\,D_{1}
as an irreducible component.
Then we get a contradiction because
(f\circ g)_{*}F=\sum_{i=1}^{l}b_{i}D_{i}
and
b_{1}>0
.
Therefore we have
(f\circ g)_{*}F=0
, and therefore
F
is
(f\circ g)
-exceptional.
Then
F=0
as discussed above.

By (

∗
⁣
∗

‣
3.1
) and the argument in the previous paragraph, we may write

(
*\!*\!*
)

\begin{split}g^{*}f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)=g^{\prime*}(K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}+B^{\prime})+F^{\prime}\end{split}

such that
F^{\prime}
is effective and
g^{\prime}
-exceptional.
By the semi-ampleness of
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}+B^{\prime}
over
Z
, the equality

{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|g^{*}f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds.
This shows that
\phi\colon Y\dashrightarrow Y^{\prime}
contracts all divisorial components of
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since
\phi
only contracts some divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, it follows that
\phi
exactly contracts divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
This is the first assertion of Proposition
3.3
.
By this fact and (

∗

‣
3.1
), we also see that
B+E
is contracted by
\phi
.

Finally, we check that
(Y^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
.
Let
Q
be a prime divisor on
X
.
By (

∗
∗
∗

‣
3.1
), we have

a(Q,X,\Delta)\leq a(Q,Y^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}).

If
Q
is exceptional over
Y^{\prime}
, then
f^{-1}_{*}Q
is contracted by
\phi
.
Therefore,
f^{-1}_{*}Q
is contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
by the first assertion of Proposition
3.3
.
Then
(f\circ g)^{-1}_{*}Q
is a component of
F^{\prime}
, and therefore

a(Q,X,\Delta)<a(Q,Y^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}).

This implies the first condition of the good minimal model in Definition
3.1
.
We recall the hypothesis of Proposition
3.3
that
K_{Y}+\Gamma=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E
and any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
P
on
Y
satisfies
a(P,X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
P\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since
\phi
exactly contracts divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, for any prime divisor
P^{\prime}
on
Y^{\prime}
, we have

a(Q^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})=-{\rm coeff}_{Q^{\prime}}(\Gamma^{\prime})=-{\rm coeff}_{\phi^{-1}_{*}Q^{\prime}}(\Gamma-E)=a(Q^{\prime},X,\Delta)

and
a(Q^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})\leq-1
if
Q^{\prime}
is exceptional over
X
.
This is the second condition of the good minimal model in Definition
3.1
.
The third condition of the good minimal model is clear.
From these facts,
(Y^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
.
∎

3.2.
Minimal model program

In this subsection, we define a step of a minimal model program and we discuss construction of a minimal model program for normal pairs.

Definition 3.4

(Minimal model program)
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism from a normal quasi-projective variety
X
to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
, and let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.

A
step of a
D
-MMP over
Z
is the diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z}

consisting of normal quasi-projective varieties
X
,
X^{\prime}
, and
V
, which are projective over
Z
, such that

•

X\to V
is a birational morphism and
X^{\prime}\to V
is a small birational morphism,

•

-D
is ample over
V
, and

•

\phi_{*}D
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and ample over
V
.

Sometimes we call
X\to V
a
D
-negative extremal contraction
.

A
sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z
is a sequence of birational contractions

X=:X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

such that each birational contraction
X_{i}\dashrightarrow X_{i+1}
forms a step of a
D_{i}
-MMP over
Z
, where
D_{i}
is the birational transform of
D
on
X_{i}
.
When
D
is of the form
K_{X}+B
for some normal pair
(X,B)
, then a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP over
Z
is often denoted by

(X,B)=:(X_{1},B_{1})\dashrightarrow(X_{2},B_{2})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},B_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots.

Let
A
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
such that
D+\lambda A
is nef over
Z
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.
We say that a sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z

X=:X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a
sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
if we put
D_{i}
(resp.
A_{i}
) as the birational transform of
D
and (resp.
A
) on
X_{i}
, then the following conditions hold.

•

A_{i}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier,

•

the non-negative real number

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$D_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

is well defined, and

•

(D_{i}+\lambda_{i}A_{i})\cdot C_{i}=0
for any curve
C_{i}\subset X_{i}
that is contracted by the
D_{i}
-negative extremal contraction of the MMP.

Remark 3.5
.

With notation as in Definition
3.4
, the morphism
X\to V
in a step of a
D
-MMP over
Z
does not necessarily satisfy
\rho(X/V)=1
.
This is the difference between a step of an MMP in Definition
3.4
and a usual step of an MMP as in
[
F17
, 4.9.1]
.
In particular,
X\to V
and
X^{\prime}\to V
in Definition
3.4
can be isomorphisms.
We adopt this definition for the convenience of proofs of results in this paper.

Remark 3.6
.

Let

X=:X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z
.
Then the following statements hold.

•

For any
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
D^{\prime}
on
X
such that
D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}uD
for some
u\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, the
D
-MMP is also a sequence of steps of a
D^{\prime}
-MMP over
Z
.

•

Suppose that the
D
-MMP is a sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
A
.
We set

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$D_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
.
By taking a common resolution of
X_{i}\dashrightarrow X_{i+1}
and using the negativity lemma, we see that
D_{i}+\lambda_{i-1}A_{i}
is nef over
Z
.
In particular, we have
\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda_{i+1}
.

•

Suppose that the
D
-MMP is a sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
A
.
Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, the
D
-MMP is a sequence of steps of a
(D-tA)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
A
.

•

Suppose that the
D
-MMP is a sequence of steps of a
D
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
A
.
Let
\lambda
be a nonnegative real number such that
D+\lambda A
is nef over
Z
.
Let
\lambda^{\prime}
be a positive real number such that
\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda
.
Then the equality

D+\mu A=\frac{\lambda^{\prime}-\mu}{\lambda^{\prime}}(D+\frac{\mu}{\lambda^{\prime}-\mu}(D+\lambda^{\prime}A))

holds for all
\mu<\lambda^{\prime}
, and therefore the
D
-MMP is also a
D
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
D+\lambda^{\prime}A
.

•

By the same argument as in
[
KM98
, Proof of Lemma 3.38]
, we can check that for any
i\geq 1
, the birational map
X\dashrightarrow X_{i}
is an isomorphism on an open subset
U_{i}\subset X
whose complement is contained in
{\rm NNef}(D/Z)
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme such that
X
is a normal variety.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
.
Suppose that
\omega
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective and
{\rm NNef}(\omega/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
.
Let
A
be an effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
such that
\omega+A
is
\pi
-nef and
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega+A)={\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
set theoretically.
We put

\lambda:={\rm inf}\{t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\omega+tA\text{ is nef over $Z$}\,\}.

Then
\lambda=0
or there exists an
\omega
-negative extremal ray
R
of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
such that
R
is rational and relatively ample at infinity (
[
F17
, Definition 6.7.2]
) and
(\omega+\lambda A)\cdot R=0
.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the argument in the lc case.
We may assume
\lambda>0
because otherwise there is nothing to prove.
By the cone theorem
[
F17
, Theorem 6.7.4]
, we may write

\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)=\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)_{\omega\geq 0}+\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)_{-\infty}+\sum_{j}R_{j}

for
\omega
-negative extremal rays
R_{j}
of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
that are rational and relatively ample at infinity.
Then
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)_{-\infty}\subset\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)_{\omega\geq 0}
since
{\rm NNef}(\omega/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
.
From this, we see that any
\omega
-negative extremal ray of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
is rational and relatively ample at infinity.
Since
\lambda>0
, there exists at least one
\omega
-negative extremal ray
R_{j}
of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
that is rational and relatively ample at infinity.

We fix a
\pi
-ample Cartier divisor
L
on
X
.
For each index
j
, let
C_{j}
be a curve on
X
such that the numerical class of
C_{j}
lies in
R_{j}
and

(L\cdot C_{j})={\rm min}\{(L\cdot C)\,|\,\text{the numerical class of $C$ lies in $R_{j}$}\}.

We call
C_{j}
a minimal curve of
R_{j}
.
Such
C_{j}
exists and

0<-(\omega\cdot C_{j})\leq 2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X

because
[
F21
, Theorem 1.6 (iii)]
shows the existence of a rational curve
C^{\prime}_{j}
spanning
R_{j}
such that
0<-(\omega\cdot C^{\prime}_{j})\leq 2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X
.

By applying
[
F21
, Lemma 4.25]
to
[X,\omega+\lambda A]
, we can find positive real numbers
r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{m}
and
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisors
\Theta_{1},\,\cdots,\,\Theta_{m}
on
X
such that

•

\sum_{i=1}^{m}r_{i}=1
and
\sum_{i=1}^{m}r_{i}\Theta_{i}=\omega+\lambda A
, and

•

for all
1\leq i\leq m
, the structure of a quasi-log scheme
[X,\Theta_{i}]
has the same non-qlc locus as
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega+\lambda A)
.

By the conditions
\lambda\leq 1
and
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega+A)={\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
set theoretically, for any indices
i
and
j
, the second condition implies that
R_{j}
is rational and relatively ample at infinity with respect to
[X,\Theta_{i}]
.
By
[
F21
, Theorem 1.6 (iii)]
, if
R_{j}
is
\Theta_{i}
-negative then there is a rational curve
C_{j}^{(i)}
spanning
R_{j}
such that
0<-(\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{j}^{(i)})\leq 2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X
.
By the definition of the minimal curve
C_{j}
of
R_{j}
, we have
C_{j}^{(i)}=\alpha\cdot C_{j}
in
N_{1}(X/Z)
for some real number
\alpha\geq 1
.
Hence,

-(\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{j})\leq 2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X.

If
R_{j}
is not
\Theta_{i}
-negative, then clearly
(\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{j})\geq-2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X
.
From this, we have

(\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{j})\geq-2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X

for any
i
and
j
.

Let
V\subset{\rm WDiv}_{\mathbb{R}}(X)
be a rational polytope spanned by
\Theta_{1},\,\cdots,\,\Theta_{m}
.
We will prove that the set

V^{\prime}:=\{\Theta^{\prime}\in V\,|\,\text{$(\Theta^{\prime}\cdot C_{j})\geq 0$ for all $j$}\}

is a rational polytope.
Fix
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that all
p\Theta_{i}
are Cartier.
For each
j
, we set

\mathcal{H}_{j}:=\left\{(t_{1},\cdots,\,t_{m})\in(\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})^{m}\,\middle|\,\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}=1,\,\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}(p\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{j})\geq 0\right\}.

Then it is sufficient to prove that
\bigcap_{j}\mathcal{H}_{j}
is a rational polytope because the linear map

\mathbb{R}^{m}\ni(t_{1},\cdots,\,t_{m})\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{m}t_{i}\Theta_{i}\in{\rm WDiv}_{\mathbb{R}}(X)

induces a surjective map from
\bigcap_{j}\mathcal{H}_{j}
to
V^{\prime}
.
We will prove that
\bigcap_{j}\mathcal{H}_{j}
is the intersection of finitely many
\mathcal{H}_{j}
.
Suppose by contradiction that
\bigcap_{j}\mathcal{H}_{j}
cannot be represented by the intersection of any finitely many
\mathcal{H}_{j}
.
By taking a subset of
\{\mathcal{H}_{j}\}_{j}
, we get an infinite sequence
\{\mathcal{H}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
such that
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{H}_{k}\supsetneq\bigcap_{k=1}^{n+1}\mathcal{H}_{k}
for all
n
.
For every
1\leq i\leq m
, by the facts
(\Theta_{i}\,\cdot\,C_{k})\geq-2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X
and
(p\Theta_{i}\,\cdot\,C_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}
,
the set
\{(p\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{k})\,|\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\}
satisfies the descending chain condition.
Replacing
\{\mathcal{H}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
by a subsequence, we may assume
(p\Theta_{1}\cdot C_{k})\leq(p\Theta_{1}\cdot C_{k+1})
for all
k
.
The property
\bigcap_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{H}_{k}\supsetneq\bigcap_{k=1}^{n+1}\mathcal{H}_{k}
is preserved after this replacement.
Replacing
\{\mathcal{H}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
by a subsequence again, we may assume
(p\Theta_{2}\cdot C_{k})\leq(p\Theta_{2}\cdot C_{k+1})
for all
k
.
Repeating this discussion and replacing
\{\mathcal{H}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
with a subsequence, we may assume that

(p\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{k})\leq(p\Theta_{i}\cdot C_{k+1})

for every
i
and
k
.
Then
\mathcal{H}_{1}\subset\mathcal{H}_{2}
by the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{k}
, which is a contradiction.
From this argument,
\bigcap_{j}\mathcal{H}_{j}
is the intersection of finitely many
\mathcal{H}_{j}
.
Thus
\bigcap_{j}\mathcal{H}_{j}
is a rational polytope, and so
V^{\prime}
is.

By the above argument, we can find positive real numbers
r^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{l}
and
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisors
\Theta^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\Theta^{\prime}_{l}
on
X
such that

•

\sum_{i=1}^{l}r^{\prime}_{i}=1
and
\sum_{i=1}^{l}r^{\prime}_{i}\Theta^{\prime}_{i}=\omega+\lambda A
, and

•

(\Theta^{\prime}_{i}\cdot C_{j})\geq 0
for any
1\leq i\leq l
and
j
.

For any
\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda
, there is an index
j
such that
(\omega+\lambda^{\prime}A)\cdot C_{j}<0
.
We also recall that
0<-(\omega\cdot C_{j})\leq 2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X
.
From these facts, we have

\begin{split}(\omega+\lambda A)\cdot C_{j}=&(\omega+\lambda^{\prime}A)\cdot C_{j}+(\lambda-\lambda^{\prime})(A\cdot C_{j})<\frac{(\lambda-\lambda^{\prime})}{\lambda^{\prime}}(\lambda^{\prime}A\cdot C_{j})\\
=&\frac{(\lambda-\lambda^{\prime})}{\lambda^{\prime}}\bigl((\omega+\lambda^{\prime}A)\cdot C_{j}-(\omega\cdot C_{j})\bigr)<2\frac{(\lambda-\lambda^{\prime})}{\lambda^{\prime}}\cdot{\rm dim}\,X\end{split}

for any
\lambda^{\prime}
and
C_{j}
as above.
On the other hand, putting
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
so that all
p^{\prime}\Theta^{\prime}_{i}
are Cartier, then the two conditions stated at the start of this paragraph imply that

(\omega+\lambda A)\cdot C_{j}=\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}r^{\prime}_{i}(p^{\prime}\Theta^{\prime}_{i}\cdot C_{j})

is zero or not less than
\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}{\rm min}\{r^{\prime}_{i}\,|\,1\leq i\leq l\}
.
By choosing
\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda
sufficiently close to
\lambda
, we obtain an index
j
such that
(\omega+\lambda A)\cdot C_{j}=0
.
Then the corresponding
\omega
-negative extremal ray
R:=R_{j}
of
\overline{\rm NE}(X/Z)
satisfies the condition of Lemma
3.7
.
∎

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
.
Let
\varphi\colon X\to V
be a birational morphism over
Z
, where
V
is normal and projective over
Z
, such that
-\omega
is
\varphi
-ample and
\varphi
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
.
Then we can construct a diagram

\textstyle{(Y,\Delta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{[X,\omega]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{[X^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{V}

over
Z
such that

•

f^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to[X^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}]
is a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair such that
Y^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime}
are projective over
V
,

•

(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
V
, and

•

\varphi^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to V
is a projective small birational morphism and the
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\omega^{\prime}
is the birational transform of
\omega
on
X^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{\prime}
-ample.

Furthermore, if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then
Y^{\prime}
is also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Proof.

Let
U_{1}\subset V
be the largest open subset over which
\varphi
is an isomorphism.
Then
U_{1}\supset\varphi({\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega))
by our assumption.
We put
U_{2}=V\setminus\varphi({\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega))
.
Note that
U_{2}
is open and
V=U_{1}\cup U_{2}
.
We put

\begin{split}Y_{1}:=(\varphi\circ f)^{-1}(U_{1}),\quad Y_{2}:=(\varphi\circ f)^{-1}(U_{2}),\quad\Delta_{1}:=\Delta|_{Y_{1}},\quad{\rm and}\quad\Delta_{2}:=\Delta|_{Y_{2}}.\end{split}

Then
(Y_{2},\Delta_{2})
is lc and
(K_{Y}+\Delta)|_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,U_{1}\cap U_{2}}0
.
By our assumption on
\varphi\colon X\to V
, we can find a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
H
on
X
such that
\omega+H\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,V}0
.
We set
H_{2}:=f^{*}H|_{Y_{2}}
.
Then
K_{Y_{2}}+\Delta_{2}+H_{2}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,U_{2}}0
.
Taking
H
generally, we may assume that
H
is effective and
(Y_{2},\Delta_{2}+H_{2})
is lc.
By
[
H19
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
HH20
, Theorem 1.7]
, there exists a sequence
(Y_{2},\Delta_{2})\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
of steps of a
(K_{Y_{2}}+\Delta_{2})
-MMP over
U_{2}

to a good minimal model
(Y^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
over
U_{2}
.
If
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then
Y_{2}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, and therefore
Y^{\prime}_{2}
is also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial by construction of a sequence of steps of the standard log MMP for
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial lc pairs
[
F17
, 4.8.16]
.
Since the relation
(K_{Y}+\Delta)|_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,U_{1}\cap U_{2}}0
holds, the
(K_{Y_{2}}+\Delta_{2})
-MMP does not modify
Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}
.

\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hskip-13.0pt\supset}
\textstyle{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hskip 13.0pt\subset}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hskip-13.0pt\supset}
\textstyle{U_{1}\cap U_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\hskip 13.0pt\subset}
\textstyle{U_{2}}

Therefore, we can glue
Y_{1}\to U_{1}
and
Y^{\prime}_{2}\to U_{2}
along
Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}\to U_{1}\cap U_{2}
and we obtain a projective morphism
g\colon(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to V
such that the inverse image of
U_{1}
(resp.
U_{2}
) is
(Y_{1},\Delta_{1})
(resp.
(Y^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}_{2})
).
By the same argument, over
U_{1}\cap U_{2}
we may glue
Y_{1}\to U_{1}
and any variety appearing in the
(K_{Y_{2}}+\Delta_{2})
-MMP over
U_{2}
.
Thus, the map

(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})

is a finite sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
V
.
If
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then
Y_{1}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.
Since
Y^{\prime}_{2}
is also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, we see that
Y^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

By construction,
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
is semi-ample over
V
.
Let
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
be the contraction over
V
induced by
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
.
Let
\varphi^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to V
be the induced morphism.
We will check that
\varphi^{\prime}
is a projective small birational morphism.
By construction,
\varphi^{\prime}
is an isomorphism over
U_{1}
, and therefore
\varphi^{\prime}
is birational.
If there is a
\varphi^{\prime}
-exceptional prime divisor
Q^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
, then there is a prime divisor
P^{\prime}
on
Y^{\prime}
such that
f^{\prime}(P^{\prime})=Q^{\prime}
.
Then the inverse map
Y^{\prime}\dashrightarrow Y
is not an isomorphism on the generic point of
P^{\prime}
.
This contradicts the fact that
(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
V
.
Thus, there is no
\varphi^{\prime}
-exceptional prime divisor, and
\varphi^{\prime}
is a projective small birational morphism.

Let
\omega^{\prime}
be the birational transform of
\omega
on
X^{\prime}
.
We will check that the diagram

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{V}

satisfies all the conditions of Theorem
3.8
.
The birational map
(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a finite sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
V
, which is the second condition of Theorem
3.8
.
We have already check that
\varphi^{\prime}
is small, and this fact also shows that
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a birational contraction.
Let
\omega_{Y^{\prime}}
be the birational transform of
f^{*}\omega
on
Y^{\prime}
.
Then
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}
.
Therefore,
\omega_{Y^{\prime}}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and semi-ample over
V
, and
f^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
is the contraction over
V
induced by
\omega_{Y^{\prime}}
.
Thus, there exists a
\varphi^{\prime}
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
such that
\omega_{Y^{\prime}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime*}D^{\prime}
.
We recall that
U_{1}\subset V
is the largest open subset over which
\varphi
is an isomorphism.
This implies that
\varphi^{\prime}
is an isomorphism over
U_{1}
and
{\rm codim}_{V}(V\setminus U_{1})\geq 2
.
By restricting the above diagram over
U_{1}
, we have

f^{\prime}|_{Y_{1}}^{*}(\omega^{\prime}|_{\varphi^{\prime-1}(U_{1})})=\omega_{Y^{\prime}}|_{Y_{1}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}|_{Y_{1}}^{*}(D^{\prime}|_{\varphi^{\prime-1}(U_{1})}).

This implies
\omega^{\prime}|_{\varphi^{\prime-1}(U_{1})}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D^{\prime}|_{\varphi^{\prime-1}(U_{1})}
.
Moreover, we have
{\rm codim}_{X^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}\setminus\varphi^{\prime-1}(U_{1}))\geq 2
since
\varphi^{\prime}
is small.
From these facts, we have
\omega^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D^{\prime}
.
Thus shows that
\omega^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and
\varphi^{\prime}
-ample.
Therefore, the third condition of Theorem
3.8
holds.
We also see that
K_{Y^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime*}\omega^{\prime}
.
This induces the structure of a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
f^{\prime}\colon(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to[X^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}]
, which is the first condition of Theorem
3.8
.

We have already checked that the
\mathbb{Q}
-factoriality of
Y
implies the
\mathbb{Q}
-factoriality of
Y^{\prime}
.
By these arguments, we obtain the desired diagram of Theorem
3.8
.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

Let

\textstyle{(Y,\Delta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{[X,\omega]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{[X^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{V}

be the diagram over
Z
in Theorem
3.8
.
By construction in the proof of Theorem
3.8
, we can write
V=U_{1}\cup U_{2}
with
Y_{2}:=(\varphi\circ f)^{-1}(U_{2})
and
Y^{\prime}_{2}:=(\varphi^{\prime}\circ f^{\prime})^{-1}(U_{2})

such that the
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP
(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
over
V
is an isomorphism on
(\varphi\circ f)^{-1}(U_{1})
and
(Y_{2},\Delta|_{Y_{2}})\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime}_{2},\Delta^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}_{2}})
is the usual log MMP over
U_{2}
for the lc pair
(Y_{2},\Delta|_{Y_{2}})
.
The
i
-th step of the
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
V
can be written as

\textstyle{(Y^{(i)},\Delta^{(i)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y^{(i+1)},\Delta^{(i+1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W^{(i)},}

where
-(K_{Y^{(i)}}+\Delta^{(i)})
and
K_{Y^{(i+1)}}+\Delta^{(i+1)}
are ample over
W^{(i)}
.
Then
Y^{(i)}\to W^{(i)}
is an isomorphism on the inverse image of
U_{1}
.
For any
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
D
on
Y^{(i)}
, we can find
r\in\mathbb{R}
such that
D-r(K_{Y^{(i)}}+\Delta^{(i)})
is numerically trivial over
W^{(i)}
.
In particular, the equality
\rho(Y^{(i)}/W^{(i)})=1
holds.

Theorem 3.10
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism to a quasi-projective scheme
Z
.
Suppose that
\omega
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective and
{\rm NNef}(\omega/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
.
Let
A
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
such that
\omega+\lambda_{0}A
is
\pi
-ample for some positive real number
\lambda_{0}
.
Then there exists a diagram

\textstyle{(Y,\Delta)=:(Y_{k_{1}},\Delta_{k_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f=:f_{1}}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{i}},\Delta_{k_{i}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{[X,\omega]=:[X_{1},\omega_{1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X_{2},\omega_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X_{i},\omega_{i}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

over
Z
, where all
Y_{k_{i}}
and
X_{i}
are projective over
Z
, such that

•

f_{i}\colon(Y_{k_{i}},\Delta_{k_{i}})\to[X_{i},\omega_{i}]
are quasi-log schemes induced by a normal pairs,

•

the sequence of upper horizontal maps is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
f^{*}A
,

•

the lower horizontal sequence of maps is a sequence of steps of an
\omega
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
, and

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then the following properties hold.

–

\lambda_{i}>\lambda_{i+1}
for all
i\geq 1
,

–

\omega_{i}+\lambda_{i-1}A_{i}
is semi-ample over
Z
for all
i\geq 1
, and

–

\omega_{i}+tA_{i}
is ample over
Z
for all
i\geq 1
and
t\in(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i-1})
.

Furthermore, if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then
Y_{k_{i}}
is also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial for every
i\geq 1
.

Proof.

We put
X_{1};=X
,
\omega_{1}:=\omega
,
Y_{k_{1}};=Y
,
\Delta_{k_{1}}:=\Delta
,
f_{1}:=f
, and
A_{1}:=A
.
We define

\lambda_{1}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{1}+\mu A_{1}$ is nef over $Z$}\}.

Then
\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{0}
.
If
\lambda_{1}=0
, there is nothing to prove.
Thus we may assume
\lambda_{1}>0
.
Then

\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}A_{1}=\frac{\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{0}}\left(\omega_{1}+\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}}(\omega_{1}+\lambda_{0}A_{1})\right).

Since
\omega_{1}+\lambda_{0}A_{1}
is ample over
Z
and the non-nef locus of
\omega_{1}
over
Z
does not intersect
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{1},\omega_{1})
, the
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle
(\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}A_{1})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X_{1},\omega_{1})}
is ample over
Z
.
By applying Theorem
2.21
to
[X_{1},\omega_{1}]
and
\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}}(\omega_{1}+\lambda_{0}A_{1})
, we see that
\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}A_{1}
is
\pi
-semi-ample.
Thus we get a contraction
\varphi\colon X_{1}\to V
over
Z
induced by
\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}A_{1}
.
Then
-\omega_{1}
is
\varphi
-ample since
\omega_{1}+\lambda_{0}A_{1}
is
\pi
-ample and
0<\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{0}
.
Moreover, since the non-nef locus of
\omega_{1}
over
Z
does not intersect
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{1},\omega_{1})
, any curve intersecting
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{1},\omega_{1})
has a positive intersection number with
\omega_{1}+\lambda_{1}A_{1}
.
Hence
\varphi
is birational and an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{1},\omega_{1})
.
By Theorem
3.8
, we get the diagram

\textstyle{(Y_{k_{1}},\Delta_{k_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{[X_{1},\omega_{1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{[X_{2},\omega_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{V}

over
Z
such that
\varphi^{\prime}\colon X_{2}\to V
is a small projective birational morphism,
\omega_{2}
is
\varphi^{\prime}
-ample, and the upper horizontal map is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{k_{1}}}+\Delta_{k_{1}})
-MMP over
V
.
Then
K_{Y_{k_{1}}}+\Delta_{k_{1}}+\lambda_{1}f_{1}^{*}A_{1}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,V}0
.
This implies that
(Y_{k_{1}},\Delta_{k_{1}})\dashrightarrow(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{k_{1}}}+\Delta_{k_{1}})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
f_{1}^{*}A_{1}
.

Let
A_{2}
be the birational transform of
A_{1}
on
X_{2}
.
By construction, there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
L
on
V
such that
L
is ample over
Z
and
\omega_{2}+\lambda_{1}A_{2}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{\prime*}L
.
Since
\omega_{2}
is
\varphi^{\prime}
-ample, there exists
\lambda^{\prime}_{1}<\lambda_{1}
, which is sufficiently close to
\lambda_{1}
, such that
\omega_{2}+\lambda^{\prime}_{1}A_{2}
is ample over
Z
.
By this discussion and the property in Remark
3.6
on the non-nef locus over
Z
(Definition
2.9
), we see that
(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\to[X_{2},\omega_{2}]\to Z
and
A_{2}
satisfy the hypothesis of Theorem
3.10
.
We define

\lambda_{2}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{2}+\mu A_{2}$ is nef over $Z$}\}.

Then
\lambda_{2}\leq\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda_{1}
by construction.
We apply the argument of the previous paragraph to
(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\to[X_{2},\omega_{2}]\to Z
and
A_{2}
, and we get the diagram

\textstyle{(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{3}},\Delta_{k_{3}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{[X_{2},\omega_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X_{3},\omega_{3}]}

over
Z
.
By repeating this discussion, we obtain the desired diagram.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(Y,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
K_{Y}+\Delta
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective and
{\rm NNef}(K_{Y}+\Delta/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Let
A
be an effective
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
Y
such that
K_{Y}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-nef and
{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)={\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta+A)
set theoretically.
Then we can run a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A

\textstyle{(Y,\Delta)=:(Y_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots,}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(Y_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
is a step of a
(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i})
-MMP over
Z
, such that the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)
and
\rho(Y_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
3.7
,
[
F17
, Theorem 6.7.3]
, and Theorem
3.8
.
∎

Corollary 3.12
.

Let
\pi\colon Y\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(Y,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
K_{Y}+\Delta
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective and
{\rm NNef}(K_{Y}+\Delta/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Let
A
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
Y
such that
K_{Y}+\Delta+\lambda_{0}A
is
\pi
-ample for some positive real number
\lambda_{0}
.
Then there exists a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A

\textstyle{(Y,\Delta)=:(Y_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots,}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(Y_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
is a step of a
(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i})
-MMP over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)
,

•

\rho(Y_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
, and

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
and
\lambda:={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
, then then the following properties hold.

–

the MMP terminates after finitely many steps or otherwise
\lambda\neq\lambda_{i}
for every
i\geq 1
, and

–

K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+tA_{i}
is semi-ample over
Z
for all
i\geq 1
and any
t\in(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i-1}]
.

Furthermore, if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then
Y_{i}
is also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial for every
i\geq 1
.

Proof.

Put
X:=Y
and
\omega:=K_{Y}+\Delta
.
We apply Theorem
3.10
to
(Y,\Delta)\overset{{\rm id}_{Y}}{\longrightarrow}[X,\omega]
, and we get a diagram

\textstyle{(Y,\Delta)=:(Y_{k_{1}},\Delta_{k_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}_{Y}=:f_{1}}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{2}},\Delta_{k_{2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{i}},\Delta_{k_{i}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{[X,\omega]=:[X_{1},\omega_{1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X_{2},\omega_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X_{i},\omega_{i}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

over
Z
satisfying the properties of Theorem
3.10
.
We check that the sequences of upper horizontal maps

(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{j},\Delta_{j})\dashrightarrow\cdots

satisfies the properties of Corollary
3.12
.
By the second property of Theorem
3.10
, the sequence of maps are the sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
.
By Remark
3.6
, the first property of Corollary
3.12
holds.
By construction of the above diagram (see Theorem
3.8
and Theorem
3.10
) and Remark
3.9
, the second property of Corollary
3.12
holds.
For each
i
, let
A_{k_{i}}
and
A_{X_{i}}
be the birational transforms of
A
on
Y_{k_{i}}
and
A_{X_{i}}
respectively.
Since the sequences of upper horizontal maps and the lower horizontal maps are sequence of steps of
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
, both
A_{k_{i}}
and
A_{X_{i}}
are
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier, and we can easily check that
A_{k_{i}}=f_{i}^{*}A_{X_{i}}
by an induction on
i
.
This implies

K_{Y_{k_{i}}}+\Delta_{k_{i}}+tA_{k_{i}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{i}+tf_{i}^{*}A_{X_{i}}

for all
i\geq 1
and
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.
We put

\lambda_{j}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{Y_{j}}+\Delta_{j}+\mu A_{j}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
j\geq 1
.
By the properties in Theorem
3.10
,
\lambda_{k_{i}}>\lambda_{k_{i+1}}
and
K_{Y_{k_{i}}}+\Delta_{k_{i}}+tA_{k_{i}}
is semi-ample over
Z
for all
i\geq 1
and
t\in(\lambda_{k_{i}},\lambda_{k_{i-1}}]
.
By construction of the MMP (see Theorem
3.8
), we have
\lambda_{j}=\lambda_{k_{i}}
for every
k_{i}\leq j<k_{i+1}
.
This implies that
K_{Y_{j}}+\Delta_{j}+tA_{j}
is semi-ample over
Z
for all
t\in(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j-1}]
.
By this discussion, the MMP

(Y,\Delta)\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{j},\Delta_{j})\dashrightarrow\cdots

satisfies the properties of Corollary
3.12
.
The preservation of the
\mathbb{Q}
-factoriality can be checked by the argument as in
[
KM98
, Proposition 3.36 and Proposition 3.37]
.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

By the standard argument, we can check that the following properties of a normal pair
(Y,\Delta)
are preserved under the
(K_{Y}+\Delta)
-MMP in Corollary
3.11
and Corollary
3.12
.

•

Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,

•

(Y,0)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial klt, and

•

(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is dlt.

We close this section with a reduction of Question
1.1
to the termination of all MMP for klt pairs.

Remark 3.14
.

Assume the termination of all MMP for all klt pairs.
Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties. Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective. Suppose that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
over
Z
does not intersect the non-lc locus of
(X,\Delta)
.
Let

(X,\Delta)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
.
The existence of the
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP follows from Corollary
3.11
.
Let
f\colon(Y,\Gamma)\to(X,\Delta)
be a dlt blow-up in Theorem
2.16
.
By using the lift of MMP as in
[
B12
, Remark 2.9]
or using Theorem
3.8
repeatedly, we get a diagram

\textstyle{(Y,\Gamma)=:(Y_{k_{1}},\Gamma_{k_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f=:f_{1}}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{2}},\Gamma_{k_{2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{k_{i}},\Gamma_{k_{i}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{(X,\Delta)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{2},\Delta_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

over
Z
such that the upper horizontal sequence of birational maps

(Y,\Gamma)\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{j},\Gamma_{j})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y}+\Gamma)
-MMP.
By construction,
(Y,\Gamma^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
is dlt, and
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
implies
{\rm NNef}(K_{Y}+\Gamma/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Gamma)=\emptyset
.
By Remark
3.6
, this
(K_{Y}+\Gamma)
-MMP is a
(Y,\Gamma^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
-MMP over
Z
.
By the special termination
[
F07b
]
, after finitely many steps the
(K_{Y}+\Gamma^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\geq 1})
-MMP will be a
(K_{Y}+\Gamma^{<1})
-MMP.
Since the pair
(Y,\Gamma^{<1})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial klt, this MMP terminates by the termination of all MMP for all klt pairs.

4.
Minimal model program along Kawamata log terminal locus

In this section we study the minimal model theory for normal pairs such that the non-nef locus of the log canonical divisor is disjoint from the non-klt locus of the normal pair.

4.1.
From non-vanishing to existence of minimal model

In this subsection, we study the existence of minimal models under the assumption on the non-vanishing theorem.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
such that
|D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
.
Then there exist a projective birational morphism
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
\widetilde{X}
, an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\widetilde{M}
on
\widetilde{X}
, and an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\widetilde{F}
on
\widetilde{X}
satisfying the following properties.

•

f^{*}D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\widetilde{M}+\widetilde{F}
,

•

\widetilde{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

putting

\widetilde{\Gamma}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F},

then
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, and

•

for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
\widetilde{X}
, at least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds.

Proof.

By applying Theorem
2.6
to
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
\Delta
, and
D
, we get a log resolution
h\colon\overline{X}\to X
of
(X,\Delta)
and effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\overline{M}
and
\overline{F}
on
\overline{X}
such that

•

h^{*}D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\overline{M}+\overline{F}
,

•

every component of
\overline{F}
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|h^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

\overline{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
, and

•

{\rm Supp}(\overline{F}+h_{*}^{-1}\Delta)\cup{\rm Ex}(h)
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
\overline{X}
.

We put

\overline{\Gamma}=h_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E^{\prime}_{j}$:$h$-exceptional}}E^{\prime}_{j}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}.

Then
(\overline{X},\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1})
is a log smooth lc pair.
By construction, we may write

\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}=h_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E^{\prime}_{j}$:$h$-exceptional}}E^{\prime}_{j}+\overline{G}_{+}-\overline{G}_{-}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\overline{G}_{+}
and
\overline{G}_{-}
on
\overline{X}
such that
\overline{G}_{+}
and
\overline{G}_{-}
have no common components,
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{G}_{+}\subset{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
, and

{\rm Supp}\,\overline{G}_{-}\subset{\rm Supp}\,h_{*}^{-1}\Delta
.

We can write
\overline{M}=\sum_{k}r_{k}\overline{M}_{k}
for some positive real numbers
r_{k}
and Cartier divisors
\overline{M}_{k}
on
\overline{X}
that are semi-ample over
Z
. We fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
\alpha r_{k}>2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X
for all
k
and
\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{G}_{+}\geq 0
.
Since we can write

K_{\overline{X}}+h_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E^{\prime}_{j}$:$h$-exceptional}}E^{\prime}_{j}=h^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+\sum_{\text{$E^{\prime}_{j}$:$h$-exceptional}}(a(E^{\prime}_{j},X,\Delta)+1)E^{\prime}_{j},

this relation and
\overline{M}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,X}-\overline{F}
imply

\begin{split}K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\overline{M}=&K_{\overline{X}}+h_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E^{\prime}_{j}$:$h$-exceptional}}E^{\prime}_{j}+\overline{G}_{+}-\overline{G}_{-}+\alpha\overline{M}\\
\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,X}&\sum_{\text{$E^{\prime}_{j}$:$h$-exceptional}}(a(E^{\prime}_{j},X,\Delta)+1)E^{\prime}_{j}-(\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{G}_{+}+\overline{G}_{-}),\end{split}

where
\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{G}_{+}+\overline{G}_{-}\geq 0
by construction of
\alpha
.
Since
\overline{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
, we can run a
(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\overline{M})
-MMP over
X
with scaling of an ample divisor.
By running a
(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\overline{M})
-MMP over
X
with scaling of an ample divisor, we get a birational contraction

\phi\colon(\overline{X},\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\overline{M})\dashrightarrow(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\widetilde{M})

over
X
, where
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\phi_{*}\overline{\Gamma}
and
\widetilde{M}=\phi_{*}\overline{M}
, such that
K_{\widetilde{X}}+\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\widetilde{M}
is the limit of movable divisors over
X
.
By the choice of
\alpha
and the argument using the length of extremal rays (
[
BCHM10
, Theorem 3.8.1]
), we see that
\widetilde{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
.

We denote the induced birational morphism
\widetilde{X}\to X
by
f
, and we put
\widetilde{F}=\phi_{*}\overline{F}
.
We check that
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
,
\widetilde{M}
, and
\widetilde{F}
satisfy all the conditions of Proposition
4.1
.
By construction,
\widetilde{X}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.
Since
h^{*}D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\overline{M}+\overline{F}
, we have

f^{*}D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\widetilde{M}+\widetilde{F}.

Therefore, the first condition of Proposition
4.1
holds.
We pick an arbitrary element
\widetilde{L}\in|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Putting
\overline{L}:=h^{*}f_{*}L
, then
\overline{L}\in|h^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and we have
\phi_{*}\overline{L}=\widetilde{L}
by the negativity lemma.
Since every component of
\overline{F}
is contained in
{\rm Bs}|h^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we have
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{L}\supset{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
.
Therefore we have
{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{L}\supset{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F}
. Since
\widetilde{L}
is arbitrary, it follows that
{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
The inverse inclusion also holds by the semi-ampleness of
\widetilde{M}
over
Z
.
Therefore

{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}},

and we see that the second condition of Proposition
4.1
holds.
Now

\widetilde{\Gamma}=\phi_{*}\overline{\Gamma}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F}

and
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair by construction, which is the third condition of Proposition
4.1
.
We put
\widetilde{G}_{+}=\phi_{*}\overline{G}_{+}
and
\widetilde{G}_{-}=\phi_{*}\overline{G}_{-}
.
Then

\begin{split}K_{\widetilde{X}}+\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\widetilde{M}=&K_{\widetilde{X}}+h_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+\widetilde{G}_{+}-\widetilde{G}_{-}+\alpha\widetilde{M}\\
\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,X}&\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}(a(E_{i},X,\Delta)+1)E_{i}-(\alpha\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{G}_{+}+\widetilde{G}_{-}),\end{split}

and
\alpha\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{G}_{+}+\widetilde{G}_{-}\geq 0
by construction.
Since
K_{\widetilde{X}}+\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1}+\alpha\widetilde{M}
is the limit of movable divisors over
X
, the negativity lemma implies that

(\alpha\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{G}_{+}+\widetilde{G}_{-})-\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}(a(E_{i},X,\Delta)+1)E_{i}\geq 0.

From this fact, for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
, if
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)>-1
then
E_{i}
is an irreducible component of
\alpha\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{G}_{+}+\widetilde{G}_{-}
.
By recalling that
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{G}_{+}\subset{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
and that
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{G}_{-}\subset{\rm Supp}\,h_{*}^{-1}\Delta
, we have

{\rm Supp}\,(\alpha\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{G}_{+}+\widetilde{G}_{-})\subset{\rm Supp}\,(h_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\widetilde{F})={\rm Supp}\,h_{*}^{-1}\Delta\cup{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

Therefore, any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
satisfying
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)>-1
is contained in
{\rm Supp}\,f_{*}^{-1}\Delta\cup{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since
E_{i}
is
f
-exceptional,
E_{i}
is contained in
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
This shows the final condition of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

This is a variant of Proposition
4.1
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
D
be an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor on
X
.
Suppose that there exists a resolution
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
of
X
for which
g^{*}D
has the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition over
Z
whose positive part is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then there exists a projective birational morphism
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
\widetilde{X}
such that putting
\widetilde{M}
and
\widetilde{F}
as the positive part and the negative part of the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
f^{*}D
over
Z
, respectively, then the following properties hold.

•

f^{*}D\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\widetilde{M}+\widetilde{F}
,

•

\widetilde{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

putting

\widetilde{\Gamma}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{F},

then
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, and

•

for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
\widetilde{X}
, at least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds.

Proof.

Let
g\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a resolution of
X
in the proposition.
By using the relative case of
[
N04
, III, 5.17 Corollary]
, for any projective birational morphism
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
from a smooth variety
X^{\prime\prime}
, the positive part of the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
g^{\prime*}g^{*}D
over
Z
is the pullback of the positive part of the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
g^{*}D
over
Z
.
In particular,
g^{\prime*}g^{*}D
has the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition over
Z
whose positive part is semi-ample over
Z
.
Therefore, for any
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
that is a log resolution of
(X,\Delta)
, the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of the pullback of
D
over
Z
has relatively semi-ample positive part.

By the above argument, we may take a log resolution
h\colon\overline{X}\to X
of
(X,\Delta)
such that the positive part of Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
f^{*}D
over
Z
, which we denote by
\overline{M}
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
We put
\overline{F}
as the negative part of Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
f^{*}D
over
Z
.
Then every component of
\overline{F}
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|h^{*}D/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
By replacing
h\colon\overline{X}\to X
with a higher log resolution, we may assume that
{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{F}+h_{*}^{-1}\Delta)\cup{\rm Ex}(h)
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
\overline{X}
.
Then the proof of Proposition
4.1
works with no changes by using
h\colon\overline{X}\to X
,
\overline{M}
, and
\overline{F}
.
∎

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Let
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
be a normal pair such that
\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\Delta
.
If
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
has a good minimal model over
Z
, then
(X,\Delta)
has a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model over
Z
.

Proof.

Since
K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}K_{X}+\Delta
and
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
has a good minimal model over
Z
, it follows that
K_{X}+\Delta
birationally has the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition over
Z
whose positive part is semi-ample over
Z
.
By Proposition
4.2
, we get a projective birational morphism
f\colon\overline{X}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
\overline{X}
such that putting
\overline{M}
and
\overline{F}
as the positive and the negative parts of the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)
over
Z
, respectively, then the following properties hold.

•

f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\overline{M}+\overline{F}
,

•

\overline{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

putting

\overline{\Gamma}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F},

then
(\overline{X},\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, and

•

for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
\overline{X}
, at least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds.

We may write

K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+\overline{E}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\overline{\Delta}
and
\overline{E}
that have no common components.
We put

\overline{B}:=\sum_{F_{j}\subset{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}}(1-{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\overline{\Delta}))F_{j}.

Since
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, we have
a(F_{j},X,\Delta)\geq-1
for any component
F_{j}
of
\overline{F}
.
By construction, the discrepancy of any component of
\overline{E}
with respect to
(X,\Delta)
is positive.
Thus
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{E}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, the following properties hold.

•

{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{B}+\overline{E})\subset{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
,

•

\overline{B}
is effective,

•

{\rm Supp}\,\overline{B}\cap{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Delta}^{>1}=\emptyset
, and

•

{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})=1
for any component
F_{j}
of
\overline{F}
.

Proposition
3.3
for
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
(X,\Delta)
,
f\colon\overline{X}\to X
, and
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})
implies that to prove the existence of a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
, it is enough to prove the existence of a birational contraction
\phi\colon\overline{X}\dashrightarrow\overline{X}^{\prime}
over
Z
, where
\overline{X}^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and projective over
Z
, such that
\phi_{*}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})
is semi-ample over
Z
and any prime divisor contracted by
\phi
is contained in
{\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.

In this paragraph, we will check that
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt on a neighborhood of
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
.
Since
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, we have

{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}\cap{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Delta}^{>1}=\emptyset.

From this and the definition of
\overline{B}
, we see that

{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}\cap{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})^{>1}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Delta}^{>1}=\emptyset.

By construction of
\overline{B}
, outside
{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})^{>1}
we have
\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}\leq\overline{\Gamma}
, where

\overline{\Gamma}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}.

Since
(\overline{X},\overline{\Gamma}^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\overline{\Gamma}^{\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, the following pair

(\overline{X},(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})^{\geq 1})

is dlt outside
{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})^{>1}
.
Hence,
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt on a neighborhood of
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
.

From now on, we will freely use the notation of the negative part of the relative Nakayama–Zariski decomposition in
[
N04
, III, §4]
.
We may write
\overline{M}=\sum_{k}r_{k}\overline{M}^{(k)}
for some positive real numbers
r_{k}
and Cartier divisors
\overline{M}^{(k)}
on
\overline{X}
that are semi-ample over
Z
.
We take
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
so that
\alpha r_{k}>2\cdot{\rm dim}\,\overline{X}
for all
k
.
Since
{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{B}+\overline{E})\subset{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
, choosing
\alpha
appropriately we may assume
\alpha\overline{F}\geq\overline{B}+\overline{E}
.
Since
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\overline{M}+\overline{F}
, we have

K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}(\alpha+1)f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)-(\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{E}-\overline{B}).

We have the equality
\overline{F}=N_{\sigma}(f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta);\overline{X}/Z)
, which is the definition of
\overline{F}
, and the obvious relation
(\alpha+1)\overline{F}\geq\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{E}-\overline{B}
.
By
[
N04
, III, 4.2 (1) Lemma]
, we obtain

\begin{split}&N_{\sigma}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M};\overline{X}/Z)\\
=&N_{\sigma}((\alpha+1)f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)-(\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{E}-\overline{B});\overline{X}/Z)\\
=&(\alpha+1)\overline{F}-(\alpha\overline{F}-\overline{E}-\overline{B})=\overline{F}+\overline{E}+\overline{B}.\end{split}

By using
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Supp}\,(\overline{F}+\overline{E}+\overline{B})
and
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we also have

\begin{split}{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}=&{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cup{\rm Supp}\,(\overline{E}+\overline{B})\\
\supset&{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+\overline{E}+\overline{B}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\\
\supset&{\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\supset{\rm Supp}\,N_{\sigma}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M};\overline{X}/Z),\end{split}

where the final inclusion follows from

\overline{X}\setminus{\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset\overline{X}\setminus{\rm Supp}\,N_{\sigma}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M};\overline{X}/Z).

From these discussions, we obtain

{\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm Supp}\,N_{\sigma}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M};\overline{X}/Z)={\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}.

Since
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt on a neighborhood of
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}
, by Corollary
3.12
, we can run a
(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of an ample divisor
\overline{A}

(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(\overline{X}_{l},\overline{\Delta}_{l}+\overline{B}_{l}+\alpha\overline{M}_{l})\dashrightarrow\cdots.

Now we have

K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}(\alpha+1)\overline{M}+\overline{F}+\overline{E}+\overline{B}.

By the choice of
\alpha
and the length of extremal rays (
[
F21
, Theorem 1.5 (iii)]
), we see that
\overline{M}_{l}
trivially intersects the curves contracted by the
(K_{\overline{X}_{l}}+\overline{\Delta}_{l}+\overline{B}_{l}+\alpha\overline{M}_{l})
-negative extremal contraction of the MMP.
Therefore the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is contained in
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We recall that
{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})=1
for any component
F_{j}
of
\overline{F}
, which follows from the definition of
\overline{B}
.
For each
l\geq 1
, we put

\lambda_{l}:={\rm inf}\set{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}{\text{$K_{\overline{X}_{l}}+\overline{\Delta}_{l}+\overline{B}_{l}+\alpha\overline{M}_{l}+\mu\overline{A}_{l}$ is nef over $Z$}}.

If
{\rm lim}_{l\to\infty}\lambda_{l}>0
, then the MMP is also a
(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M}+\lambda\overline{A})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
\overline{A}
.
Since the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is contained in
{\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}

and we have
{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})=1
for any component
F_{j}
of
\overline{F}
, we may apply the argument of
[
BCHM10
, Proof of Lemma 3.10.11 (2)]
and the special termination
[
F07b
]
with the aid of
[
BCHM10
, Theorem E]
and the
(\pi\circ f)
-ampleness of
\lambda\overline{A}
.
We see that the MMP terminates, however, it contradicts the fact
{\rm lim}_{l\to\infty}\lambda_{l}>0
.
Thus
{\rm lim}_{l\to\infty}\lambda_{l}=0
.
Since

{\rm Supp}\,N_{\sigma}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}+\alpha\overline{M};\overline{X}/Z)={\rm Supp}\,\overline{F}={\rm Supp}\,(\overline{F}+\overline{E}+\overline{B}),

we can find an index
l^{\prime}
such that
\overline{X}\dashrightarrow\overline{X}_{l^{\prime}}
contracts
\overline{F}+\overline{E}+\overline{B}
.
Then

K_{\overline{X}_{l^{\prime}}}+\overline{\Delta}_{l^{\prime}}+\overline{B}_{l^{\prime}}=(K_{\overline{X}_{l^{\prime}}}+\overline{\Delta}_{l^{\prime}}+\overline{B}_{l^{\prime}}+\alpha\overline{M}_{l^{\prime}})-\alpha\overline{M}_{l^{\prime}}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}(\alpha+1)\overline{M}_{l^{\prime}}-\alpha\overline{M}_{l^{\prime}}=\overline{M}_{l^{\prime}},

which is semi-ample over
Z
because
\overline{M}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
Putting
\overline{X}^{\prime}:=\overline{X}_{l^{\prime}}
and denoting
\overline{X}\dashrightarrow\overline{X}^{\prime}
by
\phi
, the birational contraction
\phi\colon\overline{X}\dashrightarrow\overline{X}^{\prime}
over
Z
satisfies

•

\overline{X}^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,

•

\phi_{*}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B})
is semi-ample over
Z
, and

•

any prime divisor contracted by
\phi
is a component of
{\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{B}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.

As discussed before, Proposition
3.3
implies the existence of a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model of
(X,\Delta)
over
Z
.
We finish the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be an effective
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)\neq\emptyset
and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)=\emptyset
.
Then
(X,\Delta+A)
has a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model over
Z
.

Proof.

Since
(X,\Delta)
is not klt, we see that
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\neq X
.
In particular,
|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
.
The idea is the same as the proof of Proposition
4.3
.
However, the proof of Theorem
4.4
is more complicated because we need to deal with many
\mathbb{R}
-divisors.
Therefore we divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1
.

In this step, we reduce the theorem to the case where
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)={\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)
.

Let
h\colon(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta})\to(X,\Delta)
be a dlt blow-up of
(X,\Delta)
.
Since
h^{*}A
is nef and big over
Z
, we may write
h^{*}A\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\overline{A}+\overline{G}
for some general
(\pi\circ h)
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\overline{A}
on
\overline{X}
and effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\overline{G}
on
\overline{X}
such that
{\rm Nklt}(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta})={\rm Nklt}(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G}+\overline{A})
as closed subschemes of
\overline{X}
.
Since

K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G}+\overline{A}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}h^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)

and

{\rm Nklt}(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G}+\overline{A})={\rm Nklt}(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta})\subset{\rm Nklt}(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{A})\subset h^{-1}({\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A))

set theoretically, we see that

\begin{split}&{\rm Bs}|K_{\overline{X}}+(\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G})+\overline{A}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G}+\overline{A})\\
\subset&{\rm Bs}|h^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap h^{-1}({\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A))=\emptyset.\end{split}

By Lemma
3.2
,
(X,\Delta+A)
has a good minimal model over
Z
if
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+h^{*}A)
has a good minimal model over
Z
.
By Proposition
4.3
,
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+h^{*}A)
has a good minimal model over
Z
if
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G}+\overline{A})
has a good minimal model over
Z
.
By replacing
(X,\Delta)
and
A
with
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}+\overline{G})
and
\overline{A}
respectively, we may assume that
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)={\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)
.

Step 2
.

In this step, we construct a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and some
\mathbb{R}
-divisors on
X^{\prime}
.

By applying Proposition
4.1
to
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
(X,\Delta)
, and
K_{X}+\Delta+A
, we obtain a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
X^{\prime}
, an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
M^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
, and an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
F^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
satisfying the following properties.

•

f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}M^{\prime}+F^{\prime}
,

•

M^{\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

putting

\Gamma^{\prime}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime},

then
(X^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\prime\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, and

•

for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
, at least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds.

We may write

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\Delta^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
that have no common components.
We put

B^{\prime}:=\sum_{F_{j}\subset{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}}(1-{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime}))F_{j}.

Since
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, the inequality
a(F_{j},X,\Delta)>-1
holds for any component
F_{j}
of
F^{\prime}
. Therefore
B
is effective.
Since
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, there exists an effective
f
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
G^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
such that
-G^{\prime}
is
f
-ample.
Rescaling
G^{\prime}
, we may assume that
-G^{\prime}+f^{*}A
is ample over
Z
.
We can write

G^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}

for some
\Theta^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\Phi^{\prime}\geq 0
such that every component of
\Theta^{\prime}
is a component of
B^{\prime}
and any component of
\Phi^{\prime}
is not a component of
B^{\prime}
.
By rescaling
G^{\prime}
again, we may further assume that
B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}\geq 0
.
Finally, we take a general member
A^{\prime}\in|-G^{\prime}+f^{*}A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
(X^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\prime\geq 1}+A^{\prime})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair. Then
A^{\prime}
is ample over
Z
.

By the above discussion, we obtain a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
satisfying the properties of Proposition
4.1
, an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
M^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
, effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
F^{\prime}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime}
,
B^{\prime}
,
G^{\prime}
,
\Theta^{\prime}
, and
\Phi^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
, and a general
(\pi\circ f)
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
A^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
such that

•

we can write

f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}M^{\prime}+F^{\prime}

such that
M^{\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

\Delta^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
satisfy

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}

and the condition that
\Delta^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
have no common components,

•

B^{\prime}
is defined by

B^{\prime}:=\sum_{F_{j}\subset{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}}(1-{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime}))F_{j},

•

G^{\prime}
is
f
-exceptional and we may write

G^{\prime}=\Theta^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}

such that
B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}\geq 0
and any component of
\Phi^{\prime}
is not a component of
B^{\prime}
,

•

\Gamma^{\prime}
is defined by

\Gamma^{\prime}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime},

and

•

we have

A^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}-G^{\prime}+g^{*}A

such that
(X^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\prime\geq 1}+A^{\prime})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
