-factorial dlt pair.

The divisor
\Gamma^{\prime}
and its property will be used in Step
5
.

Step 3
.

In this step, we study properties of the effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors defined in Step
2
.

By construction, the discrepancy of any component of
E^{\prime}
with respect to
(X,\Delta)
is positive.
Since at least
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
, we have
{\rm Supp}\,E^{\prime}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Now
{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}\subset{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
by construction, and
B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}\geq 0
implies
{\rm Supp}\,\Theta^{\prime}\subset{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}
.
From these facts and the property
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

{\rm Supp}\,(E^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Theta^{\prime})\subset{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

Since
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, the inequality
a(F_{j},X,\Delta)>-1
holds for any component
F_{j}
of
F^{\prime}
.
By the definitions of
B^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime}
, we have

{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}={\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

We also have
{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}\cap{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\prime\geq 1}=\emptyset
by construction of
B^{\prime}
.
The definition of
\Phi^{\prime}
shows that
\Phi^{\prime}
is
f
-exceptional and any component of
\Phi^{\prime}
is not an irreducible component of
{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}
.
Since at least
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
, the discrepancy of any component of
\Phi
with respect to
(X,\Delta)
is not greater than
-1
.
Then

{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}\cap{\rm Supp}\,(\Delta^{\prime\geq 1}+\Phi^{\prime})\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap f^{-1}({\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta))=\emptyset.

By the definition of
B^{\prime}
and the equality
{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}={\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
, it follows that

{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime})={\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime})=1

for any component
F_{j}
of
F^{\prime}
.

By construction in Step
2
, we can write

\begin{split}K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})\end{split}

and
B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}
is effective.
We can also write

\begin{split}K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}.\end{split}

From the above arguments, the following properties hold.

(a)

B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}
is effective and

{\rm Supp}\,(E^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Theta^{\prime})={\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}={\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}},

(b)

we have

{\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}\cap{\rm Supp}\,(\Delta^{\prime\geq 1}+\Phi^{\prime})=\emptyset,

(c)

the equality

{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime})=1

holds for any component
F_{j}
of
F^{\prime}
, and

(d)

we have

\begin{split}&K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}),\qquad{\rm and}\\
&K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}.\end{split}

Step 4
.

By (
a
) and (
d
), Proposition
3.3
for
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
(X,\Delta+A)
,
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
, and
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))
implies that to prove the existence of a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
, it is sufficient to prove the existence of a birational contraction
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
over
Z
, where
X^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and projective over
Z
, such that the divisor
\phi_{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))
is semi-ample over
Z
and any prime divisor contracted by
\phi
is an irreducible component of
{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Step 5
.

In this step, we will check that
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt on a neighborhood of
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
.

We set
U^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\setminus{\rm Supp}\,(\Delta^{\prime\geq 1}+\Phi^{\prime})
.
By the definitions of
B^{\prime}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}
in Step
2
, the divisor
(\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime})|_{U^{\prime}}
is a boundary
\mathbb{R}
-divisor and we have

(\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime})|_{U^{\prime}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}|_{U^{\prime}}.

Since
(X^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\prime\geq 1}+A^{\prime})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, which is the final property in the list of properties in Step
2
, we see that
(U^{\prime},(\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})|_{U^{\prime}})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair.
By (
a
) and (
b
) in Step
3
, we have

{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}\cap{\rm Supp}\,(\Delta^{\prime\geq 1}+\Phi^{\prime})={\rm Supp}\,B^{\prime}\cap{\rm Supp}\,(\Delta^{\prime\geq 1}+\Phi^{\prime})=\emptyset.

This shows that
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}\subset U^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt on a neighborhood of
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
.

Step 6
.

In this step, we will construct the birational contraction
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
over
Z
mentioned in Step
4
.

Let
M^{\prime}
be the
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X^{\prime}
defined in the construction of
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
in Step
1
.
We may write
M^{\prime}=\sum_{k}r_{k}M^{\prime(k)}
for some positive real numbers
r_{k}
and Cartier divisors
M^{\prime(k)}
on
X^{\prime}
that are semi-ample over
Z
.
We take
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
so that
\alpha r_{k}>2\cdot{\rm dim}\,X^{\prime}
for all
k
.
We fix a general element
N^{\prime}\in|M^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
such that

{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})={\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}).

By (
a
) and (
d
) in Step
3
, we have

\begin{split}&{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\\
\subset&{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\\
=&{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\\
\subset&{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cup{\rm Supp}\,(E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))={\rm Supp\,}F^{\prime}.\end{split}

By this property and the dlt property of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
on a neighborhood of
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
, the non-nef locus of
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime}
over
Z
does not intersect
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})
.

By Corollary
3.12
, we can run a
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of an ample divisor

(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime}_{l},\Delta^{\prime}_{l}+B^{\prime}_{l}+\Phi^{\prime}_{l}+A^{\prime}_{l}+\alpha N^{\prime}_{l})\dashrightarrow\cdots.

By the choice of
\alpha
and the length of extremal rays (
[
F21
, Theorem 1.5 (iii)]
), every
N^{\prime}_{l}
trivially intersects the curves contracted by the extremal contraction of the MMP.
Hence, the
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})
-MMP is a
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
-MMP and
N^{\prime}_{l}
is semi-ample over
Z
for every
l
.
By this fact and (
d
) in Step
3
, prime divisors contracted in this MMP are contained in

{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

By (
d
) in Step
3
and the relation
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}M^{\prime}+F^{\prime}
, which is the first property of
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
in Step
1
, we have

\begin{split}K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}&\;f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})+\alpha N^{\prime}\\
\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}&(\alpha+1)N^{\prime}+(F^{\prime}+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})).\end{split}

By (
a
) in Step
3
, we have

{\rm Supp}\,(F^{\prime}+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))={\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}.

Since every
N^{\prime}_{l}
trivially intersects the curves contracted by the extremal contraction of the
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})
-MMP, the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is contained in
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
.
By (
c
) in Step
3
and the dlt property of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
around
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
, we may apply the special termination
[
F07b
]
to each lc center contained in
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}
with the aid of
[
BCHM10
, Theorem E]
and the
(\pi\circ f)
-ampleness of
A^{\prime}
.
We note that we may apply
[
BCHM10
, Theorem E]
to our setting because we can use
A^{\prime}_{l}
and
[
BCHM10
, Proof of Lemma 3.10.11 (2)]
to produce an ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X^{\prime}_{l}
for every
l
.
We see that the
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})
-MMP over
Z
terminates with a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial minimal model over
Z

(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha N^{\prime})\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime}_{n},\Delta^{\prime}_{n}+B^{\prime}_{n}+\Phi^{\prime}_{n}+A^{\prime}_{n}+\alpha N^{\prime}_{n}).

Moreover,
N^{\prime}_{n}
is semi-ample over
Z
by construction.

Let
F^{\prime}_{n}
and
E^{\prime}_{n}
be the birational transforms of
F^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}_{n}
, respectively.
By Corollary
3.11
, we may run a
(K_{X^{\prime}_{n}}+\Delta^{\prime}_{n}+B^{\prime}_{n}+\Phi^{\prime}_{n}+A^{\prime}_{n})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
\alpha N^{\prime}_{n}
.
By the same argument as above, we may apply the special termination
[
F07b
]
to each lc center contained in
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}_{n}
with the aid of
[
BCHM10
, Theorem E]
.
We note that we have

K_{X^{\prime}_{n}}+\Delta^{\prime}_{n}+B^{\prime}_{n}+\Phi^{\prime}_{n}+A^{\prime}_{n}+tN^{\prime}_{n}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}(t+1)N^{\prime}_{n}+(F^{\prime}_{n}+E^{\prime}_{n}+(B^{\prime}_{n}-\Theta^{\prime}_{n}))

for all
0\leq t\leq\alpha
, and
{\rm Supp}\,(F^{\prime}_{n}+E^{\prime}_{n}+(B^{\prime}_{n}-\Theta^{\prime}_{n}))={\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}_{n}
by (
a
) in Step
3
.
Hence, the
(K_{X^{\prime}_{n}}+\Delta^{\prime}_{n}+B^{\prime}_{n}+\Phi^{\prime}_{n}+A^{\prime}_{n})
-MMP only modifies
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}_{n}
, and if all components of
F^{\prime}_{n}
are contracted after finitely many steps then the MMP terminates.
We see that the MMP terminates with a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial minimal model over
Z

(X^{\prime}_{n},\Delta^{\prime}_{n}+B^{\prime}_{n}+\Phi^{\prime}_{n}+A^{\prime}_{n})\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}).

Let
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}_{n}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
be the composition.
Then

(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
-MMP over
Z
to a minimal model over
Z
.

We check that
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
By the argument in
[
BCHM10
, Proof of Lemma 3.10.11 (2)]
, we may write
A^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\Omega^{\prime\prime}+L^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
is effective,
L^{\prime\prime}
is ample over
Z
, and

{\rm Nlc}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})={\rm Nlc}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+\Omega^{\prime\prime}).

Moreover, since

K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\phi_{*}N^{\prime}+\phi_{*}(F^{\prime}+E^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))

and
{\rm Nlc}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})\cap{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}=\emptyset
(see Step
5
), the semi-ampleness of
N^{\prime}
over
Z
and Remark
3.6
imply that the
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle

(K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+\Omega^{\prime\prime}+L^{\prime\prime})|_{{\rm Nlc}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+\Omega^{\prime\prime})}

is semi-ample over
Z
.
Now we apply Theorem
2.21
to
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+\Omega^{\prime\prime})
and
L^{\prime\prime}
, and we see that

K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime}+\Omega^{\prime\prime}+L^{\prime\prime}

is semi-ample over
Z
.

By (
d
) in Step
3
, the following properties hold.

•

X^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,

•

\phi
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))
-MMP over
Z
that contracts prime divisors contained in
{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime})/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and

•

the divisor

\phi_{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A+(B^{\prime}-\Theta^{\prime}))\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\phi_{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime}+A^{\prime})

is semi-ample over
Z
.

As discussed in Step
4
, Proposition
3.3
implies the existence of a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.
We finish the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be an effective
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)\neq\emptyset
and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)=\emptyset
.
Then there exists a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
X^{\prime}
and a birational contraction
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
over
Z
to a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
X^{\prime\prime}
, which is projective over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

At least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
,

•

\phi
contracts all divisorial components of
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\phi
is an isomorphism on
X^{\prime}\setminus{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and

•

writing

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\Delta^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
having no common components, then
(X^{\prime\prime},\phi_{*}(\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A))
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.4
,
(X,\Delta+A)
has a good minimal model over
Z
.
Thus,
K_{X}+\Delta+A
birationally has the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition over
Z
whose positive part is semi-ample over
Z
.
Applying Proposition
4.2
to
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
(X,\Delta)
and
K_{X}+\Delta+A
, we get a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
X^{\prime}
such that putting
M^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime}
as the positive and the negative parts of the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
over
Z
, respectively, then the following properties hold.

•

f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}M^{\prime}+F^{\prime}
,

•

M^{\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}={\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
,

•

putting

\Gamma^{\prime}=f_{*}^{-1}\Delta+\sum_{\text{$E_{i}$:$f$-exceptional}}E_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime},

then
(X^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Gamma^{\prime\geq 1})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, and

•

for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
, at least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds.

We may write

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\Delta^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
that have no common components.
We put

B^{\prime}:=\sum_{F_{j}\subset{\rm Supp}\,F^{\prime}}(1-{\rm coeff}_{F_{j}}(\Delta^{\prime}))F_{j}.

By the same argument as in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, we get a sequence of a steps of a

(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+A^{\prime}+\alpha M^{\prime})
-MMP

\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}

over
Z
such that
\phi
is also a

(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
-MMP and
\phi_{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
is semi-ample over
Z
.
This
\phi
satisfies the following.

•

\overline{X}^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,

•

\phi_{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+A^{\prime})
is semi-ample over
Z
, and

•

\phi
contracts all divisorial components of
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\phi
is an isomorphism on
X^{\prime}\setminus{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Therefore,
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
are what we wanted (see Proposition
3.3
).
∎

4.2.
Termination of minimal model program

In this subsection, we study the termination of MMP with scaling of an ample divisor.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be an effective
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)\neq\emptyset
and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Let

(X,\Delta+A)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1}+A_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+A_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
such that if we put

\lambda_{i}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$(K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+A_{i})+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then

{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.
Then
\lambda_{m}=0
for some
m
and
(X_{m},\Delta_{m}+A_{m})
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.

Proof.

Replacing
A
if necessary, we may assume
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)={\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)
as closed subschemes of
X
.
Then
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)=\emptyset
.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1
.

In this step, we will construct some varieties and
\mathbb{R}
-divisors, and we will discuss properties of them.

By Corollary
4.5
, there exists a projective birational morphism
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
X^{\prime}
and a birational contraction
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
over
Z
to a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial variety
X^{\prime\prime}
, which is projective over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

At least one of
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq-1
or
E_{i}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
holds for any
f
-exceptional prime divisor
E_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
,

•

\phi
contracts all divisorial components of
{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\phi
is an isomorphism on
X^{\prime}\setminus{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, and

•

writing

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}

for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
\Delta^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
having no common components, then
(X^{\prime\prime},\phi_{*}(\Delta^{\prime}+f^{*}A))
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.

We put
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\phi_{*}\Delta
and
A^{\prime\prime}=\phi_{*}f^{*}A
.
By the equality
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}
and the first property, we have
{\rm Supp}\,E^{\prime}\subset{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
By this inclusion and the equality
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A)=\emptyset
, we have

\begin{split}&{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap f^{-1}({\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A))=\emptyset,\quad{\rm and}\\
&{\rm Supp}\,E^{\prime}\cap f^{-1}({\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta+A))=\emptyset.\end{split}

By the definition of good minimal models (Definition
3.1
) of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
and the negativity lemma, the inequality

a(P,X,\Delta+A)\leq a(P,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})

holds for any prime divisor
P
over
X
.
By these facts and the fact that
\phi\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
is an isomorphism on
X^{\prime}\setminus{\rm Bs}|f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we see that
\phi^{-1}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})
and the following properties hold.

(i)

{\rm Bs}|A^{\prime\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
, and

(ii)

for any prime divisor
P
over
X^{\prime\prime}
, if
a(P,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})\leq-1
then

a(P,X,\Delta+A)=a(P,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}).

We can write
A^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}H^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime}
for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
B^{\prime\prime}
on
X^{\prime\prime}
and an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
H^{\prime\prime}
on
X^{\prime\prime}
which is ample over
Z
.
We fix
u\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that

•

{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})={\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+uH^{\prime\prime})

•

{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})={\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})
, and

•

for any prime divisor
D
on
X
which is exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
, we have

a(D,X,\Delta+(1+u)A)<a(D,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime}).

Then
\phi^{-1}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})
.
By the equality
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E^{\prime}
and the definitions of
A^{\prime\prime}
and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, we see that

(iii)

for any prime divisor
Q
over
X^{\prime\prime}
, if
a(Q,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})\leq-1
or
Q
is not exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
then

a(D^{\prime\prime},X,\Delta+(1+u)A)=a(D^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime}).

Let
X^{\prime\prime}\to W
be the contraction over
Z
induced by
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}
.
Then

{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+(\Delta^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})+uH^{\prime\prime}/W|_{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\rm Bs}|u(H^{\prime\prime}+B^{\prime\prime})/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}={\rm Bs}|A^{\prime\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}.

By the relation
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})={\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+uH^{\prime\prime})

and (
i
), we see that

{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+(\Delta^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})+uH^{\prime\prime}/W|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},(\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime})+uH^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset.

By Theorem
4.4
, the pair
(X^{\prime\prime},(\Delta^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})+uH^{\prime\prime})
has a good minimal model over
W
.
We also have

\begin{split}&{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime}/W|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})\\
\subset&{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+(\Delta^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})+uH^{\prime\prime}/W|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+uH^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset.\end{split}

Since
\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}+uB^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})+uH^{\prime\prime}
, the pair
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})
has a good minimal model over
W
by Proposition
4.3
.

Step 2
.

In this step we define a diagram and an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor used in the proof.

Let
(X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime})
be a good minimal model of
(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})
over
W
.
We denote
X^{\prime\prime}\to W
and
X^{\prime\prime\prime}\to W
by
\tau^{\prime\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}
, respectively.
Let
g\colon Y\to X
,
g_{2}\colon Y\to X^{\prime\prime}
, and
g_{3}\colon Y\to X^{\prime\prime\prime}
be a common resolution of
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Now we have the following diagram

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{g_{2}}
\scriptstyle{g_{3}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{\phi\circ f^{-1}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{W.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z}

We define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}
on
X^{\prime\prime\prime}
by

K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}=g_{3*}g_{2}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}).

Note that
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}
may not be effective.
Since
\tau^{\prime\prime}
is the contraction induced by
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}
, we see that
K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and
K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,W}0
.
In particular, the negativity lemma implies

g_{2}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})=g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}).

Moreover, there exists
\epsilon\in(0,1)
such that

K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\epsilon\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\epsilon)\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\epsilon(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime})+(1-\epsilon)(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime})

is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then, for any
\delta\in[0,\epsilon]
, the divisor

K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\delta\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\delta)\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}

is semi-ample over
Z
.
For any
\delta\in[0,\epsilon]
, we define
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
by

(
\clubsuit
)

\begin{split}E_{Y}^{(\delta)}:=&g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+(1+\delta u)A)-g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\delta\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\delta)\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime})\\
=&(1-\delta)\bigl(g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+A)-g_{2}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})\bigr)\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\;+\delta\bigl(g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+(1+u)A)-g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime})\bigr).\end{split}

Step 3
.

In this step, we will prove that
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
in (

♣

‣
2
) is effective and
g_{3}
-exceptional for any
\delta\in[0,\epsilon]
.

By construction of
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
, it follows that

\begin{split}E_{Y}^{(\delta)}=&g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+(1+\delta u)A)-g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\delta\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\delta)\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime})\\
=&(1-\delta)\sum_{P}\bigl(a(P,X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime})-a(P,X,\Delta+A)\bigr)P\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\;+\delta\sum_{P}\bigl(a(P,X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u})-a(P,X,\Delta+(1+u)A)\bigr)P,\end{split}

where
P
runs over prime divisors on
Y
.

We first prove
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}\geq 0
.
For any prime divisor
\bar{D}
on
X
, the definition of good minimal model (Definition
3.1
) and the negativity lemma shows

a(\bar{D},X,\Delta+A)\leq a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}),

and

a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})\leq a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}).

If
\bar{D}
is not exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
, then

a(\bar{D},X,\Delta+(1+u)A)=a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})

by (
iii
).
If
\bar{D}
is exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
, then

a(\bar{D},X,\Delta+(1+u)A)<a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})

by the choice of
u\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
In both cases, we have

a(\bar{D},X,\Delta+(1+u)A)\leq a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})\leq a(\bar{D},X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}).

Therefore, for any component
\bar{E}
on
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
that is not exceptional over
X
, we have

{\rm coeff}_{\bar{E}}(E_{Y}^{(\delta)})\geq 0.

By applying the negativity lemma to
g\colon Y\to X
and
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
, we obtain
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}\geq 0
.

Next, we prove that
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
is
g_{3}
-exceptional.
We pick a prime divisor
\tilde{D}
on
Y
that is not exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Then the definition of good minimal model (Definition
3.1
) shows

a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u})=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime}).

If
\tilde{D}
is not exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
, then

a(\tilde{D},X,\Delta+A)=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}),

by the definition of good minimal model (Definition
3.1
), and (
iii
) shows

a(\tilde{D},X,\Delta+(1+u)A)=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u}).

Therefore we have
{\rm coeff}_{\tilde{D}}(E_{Y}^{(\delta)})=0
if
\tilde{D}
is not exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
.
If
\tilde{D}
is exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
, then

a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u})=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})\leq-1

by the definition of good minimal model (Definition
3.1
).
By (
ii
), we have

a(\tilde{D},X,\Delta+A)=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+A^{\prime\prime}),

and furthermore (
iii
) implies

a(\tilde{D},X,\Delta+(1+u)A)=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+(1+u)A^{\prime\prime})=a(\tilde{D},X^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u}).

Therefore
{\rm coeff}_{\tilde{D}}(E_{Y}^{(\delta)})=0
even if
\tilde{D}
is exceptional over
X^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
is
g_{3}
-exceptional.
From these arguments,
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}
is effective and
g_{3}
-exceptional for any
\delta\in[0,\epsilon]
.

Step 4
.

With this step we complete the proof.

By (

♣

‣
2
), for any
\delta\in[0,\epsilon]
we obtain

g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+(1+\delta u)A)=g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\delta\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\delta)\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime})+E_{Y}^{(\delta)}

such that
K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\delta\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\delta)\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
and
E_{Y}^{(\delta)}\geq 0
is
g_{3}
-exceptional.

We use the
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP over
Z
and
\lambda_{i}
in Theorem
4.6
.
Fix
m
such that
\lambda_{m}<\lambda_{m-1}\leq\epsilon u
.
Such
m
exists since
u>0
,
\epsilon>0
, and
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.
Let
h\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
and
h_{m}\colon Y^{\prime}\to X_{m}
be a common resolution of
Y\dashrightarrow X_{m}
.
We have the following diagram

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{g_{3}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{h_{m}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{m}}

over
Z
.
By construction, for any
t\in(\lambda_{m},\lambda_{m-1})
, we can write

h^{*}g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+(1+t)A)=h_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+(1+t)A_{m})+F_{Y^{\prime}}^{(t)}

for some effective
h_{m}
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
F_{Y^{\prime}}^{(t)}
on
Y^{\prime}
.
Now
t=\frac{t}{u}\cdot u
and
\frac{t}{u}\leq\epsilon
.
Hence

h_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+(1+t)A_{m})+F_{Y^{\prime}}^{(t)}=h^{*}g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\frac{t}{u}\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\frac{t}{u})\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime})+h^{*}E_{Y}^{{}^{(\frac{t}{u})}}.

Since
h_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+(1+t)A_{m})
and
K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\frac{t}{u}\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\frac{t}{u})\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}
are nef over
Z
and
F_{Y^{\prime}}^{(t)}
(resp.
E_{Y}^{{}^{(\frac{t}{u})}}
) is effective and
h_{m}
-exceptional (resp.
g_{3}
-exceptional), by the negativity lemma, we see that
F_{Y^{\prime}}^{(t)}=h^{*}E_{Y}^{{}^{(\frac{t}{u})}}
.
Therefore, we get the equality

h_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+(1+t)A_{m})=h^{*}g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\frac{t}{u}\Gamma_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}+(1-\frac{t}{u})\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime})

for all
t\in(\lambda_{m},\lambda_{m-1})
.
Then

h_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+A_{m})=h^{*}g_{3}^{*}(K_{X^{\prime\prime\prime}}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}),

and the right hand side is semi-ample over
Z
by construction of
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
In particular, we have
\lambda_{m}=0
, and
(X_{m},\Delta_{m}+A_{m})
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.

We finish the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be an effective
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)\neq\emptyset
and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Let

(X,\Delta+A)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1}+A_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+A_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
constructed by using Corollary
3.12
.
We put

\lambda_{i}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$(K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+A_{i})+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
.
Then
\lambda_{m}=0
for some
m
and
(X_{m},\Delta_{m}+A_{m})
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.

Proof.

We have

\lambda_{i}>\lambda_{i+1}
for all
i\geq 1
.
We define
\lambda:={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
.
By replacing
A
with
\lambda A
, we may assume
\lambda=0
.
Then the corollary follows from Theorem
4.6
.
∎

4.3.
Non-vanishing

In this subsection we prove the non-vanishing theorem.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
\Delta
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then the equality
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
holds.

Proof.

We may assume that
(X,\Delta)
is not klt.
By replacing
A
with a general member of
|A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we may assume
A\geq 0
.
Let

(X,\Delta+A)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1}+A_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+A_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
constructed by using Corollary
3.12
.
Then the non-isomorphic locus of the
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
(Remark
3.6
).
We define

\lambda_{i}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$(K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+A_{i})+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
and
\lambda:={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
.
By construction, the MMP terminates after finitely many steps or otherwise
\lambda\neq\lambda_{i}
for every
i\geq 1
, and
K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+A+tA_{i}
is semi-ample over
Z
for every
i\geq 1
and
t\in(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i-1}]
.
Assuming
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+\lambda A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, then Theorem
4.6
implies
\lambda_{j}=\lambda
for some
j
.
Then the equality
\lambda=0
holds because otherwise we get a contradiction, and
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
From this discussion, it is sufficient to prove
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+\lambda A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

We fix a nonnegative rational numbers
q
and
q^{\prime}
such that

q^{\prime}\leq\lambda\leq q.

We note that we may take
q=q^{\prime}=\lambda
when
\lambda
is a rational number.
By the hypothesis of Proposition
4.8
that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is semi-ample over
Z
, we can find an integer
p>1
such that

•

pq\in\mathbb{Z}
and
p(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
,
pA
, and
pqA
are all Cartier, and

•

p(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
,
pA|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
, and
pqA|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}

are globally generated over
Z
.

In this paragraph, we will prove
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+qA/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
We have
\frac{1+pq}{p-1}>\lambda
.
Since
\lambda={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
and
\frac{1+pq}{p-1}>q
, we can find
m
such that the birational contraction

X\dashrightarrow X_{m}

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A+qA)
-MMP over
Z
and the divisor

K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+A_{m}+\frac{1+pq}{p-1}A_{m}

is semi-ample over
Z
.
By Remark
3.6
, the birational map
X\dashrightarrow X_{m}
is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
.
We put

L:=p(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+A_{m}+qA_{m}).

Then
L
is a Weil divisor on
X_{m}
and Cartier on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})
.
We can write

L=K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+(p-1)\left(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+A_{m}+\frac{1+pq}{p-1}A_{m}\right)

and
(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+A_{m}+\frac{1+pq}{p-1}A_{m})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})}
is ample over
Z
because
X\dashrightarrow X_{m}
is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, the inequality
\frac{1+pq}{p-1}>0
holds, and
p(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is globally generated over
Z
.
Therefore
L-(K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m})
is in particular nef and log big over
Z
with respect to
(X_{m},\Delta_{m})
.
From these facts, we can use Theorem
2.27
to
(X_{m},\Delta_{m})
and
L
.
Putting
\pi_{m}\colon X_{m}\to Z
and
\mathcal{I}
as the structure morphism and the defining deal sheaf of
{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})
respectively, then Theorem
2.27
implies that

R^{1}\pi_{m*}(\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}(L))=0.

We consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}(L)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}(L)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})}(L|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})})\longrightarrow 0.

By taking the pushforward to
Z
, we get the following exact sequence

\begin{split}\pi_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}(L)\longrightarrow\pi_{m*}\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})}(L|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})})\longrightarrow R^{1}\pi_{m*}(\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{m}}(L))=0.\end{split}

By construction of
p
and
L
, the line bundle
L|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})}
is globally generated over
Z
.
Hence, we have

{\rm Bs}|K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+A+qA_{m}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})={\rm Bs}|L/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X_{m},\Delta_{m})=\emptyset.

Since
X\dashrightarrow X_{m}
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A+qA)
-MMP over
Z
and the map is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, we have
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+qA/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

In this paragraph, we will find a rational number
\tilde{q}\in[q^{\prime},\lambda]
such that

{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+\tilde{q}A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset.

We fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that

\frac{\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor}{k}\geq q^{\prime}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad k\lambda-\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor<\frac{1}{p}.

Such
k
always exists by the argument using the Diophantine approximation (see, for example,
[
BCHM10
, Subsection 3.7]
).
We put

\tilde{q}:=\frac{\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor}{k}.

Then
\tilde{q}\in[q^{\prime},\lambda]
.
We will prove that this
\tilde{q}
is the desired rational number.
By definition, we have

\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp-1}>\frac{1+\bigl(k\lambda-\frac{1}{p}\bigr)p}{kp-1}=\frac{kp\lambda}{kp-1}>\lambda.

Since
\lambda={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
and
\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp-1}>\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp}>\tilde{q}
, we can find
n
such that the birational contraction

X\dashrightarrow X_{n}

is a finite sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A+\tilde{q}A)
-MMP over
Z
and the divisor

K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+A_{n}+\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp-1}A_{n}

is semi-ample over
Z
.
By Remark
3.6
, the birational map
X\dashrightarrow X_{n}
is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
.
We put

L^{\prime}:=kp(K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+A_{n}+\tilde{q}A_{n})=kp(K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+A_{n})+p\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor A_{n}.

By the definitions of
p
,
k
, and
\tilde{q}
, it follows that
L^{\prime}
is a Weil divisor on
X_{n}
and Cartier on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})
.
We can write

L^{\prime}=K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+(kp-1)\left(K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+A_{n}+\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp-1}A_{n}\right)

and
(K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+A_{n}+\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp-1}A_{n})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})}
is ample over
Z
because
X\dashrightarrow X_{n}
is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
,
\frac{1+\lfloor k\lambda\rfloor p}{kp-1}>0
, and
p(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is globally generated over
Z
.
Therefore
L^{\prime}-(K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n})
is in particular nef and log big over
Z
with respect to
(X_{n},\Delta_{n})
.
From these facts, we can use Theorem
2.27
to
(X_{n},\Delta_{n})
and
L^{\prime}
.
Putting
\pi_{n}\colon X_{n}\to Z
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
as the structure morphism and the defining deal sheaf of
{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})
respectively, then Theorem
2.27
shows

R^{1}\pi_{n*}(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(L^{\prime}))=0.

We consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(L^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(L^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})}(L^{\prime}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})})\longrightarrow 0.

By taking the pushforward to
Z
, we get the following exact sequence

\begin{split}\pi_{n*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(L^{\prime})\longrightarrow\pi_{n*}\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})}(L^{\prime}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})})\longrightarrow R^{1}\pi_{n*}(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{n}}(L^{\prime}))=0.\end{split}

By construction of
p
and
L^{\prime}
, the line bundle
L^{\prime}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})}
is globally generated over
Z
.
Hence, we have

{\rm Bs}|K_{X_{n}}+\Delta_{n}+(1+\tilde{q})A_{n}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})={\rm Bs}|L^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X_{n},\Delta_{n})=\emptyset.

Since
X\dashrightarrow X_{n}
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A+\tilde{q}A)
-MMP over
Z
and the map is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, we have
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+\tilde{q}A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

Now we have nonnegative rational numbers
\tilde{q}\leq\lambda\leq q
such that

\begin{split}{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+qA/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=&\emptyset,\qquad{\rm and}\\
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+\tilde{q}A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=&\emptyset.\end{split}

Since
K_{X}+\Delta+A+\lambda A
is a convex linear combination of
K_{X}+\Delta+A+qA
and
K_{X}+\Delta+A+\tilde{q}A
, we see that

{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A+\lambda A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset.

Then Proposition
4.8
holds as discussed.
∎

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
\Delta
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
.
Suppose that there exist positive real numbers
r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l}
and
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
A_{1},\,\cdots,\,A_{l}
on
X
such that

•

\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}=1
and
A=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}A_{i}
, and

•

for every
1\leq i\leq l
, the
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
is semi-ample over
Z
.

Then there exist positive real numbers
r^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{m}
, positive integers
p_{1},\,\cdots,\,p_{m}
that are greater than one, and
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
A^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,A^{\prime}_{m}
on
X
such that

•

\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}=1
and
A=\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}
,

•

p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta+A^{\prime}_{j})
and
p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}
are Cartier for every
1\leq j\leq m
,

•

p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta+A^{\prime}_{j})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
and
p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are globally generated over
Z
for every
1\leq j\leq m
, and

•

\frac{p_{j}}{p_{j}-1}A^{\prime}_{j}-A
is ample over
Z
for every
1\leq j\leq m
.

Proof.

This is an application of
[
BCHM10
, Lemma 3.7.7]
.
By the hypothesis, we can find an integer
p\geq 2
such that

•

p(K_{X}+\Delta)
and all
pA_{i}
are Cartier, and

•

all
p(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
and
pA_{i}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are globally generated over
Z
.

Put
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l})
.
We apply
[
BCHM10
, Lemma 3.7.7]
to
p
,
\frac{1}{2}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}
, and the following set

\mathcal{S}:=\left\{(a_{1},\,\cdots,\,a_{l})\in\mathbb{R}^{l}\,\middle|\,\text{$a_{i}\geq 0$ for all $i$ and $\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}=1$}\right\}\ni\boldsymbol{r}.

There exist rational points
\boldsymbol{q}^{(1)},\,\cdots,\,\boldsymbol{q}^{(m)}
of
\mathcal{S}
and positive integers
p_{1},\,\cdots,\,p_{m}
, which are divisible by
p
, such that
\boldsymbol{r}
is a convex linear combination of
\boldsymbol{q}^{(1)},\,\cdots,\,\boldsymbol{q}^{(m)}
and for every
1\leq j\leq m
the following conditions

||\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}||<\frac{1}{p_{j}}\cdot\frac{1}{2}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad\frac{p_{j}}{p}\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}

hold.
Then
p_{j}\geq 2
for all
1\leq j\leq m
since
p\geq 2
, and the first property implies that
\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}
is not the origin for all
1\leq j\leq m
.
For each
1\leq j\leq m
, we put

(q^{(j)}_{1},\,\cdots,\,q^{(j)}_{l}):=\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad A^{\prime}_{j}:=\sum_{i=1}^{l}q^{(j)}_{i}A_{i}.

Since
\boldsymbol{r}
is a convex linear combination of
\boldsymbol{q}^{(1)},\,\cdots,\,\boldsymbol{q}^{(m)}
, we can write
\boldsymbol{r}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}
with nonnegative real numbers
r^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{m}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}=1
.
By removing indices
j
such that
r^{\prime}_{j}=0
, we may assume that all
r^{\prime}_{j}
are positive.

From now on we prove that these
r^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,r^{\prime}_{m}
,
p_{1},\,\cdots,\,p_{m}
, and
A^{\prime}_{1},\,\cdots,\,A^{\prime}_{m}
satisfy the properties of Proposition
4.9
.
Note that all
A^{\prime}_{j}
are
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors by construction.
It is obvious by definition that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}=1
, and
\boldsymbol{r}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}
shows
r_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.
Hence, we have

A=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}A_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}A_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}r^{\prime}_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}.

Therefore the first property of Proposition
4.9
holds.

The condition
\frac{p_{j}}{p}\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
implies
p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}\in p\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
for all
1\leq i\leq l
.
Since all
pA_{i}
are Cartier and all
pA_{i}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are globally generated over
Z
, the relation

p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}A_{i}

implies that all
p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}
is Cartier and all
p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are globally generated over
Z
.
Since
p_{j}\in p\mathbb{Z}
and
p(K_{X}+\Delta)
is Cartier, we see that
p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta)
is Cartier for all
1\leq j\leq m
.
In particular,
p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta+A^{\prime}_{j})
and
p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}
are Cartier for every
1\leq j\leq m
.
This is the second property of Proposition
4.9
.

In the previous paragraph, we have already proved that
p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is globally generated over
Z
for every
1\leq j\leq m
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{S}
, we see that
\sum_{i=1}^{l}q^{(j)}_{i}=1
holds for all
1\leq j\leq m
.
This implies that
\sum_{i=1}^{l}p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}=p_{j}
for every
1\leq j\leq m
.
Then

\begin{split}p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta+A^{\prime}_{j})=p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta)+p_{j}A^{\prime}_{j}=&\sum_{i=1}^{l}p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}(K_{X}+\Delta)+\sum_{i=1}^{l}p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}A_{i}\\
=&\sum_{i=1}^{l}p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i}).\end{split}

As explained above,
p_{j}q^{(j)}_{i}\in p\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
for every
1\leq i\leq l
and
1\leq j\leq m
.
Since all the
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundles
p(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are globally generated over
Z
, it follows that
p_{j}(K_{X}+\Delta+A^{\prime}_{j})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is globally generated over
Z
for every
1\leq j\leq m
.
Therefore, the third property of Proposition
4.9
holds.

Finally, since
||\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{q}^{(j)}||<\frac{1}{p_{j}}\cdot\frac{1}{2}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}
, we have
|r_{i}-q^{(j)}_{i}|<\frac{1}{2p_{j}}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}
for all
1\leq i\leq l
and
1\leq j\leq m
.
Then

q^{(j)}_{i}\geq r_{i}>\frac{1}{2}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}\qquad{\rm or}\qquad q^{(j)}_{i}>r_{i}-\frac{1}{2p_{j}}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}.

This shows

|r_{i}-q^{(j)}_{i}|<\frac{1}{2p_{j}}{\rm min}\{r_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq l}<\frac{q^{(j)}_{i}}{p_{j}}<\frac{q^{(j)}_{i}}{p_{j}-1}.

By simple calculations, for every
1\leq i\leq l
and
1\leq j\leq m
we have

r_{i}<q^{(j)}_{i}+\frac{q^{(j)}_{i}}{p_{j}-1}=\frac{p_{j}}{p_{j}-1}q^{(j)}_{i}.

Since
A^{\prime}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}q^{(j)}_{i}A_{i}
and
A=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}A_{i}
by definition,
\frac{p_{j}}{p_{j}-1}A^{\prime}_{j}-A
is ample over
Z
for every
1\leq j\leq m
.
Therefore, the final property of Proposition
4.9
holds.
∎

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

We prove the theorem in several steps.

Step 1
.

In this step, we reduce Theorem
4.10
to the case where
\Delta
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor.

By the argument from convex geometry, the set

\{\Delta^{\prime}\in{\rm WDiv}_{\mathbb{R}}(X)\,|\,\text{$K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime}$ is $\mathbb{R}$-Cartier and ${\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)={\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta^{\prime})$}\}

contains a rational polytope
\mathcal{D}\ni\Delta
in the
\mathbb{R}
-vector space spanned by the components of
\Delta
.
We take an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\Delta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
such that
(K_{X}+\Delta)-(K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime})+A
is
\pi
-ample.
Putting
A^{\prime}=(K_{X}+\Delta)-(K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime})+A
, then
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
is a pair,
A^{\prime}
is
\pi
-ample,
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta^{\prime})={\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, and
K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime}+A^{\prime}=K_{X}+\Delta+A
.
From them, it is easy to check that we may replace
(X,\Delta)
and
A
by
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}
respectively.
Replacing
(X,\Delta)
and
A
by
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}
respectively, we may assume that
\Delta
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor.

Step 2
.

In this step, we discuss a decomposition of
A
into
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors.

We may write

A=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}A_{i},

where
r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l}
are positive real numbers and
A_{i}
are
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors.
We consider the set

\left\{(a_{1},\,\cdots,\,a_{l})\in(\mathbb{R}_{>0})^{l}\,\middle|\begin{array}[]{l}\text{$(K_{X}+\Delta+\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}$ is semi-ample over $Z$}\end{array}\right\}.

By the argument from convex geometry, the set contains a rational polytope
\mathcal{C}
such that
(r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l})\in\mathcal{C}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\boldsymbol{q}_{k}
be the vertices of
\mathcal{C}
.
For each
1\leq j\leq k
, we can write
\boldsymbol{q}_{j}=(q^{(j)}_{1},\,\cdots,\,q^{(j)}_{l})
for some positive rational numbers
q^{(j)}_{1},\,\cdots,\,q^{(j)}_{l}
.
For every
1\leq j\leq k
, let
\tilde{A}_{j}
be the ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor corresponding to
\boldsymbol{q}_{j}
, in other words,

\tilde{A}_{j}:=\sum_{i=1}^{l}q^{(j)}_{i}A_{i}.

Then all
\tilde{A}_{j}
are
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier,
(K_{X}+\Delta+\tilde{A}_{j})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are finite
\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
-linear combinations of invertible sheaves on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
that are globally generated over
Z
, and we can write
A=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{r}_{j}\tilde{A}_{j}
for some positive real numbers
\tilde{r}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\tilde{r}_{k}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{r}_{j}=1
.

By replacing the sets
\{A_{1},\,\cdots,\,A_{l}\}
and
\{r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l}\}
by the sets
\{\tilde{A}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\tilde{A}_{k}\}
and
\{\tilde{r}_{1},\,\cdots,\,\tilde{r}_{k}\}
respectively, we obtain a decomposition of
A
into
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors

A=\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}A_{i}

with
r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{l}r_{i}=1
and
(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is semi-ample over
Z
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.
By Proposition
4.9
and replacing
r_{1},\,\cdots,\,r_{l}
and
A_{1},\,\cdots,\,A_{l}
again, we may assume the existence of positive integers
p_{1},\,\cdots,\,p_{l}
, which are greater than one, such that

(I)

p_{i}(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})
and
p_{i}A_{i}
are Cartier,

(II)

p_{i}(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
and
p_{i}A_{i}|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
are globally generated over
Z
for every
1\leq i\leq l
, and

(III)

\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i}-A
is ample over
Z
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.

Step 3
.

In this step, we prove
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
for every
1\leq i\leq l
.
Throughout this step, we fix
i
.

By (
III
) and the condition
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, which is the hypothesis of Theorem
4.10
, we have
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i}/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
By (
II
), the
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
(K_{X}+\Delta+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is ample over
Z
.
Hence, we may apply Proposition
4.8
to
\pi\colon X\to Z
,
(X,\Delta)
, and
\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i}
.
We see that

{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset.

We take a general member
H_{i}\in|A_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
.
By Corollary
4.7
, we get a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}H_{i})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}H_{i}
that terminates with a good minimal model over
Z

(X,\Delta+\tfrac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}H_{i})\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\tfrac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}H^{\prime}).

Note that the good minimal model depends on
i
, although we remove the index
i
because
i
was fixed.
Let
A^{\prime}
be the birational transform of
A_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
.
By construction, the MMP is a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A_{i}
, and
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A^{\prime}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
In particular,
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}
are both
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.
By the property
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i}/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
and Remark
3.6
, the birational map
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
.

We put

L:=p_{i}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+A^{\prime}).

By (
I
),
L
is a Weil divisor on
X^{\prime}
and
L
is Cartier on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
.
We can write

L=K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+(p_{i}-1)\left(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A^{\prime}\right).

Then
(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A^{\prime})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is ample over
Z
.
This is because
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
and
(K_{X}+\Delta+\frac{p_{i}}{p_{i}-1}A_{i})|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
is ample over
Z
by (
II
).
Therefore
L-(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime})
is in particular nef and log big over
Z
with respect to
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
.
From these facts, we can apply Theorem
2.27
to
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
and
L
.
Putting
\pi^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Z
and
\mathcal{I}
as the structure morphism and the defining ideal sheaf of
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
respectively, then Theorem
2.27
implies

R^{1}\pi^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L))=0.

We consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})}(L|_{{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})})\longrightarrow 0.

By taking the pushforward to
Z
, we get the following exact sequence

\begin{split}\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L)\longrightarrow\pi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})}(L|_{{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})})\longrightarrow R^{1}\pi^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{I}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}(L))=0.\end{split}

By (
II
) and construction of
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
, the restriction
L|_{{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})}
is a line bundle on
{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
and this is globally generated over
Z
.
Hence, we have

{\rm Bs}|K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+A^{\prime}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})={\rm Bs}|L/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})=\emptyset.

Since
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i})
-MMP over
Z
and the map is an isomorphism on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, we have
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

By construction in Step
2
, the divisor
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is a convex linear combination of
K_{X}+\Delta+A_{1},\,\cdots,\,K_{X}+\Delta+A_{l}
.
Since
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A_{i}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
for all
1\leq i\leq l
, we have

{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset.

We finish the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.11
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Let

(X,\Delta+A)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1}+A_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+A_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
. We put

\lambda_{i}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$(K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+A_{i})+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
.
Then

{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{m}
for some
m
.
In particular, if
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
then the
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP terminates with a good minimal model over
Z
.

Proof.

If
(X,\Delta)
is klt, then the theorem follows from
[
BCHM10
]
.
If
(X,\Delta)
is not klt, then we put
\lambda:={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
.
Replacing
A
by
\lambda A
, we may assume
\lambda=0
.
Then the theorem follows from Theorem
4.10
and Theorem
4.6
.
∎

5.
Minimal model program along log canonical locus

In this section we study the minimal model theory for normal pairs such that the non-nef locus of the log canonical divisor is disjoint from the non-lc locus of the normal pair.

5.1.
Proof of main result

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
5.3
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair, and let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
\omega+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(\omega+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(\omega+A)|_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Let

X=:X_{1}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of an
(\omega+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
such that if we put

\lambda_{i}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{i}+A_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then

{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.
Then
\lambda_{m}=0
for some
m
.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
2.26
to
(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
and
\frac{1}{2}A
.
We get a normal pair
(X,\Gamma)
such that

K_{X}+\Gamma\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\omega+\frac{1}{2}A
and
{\rm Nklt}(X,\Gamma)
is a closed subscheme of
{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)
.
We consider
(X,\Gamma)
and
\frac{1}{2}A
.
Since

K_{X}+\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}A\sim_{\mathbb{R},\,Z}\omega+A,

we have
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}A/Z)\cap{\rm Nklt}(X,\Gamma)=\emptyset
.
Since
(\omega+A)|_{{\rm Nqklt}(X,\omega)}
is semi-ample over
Z
, it follows that
(K_{X}+\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}A)|_{{\rm Nklt}(X,\Gamma)}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
By Remark
3.6
, we may regard

X\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots
as a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
\frac{1}{2}A
.
We may apply Theorem
4.11
to
X\to Z
,
(X,\Gamma)
, and
\frac{1}{2}A
, and we see that
\lambda_{m}=0
for some
m
.
∎

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
f\colon(Y,\Delta)\to[X,\omega]
be a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair, and let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
\omega+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(\omega+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(\omega+A)|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then there exists a sequence of steps of an
(\omega+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A

X=:X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{n}

such that
\omega_{n}+A_{n}
is semi-ample over
Z
, where
\omega_{n}
and
A_{n}
are the birational transforms of
\omega
and
A
on
X_{n}
respectively.

Proof.

By taking a dlt blow-up of
(\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{\Delta})\to(Y,\Delta)
and replacing
(Y,\Delta)
with
(\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{\Delta})
, we may assume that
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
is dlt.
By replacing
A
with a general member of
|A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we may assume that
A
is an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor and
{\rm Nklt}(Y,\Delta+f^{*}A)={\rm Nklt}(Y,\Delta)
and
{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta+f^{*}A)={\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)
hold as relations of closed subschemes of
Y
.
We may further assume that
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}+f^{*}A)
is a dlt pair whose lc centers are those of
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 1
.

In this step, we construct a diagram used in the proof.

By Theorem
3.10
, there exists a diagram

\textstyle{\hskip-14.22636pt(Y,\Delta+f^{*}A)=:(Y_{1},\Delta_{1}+H_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f=:f_{1}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i}}
\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{[X,\omega+A]=:[X_{1},\omega_{1}+A_{1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[X_{i},\omega_{i}+A_{i}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots}

over
Z
, where
A_{i}
(resp.
H_{i}
) is the birational transform of
A
(resp.
f^{*}A
) on
X_{i}
(resp.
Y_{i}
), such that

•

f_{i}\colon(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\to[X_{i},\omega_{i}+A_{i}]
is a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair such that
Y_{i}
and
X_{i}
are projective over
Z
and every
Y_{i}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,

•

the sequence of maps

(Y_{1},\Delta_{1}+H_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{1}}+\Delta_{1}+H_{1})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
H_{1}
,

•

the sequence of maps

X_{1}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of an
(\omega_{1}+A_{1})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A_{1}
, and

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{i}+A_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then the following properties hold.

–

\lambda_{i}>\lambda_{i+1}
for all
i\geq 1
,

–

\omega_{i}+\lambda_{i-1}A_{i}
is semi-ample over
Z
for all
i\geq 1
, and

–

\omega_{i}+A_{i}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{i}
is ample over
Z
for all
i\geq 1
and
\lambda^{\prime}\in(\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i-1})
.

Note that the birational map
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{i+1}
is not necessarily a step of an MMP.
In fact,
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{i+1}
can be a finite sequence of steps of an MMP.

Put
\lambda={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
.
Replacing
A
by a general member of
|\lambda A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
, we may assume
\lambda=0
.
By construction,
\omega_{i}
and
A_{i}
are
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier for any
i\geq 1
.
Moreover,
X_{i}\dashrightarrow X_{i+1}
and
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{i+1}
are birational contractions.
By an induction on
i
, we see that

H_{i}=f_{i}^{*}A_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
.
Since
{\rm NNef}(\omega+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
, we have

{\rm NNef}(K_{Y_{1}}+\Delta_{1}+H_{1}/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(Y_{1},\Delta_{1})=\emptyset.

By Remark
3.6
, the birational map

Y_{1}\dashrightarrow Y_{i}

is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nlc}(Y,\Delta)
for every
i\geq 1
.
By construction,
{\rm Nlc}(Y_{1},\Delta_{1}+H_{1})={\rm Nlc}(Y_{1},\Delta_{1})
as closed subschemes, and therefore
{\rm Nlc}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})={\rm Nlc}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})
as closed subschemes for every
i\geq 1
.
Moreover,

Y_{1}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow Y_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{1}}+\Delta^{<1}_{1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{1}+H_{1})
-MMP over
Z
.
We also see that
(Y_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i}+H_{i})
is a dlt pair and the lc centers of the pair are those of
(Y_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i})
.
This is because
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}+f^{*}A)
is a dlt pair whose lc centers are those of
(Y,\Delta^{<1}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1})
.
Thus
{\rm Nklt}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})={\rm Nklt}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})
as closed subschemes of
Y_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
.

By these discussions, we may assume that
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
and the quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\to[X_{i},\omega_{i}+A_{i}]
satisfies the following properties for every
i\geq 1
.

•

H_{i}=f_{i}^{*}A_{i}
, and therefore
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})\to[X_{i},\omega_{i}]
is a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair,

•

(Y_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i}+H_{i})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair whose lc centers are those of
(Y_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i})
, and

•

{\rm Nlc}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})={\rm Nlc}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})
and
{\rm Nklt}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})={\rm Nklt}(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})
as closed subschemes of
Y_{i}
.

Step 2
.

We will apply the argument of the special termination
[
F07b
]
to our situation.
We will prove that for any integer
0\leq d<{\rm dim}\,Y
, there exists
m_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that the non-isomorphic locus of the
(K_{Y_{m_{d}}}+\Delta_{m_{d}}+H_{m_{d}})
-MMP over
Z

(Y_{m_{d}},\Delta_{m_{d}}+H_{m_{d}})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is disjoint from any lc center of
(Y_{m_{d}},\Delta_{m_{d}})
whose dimension is less than or equal to
d
.
We prove the statement by an induction on
d
.

We can find
i_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that for any
i\geq i_{0}
, the birational map
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{i+1}
is an isomorphism on the generic point of any lc center of
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})
.
Therefore, we may set
m_{0}:=i_{0}
.
The case
d=0
of the above statement holds true, and we may assume that
Y_{m_{0}}\dashrightarrow Y_{i}
is an isomorphism on the generic point of any lc center of
(Y_{m_{0}},\Delta_{m_{0}})
for any
i\geq m_{0}
.

Step 3
.

We assume the existence of
m_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
of the statement in Step
2
.
We may assume
m_{d}\geq m_{0}
.
We fix an arbitrary
(d+1)
-dimensional lc center
S_{m_{d}}
of
(Y_{m_{d}},\Delta_{m_{d}})
.
By the choice of
m_{0}
, for any
i\geq m_{d}
there is an lc center
S_{i}
of
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i})
such that the birational map
Y_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow Y_{i}
induces a birational map
S_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow S_{i}
over
Z
.
In this step, we construct a normal pair
(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}})
and an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
H_{S_{i}}
on
S_{i}
for each
i\geq m_{d}
.

Because
(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair, as in Theorem
2.19
we can define an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
B_{S_{i}}
and an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
G_{S_{i}}
on
S_{i}
by

\begin{split}K_{S_{i}}+B_{S_{i}}=(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i})|_{S_{i}},\quad{\rm and}\quad G_{S_{i}}:=(\Delta^{>1}_{i}-{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1}_{i})|_{S_{i}}.\end{split}

Then
K_{S_{i}}+B_{S_{i}}+G_{S_{i}}=(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i})|_{S_{i}}
.
We define an
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier divisor
H_{S_{i}}
on
S_{i}
by

H_{S_{i}}:=H_{i}|_{S_{i}}.

Since
(Y_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i}+H_{i})
is a dlt pair and the lc centers of the pair are those of
(Y_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i})
, we see that
(S_{i},B_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}})
is a dlt pair whose lc centers are those of
(S_{i},B_{S_{i}})
.
Since the inclusion
{\rm Supp}\,G_{S_{i}}\subset{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{>1}_{i}|_{S_{i}}
holds and the non-isomorphic locus of the
(K_{Y_{1}}+\Delta_{1}+H_{1})
-MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(Y_{1},\Delta_{1})
, the birational map
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is an isomorphism on an open subset containing
{\rm Supp}\,G_{S_{i}}
.
Therefore, the birational transform of
G_{S_{i}}
on
S_{i+1}
is equal to
G_{S_{i+1}}
.
By using the argument of the special termination
[
F07b
]
and replacing
m_{d}
, we may assume that for any
i\geq m_{d}
, the birational map
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is small and the birational transforms of
B_{S_{i}}
and
H_{S_{i}}
on
S_{i+1}
are equal to
B_{S_{i+1}}
and
H_{S_{i+1}}
, respectively.
Since any lc center of
(S_{i},B_{S_{i}}+G_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}})
is an lc center of
(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})
whose dimension is less than or equal to
d
, the birational map
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is an isomorphism on an open subset containing all the lc centers of
(S_{i},B_{S_{i}}+G_{S_{i}})
.
By recalling that
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is an isomorphism around
{\rm Supp}\,G_{S_{i}}
, we see that
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(S_{i},B_{S_{i}}+G_{S_{i}})
.

We set
\Delta_{S_{i}}=B_{S_{i}}+G_{S_{i}}
.
By the above argument and Theorem
2.19
, for any
i\geq m_{d}
, the divisors
\Delta_{S_{i}}
and
H_{S_{i}}
satisfy the following properties.

•

(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}})
is a normal pair defined by adjunction

K_{S_{i}}+\Delta_{S_{i}}=(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i})|_{S_{i}},

•

(S_{i},\Delta^{<1}_{S_{i}}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{S_{i}})
is a dlt pair defined by adjunction

K_{S_{i}}+\Delta^{<1}_{S_{i}}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{S_{i}}=(K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta^{<1}_{i}+{\rm Supp}\,\Delta^{\geq 1}_{i})|_{S_{i}},

•

the birational map
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is small and an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}})
, and

•

the birational transforms of
\Delta_{S_{i}}
and
H_{S_{i}}
on
S_{i+1}
are equal to
\Delta_{S_{i+1}}
and
H_{S_{i+1}}
, respectively.

Step 4
.

In this step, we consider the case when the image of
S_{m_{d}}
on
X_{m_{d}}
is contained in
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})
.

By Remark
3.6
and the hypothesis
{\rm NNef}(\omega+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
of Theorem
5.2
, the non-isomorphic locus of the
(\omega+A)
-MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
.
Thus, we have
{\rm NNef}(\omega_{i}+A_{i}/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X_{i},\omega_{i})=\emptyset
.
Since
K_{Y_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+H_{i}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}f_{i}^{*}(\omega_{i}+A_{i})
, if the image of
S_{m_{d}}
on
X_{m_{d}}
is contained in
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})
, then
K_{S_{i}}+\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}}
is nef over
Z
for any
i\geq m_{d}
.
For any
m_{d}\leq i\leq j
, by taking a common resolution of
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{j}
and using the negativity lemma, we see that any prime divisor
P^{\prime}
over
S_{i}
satisfies

a(P^{\prime},S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}})=a(P^{\prime},S_{j},\Delta_{S_{j}}+H_{S_{j}}).

Since
Y_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow Y_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{m_{d}}}+\Delta_{m_{d}}+H_{m_{d}})
-MMP over
Z
, by the same argument as
[
KM98
, Proof of Lemma 3.38]
, the above equality on the discrepancies implies that
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{j}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
S_{i}
for any
m_{d}\leq i\leq j
.

Step 5
.

From this step to Step
7
, we consider the case when the image of
S_{m_{d}}
on
X_{m_{d}}
is not contained in
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})
.
Then the image of
S_{i}
on
X_{i}
is not contained in
{\rm Nqlc}(X_{i},\omega_{i})
for any
i\geq m_{d}
.
We note that this condition is used for Theorem
2.25
.
In this step we construct a sequence of small birational maps

T_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow T_{i}\dashrightarrow T_{i+1}\dashrightarrow\cdots

that will be used to prove the existence of
m_{d+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
of the statement in Step
2
.

For each
i\geq m_{d}
, we have the diagram

\textstyle{(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i}}
\textstyle{(Y_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1}+H_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i+1}}
\textstyle{[X_{i},\omega_{i}+A_{i}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\textstyle{[X_{i+1},\omega_{i+1}+A_{i+1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{V_{i}}

over
Z
such that
\varphi_{i}\colon X_{i}\to V_{i}
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{i}\colon X_{i+1}\to V_{i}
form a step of an
(\omega_{i}+A_{i})
-MMP over
Z
in Definition
3.4
.
In other words,
\varphi_{i}
is a projective birational morphism,
\varphi^{\prime}_{i}
is a projective small birational morphism, and both
-(\omega_{i}+A_{i})
and
\omega_{i+1}+A_{i+1}
are ample over
V_{i}
.
Let
g_{i}\colon S_{i}\to T_{i}
(resp.
g_{i+1}\colon S_{i+1}\to T_{i+1}
) be the Stein factorization of the restriction
f_{i}|_{S_{i}}
(resp.
f_{i+1}|_{S_{i+1}}
).
Let
\psi_{i}\colon T_{i}\to W_{i}
be the Stein factorization of
T_{i}\to V_{i}
.
Then the morphism
T_{i+1}\to V_{i}
factors through a contraction
T_{i+1}\to W_{i}
, which we denote by
\psi^{\prime}_{i}
.
Let
\omega_{T_{i}}
(resp.
\omega_{T_{i+1}}
) be the pullback of
\omega_{i}
(resp.
\omega_{i+1}
) to
T_{i}
(resp.
T_{i+1}
), and
let
A_{T_{i}}
(resp.
A_{T_{i+1}}
) be the pullback of
A_{i}
(resp.
A_{i+1}
) to
T_{i}
(resp.
T_{i+1}
).
Then we have

g_{i}^{*}A_{T_{i}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}H_{S_{i}}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad g_{i+1}^{*}A_{T_{i+1}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}H_{S_{i+1}},

and we have the following diagram

\textstyle{(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{i}}
\textstyle{(S_{i+1},\Delta_{S_{i+1}}+H_{S_{i+1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{i+1}}
\textstyle{[T_{i},\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{i}}
\textstyle{[T_{i+1},\omega_{T_{i+1}}+A_{T_{i+1}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{W_{i},}

where
(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}})\overset{g_{i}}{\longrightarrow}[T_{i},\omega_{T_{i}}]
and
(S_{i+1},\Delta_{S_{i+1}})\overset{g_{i+1}}{\longrightarrow}[T_{i+1},\omega_{T_{i+1}}]

are quasi-log schemes induced by normal pairs (Theorem
2.25
), and the divisors
-(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}})
and
\omega_{T_{i+1}}+A_{T_{i+1}}
are ample over
W_{i}
.
We recall that
{\rm lim}_{j\to\infty}\lambda_{j}=0
, where

\lambda_{j}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{j}+A_{j}+\mu A_{j}$ is nef over $Z$}\}.

Then
K_{S_{j}}+\Delta_{S_{j}}+H_{S_{j}}+tH_{S_{j}}
is semi-ample over
Z
for all
j>i
and
t\in(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j-1}]
.
Because
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{j}
is small and the birational transforms of
\Delta_{S_{i}}
and
H_{S_{i}}
on
S_{j}
are equal to
\Delta_{S_{j}}
and
H_{S_{j}}
respectively,
K_{S_{i}}+\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}}
is the limit of movable divisors over
Z
.
Hence,
\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}
is pseudo-effective over
Z
.
This implies that
\psi_{i}
is birational.
