By construction of the diagram,
\psi^{\prime}_{i}\circ g_{i+1}
coincides with
\psi_{i}\circ g_{i}
over the generic point of
W_{i}
.
Thus
\psi^{\prime}_{i}
is also birational.
If there exists a
\psi_{i}
-exceptional prime divisor
Q
on
T_{i}
, then we can find a prime divisor
P
on
S_{i}
such that
g_{i}(P)=Q
and
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is not an isomorphism on the generic point of
P
.
This contradicts the fact that
S_{i}\dashrightarrow S_{i+1}
is small.
Therefore
\psi_{i}
is small.
By the same argument, we see that
\psi^{\prime}_{i}
is small.
Hence,
T_{i}\dashrightarrow T_{i+1}
is small, and the birational transform of
\omega_{T_{i}}
(resp.
A_{T_{i}}
) on
T_{i+1}
is equal to
\omega_{T_{i+1}}
(resp.
A_{T_{i+1}}
).
Therefore, the sequence of birational maps

T_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow T_{i}\dashrightarrow T_{i+1}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of an
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})
-MMP over
Z
in Definition
3.4
.
Moreover, it follows that the
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})
-MMP is an
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A_{T_{m_{d}}}
in Definition
3.4
.
Indeed, define

\nu_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}+\mu A_{T_{i}}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq m_{d}
, and pick any curve
C_{i}\subset T_{i}
contracted by
\psi_{i}\colon T_{i}\to W_{i}
.
Then
\nu_{i}\leq\lambda_{i}
, where
\lambda_{i}
is the nonnegative real number defined in Step
1
.
Now
(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}})\,\cdot\,C_{i}<0
and
(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}+\lambda_{i}A_{T_{i}})\,\cdot\,C_{i}=0
by construction.
They imply
(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}+\nu_{i}A_{T_{i}})\,\cdot\,C_{i}\leq 0
.
We also have
(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}+\nu_{i}A_{T_{i}})\,\cdot\,C_{i}\geq 0
by the definition of
\nu_{i}
.
Therefore, it follows that
(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}+\nu_{i}A_{T_{i}})\,\cdot\,C_{i}=0,
which is the condition of the MMP with scaling in Definition
3.4
.

By these discussions, the sequence of small birational maps

T_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow T_{i}\dashrightarrow T_{i+1}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of an
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A_{T_{m_{d}}}
.

Step 6
.

As in Step
5
, we define

\nu_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}+\mu A_{T_{i}}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq m_{d}
.
By construction of the diagram in Step
1
(see also Theorem
3.10
), there exists
\lambda^{\prime}\in(\lambda_{m_{d}},\lambda_{m_{d}-1})
such that
\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}}
is ample over
Z
.
In this step, we prove
\nu_{m^{\prime}}=0
for some
m^{\prime}\geq m_{d}
, in other words, we prove that
T_{i}\dashrightarrow T_{i+1}
is an isomorphism for every
i\geq m^{\prime}
(see also Remark
3.5
).
We will use Lemma
5.1
.

We first prove that
{\rm NNef}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}/Z)\cap{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})=\emptyset
.
We pick an arbitrary
t\in(0,\lambda^{\prime})
.
We may write

(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})+t(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}})=(1+t)\left(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}A_{T_{m_{d}}}\right).

Since
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
, we can find
i^{\prime}
such that
\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}\in(\lambda_{i^{\prime}},\lambda_{i^{\prime}-1}]
.
Since
\omega_{T_{i^{\prime}}}+A_{T_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}A_{T_{i^{\prime}}}
is semi-ample over
Z
and

K_{S_{i^{\prime}}}+\Delta_{S_{i^{\prime}}}+H_{S_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}H_{S_{i^{\prime}}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}g_{i^{\prime}}^{*}(\omega_{T_{i^{\prime}}}+A_{T_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}A_{T_{i^{\prime}}}),

it follows that
K_{S_{i^{\prime}}}+\Delta_{S_{i^{\prime}}}+H_{S_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}H_{S_{i^{\prime}}}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
Now the birational map
S_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow S_{i^{\prime}}
is small and an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
{\rm Nklt}(S_{m_{d}},\Delta_{S_{m_{d}}})
.
Thus

{\rm Bs}|K_{S_{m_{d}}}+\Delta_{S_{m_{d}}}+H_{S_{m_{d}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}H_{S_{m_{d}}}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nklt}(S_{m_{d}},\Delta_{S_{m_{d}}})=\emptyset.

Since

K_{S_{m_{d}}}+\Delta_{S_{m_{d}}}+H_{S_{m_{d}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}H_{S_{m_{d}}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}g_{m_{d}}^{*}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}A_{T_{m_{d}}})

and
g_{m_{d}}({\rm Nklt}(S_{m_{d}},\Delta_{S_{m_{d}}}))={\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
set-theoretically, we have

\begin{split}&{\rm Bs}|(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})+t(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}})/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})\\
=&{\rm Bs}|\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\frac{\lambda^{\prime}t}{1+t}A_{T_{m_{d}}}/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})=\emptyset.\end{split}

Since
t\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
is arbitrary,
{\rm NNef}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}/Z)\cap{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})=\emptyset
.

Next we prove that
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
We recall the hypothesis of Theorem
5.2
that

{\rm NNef}(\omega+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)=\emptyset
.
This condition and Remark
3.6
show that the non-isomorphic locus of the
(\omega+A)
-MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)
.
Recall also that
(\omega+A)|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X,\omega)}
is semi-ample over
Z
, which is the hypothesis of Theorem
5.2
.
Therefore,
(\omega_{m_{d}}+A_{m_{d}})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
Applying Theorem
2.25
to
[Y_{m_{d}},\Delta_{m_{d}}]\to(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})
and
S_{m_{d}}
, we see that
T_{m_{d}}\to X_{m_{d}}
induces a morphism
{\rm Nqlc}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})\to{\rm Nqlc}(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})
of closed subschemes. Then
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})}
is the pullback of
(\omega_{m_{d}}+A_{m_{d}})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(X_{m_{d}},\omega_{m_{d}})}
by this morphism.
Thus,
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{{\rm Nqlc}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
If
{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
coincides with
{\rm Nqlc}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
as a closed subscheme of
T_{m_{d}}
, then
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})}
is clearly semi-ample over
Z
. If
{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
does not coincide with
{\rm Nqlc}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
as a closed subscheme of
T_{m_{d}}
, then there exists a qlc center of
[T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}]
.
We put

T^{\prime}={\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})

and we apply the adjunction for quasi-log schemes (
[
F17
, Theorem 6.1.2 (i)]
). We may define the structure of a quasi-log scheme
[T^{\prime},\omega_{T^{\prime}}]
on
T^{\prime}
such that
\omega_{T^{\prime}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}|_{T^{\prime}}
,
{\rm Nqlc}(T^{\prime},\omega_{T^{\prime}})={\rm Nqlc}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
, and the qlc centers of
[T^{\prime},\omega_{T^{\prime}}]
are exactly the qlc centers of
[T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}]
contained in
T^{\prime}
. Now
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{T^{\prime}}
is nef over
Z
because the non-nef locus of
\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}
over
Z
does not intersect
T^{\prime}={\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})
.
Then

(1+\lambda^{\prime})(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{T^{\prime}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\lambda^{\prime}\left(\omega_{T^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{T^{\prime}}\right).

Hence, we may apply Theorem
2.21
to
[T^{\prime},\omega_{T^{\prime}}]
and
\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{T^{\prime}}
, and we see that

(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{{\rm Nqklt}(T_{m_{d}},\omega_{T_{m_{d}}})}=(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})|_{T^{\prime}}

is semi-ample over
Z
.

We have

(1+\lambda^{\prime})(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})=\lambda^{\prime}\left(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}})\right).

We put
A^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime}}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}+\lambda^{\prime}A_{T_{m_{d}}})
.
Then

\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A^{\prime}=\frac{1+\lambda^{\prime}}{\lambda^{\prime}}(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}}).

By Remark
3.6
, the
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A_{T_{m_{d}}})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A_{T_{m_{d}}}

T_{m_{d}}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow T_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is also a sequence of steps of an
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A^{\prime})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A^{\prime}
.

By the above discussions, we may apply Lemma
5.1
to the
(\omega_{T_{m_{d}}}+A^{\prime})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
5.1
, we have
\nu_{m^{\prime}}=0
for some
m^{\prime}\geq m_{d}
.

Step 7
.

In this step we prove that
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{j}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
S_{i}
for any
m^{\prime}\leq i\leq j
, where
m^{\prime}
is the integer in Step
6
, which satisfies
\nu_{m^{\prime}}=0
.

We recall that

(Y_{m_{d}},\Delta_{m_{d}}+H_{m_{d}})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{m_{d}}}+\Delta_{m_{d}}+H_{m_{d}})
-MMP over
Z
and the restriction of this sequence to
S_{m_{d}}
is a sequence of small birational maps

(S_{m_{d}},\Delta_{S_{m_{d}}}+H_{S_{m_{d}}})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}})\dashrightarrow\cdots

such that

K_{S_{i}}+\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}g_{i}^{*}(\omega_{T_{i}}+A_{T_{i}}),

where
(S_{i},\Delta_{S_{i}})\overset{g_{i}}{\longrightarrow}[T_{i},\omega_{T_{i}}]
is the structure of a quasi-log scheme induced by a normal pair.
By Step
6
, the divisor
K_{S_{i}}+\Delta_{S_{i}}+H_{S_{i}}
is nef over
Z
for any
i\geq m^{\prime}
.
By the same argument as in Step
4
, the birational map
Y_{i}\dashrightarrow Y_{j}
is an isomorphism on a neighborhood of
S_{i}
for any
m^{\prime}\leq i\leq j
.

Step 8
.

In this step we complete the argument of the special termination by finding
m_{d+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
in the statement of Step
2
.

By Step
4
and Step
7
, for any
(d+1)
-dimensional lc center of
(Y_{m_{d}},\Delta_{m_{d}})
, we can find
m_{d+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
in the statement of Step
2
.
In other words, the non-isomorphic locus of the
(K_{Y_{m_{d+1}}}+\Delta_{m_{d+1}}+H_{m_{d+1}})
-MMP over
Z

(Y_{m_{d+1}},\Delta_{m_{d+1}}+H_{m_{d+1}})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is disjoint from any lc center of
(Y_{m_{d+1}},\Delta_{m_{d+1}})
whose dimension is less than or equal to
d+1
.
By an induction on
0\leq d<{\rm dim}\,Y
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that each step of the
(K_{Y_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+H_{m})
-MMP over
Z

(Y_{m},\Delta_{m}+H_{m})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(Y_{i},\Delta_{i}+H_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is an isomorphism on an open subset containing all lc centers of
(Y_{m},\Delta_{m})
.
Since the non-isomorphic locus of the
(K_{Y_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+H_{m})
-MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(Y_{m},\Delta_{m})
, the non-isomorphic locus of the
(K_{Y_{m}}+\Delta_{m}+H_{m})
-MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nklt}(Y_{m},\Delta_{m})
.

Step 9
.

In this step we prove
\lambda_{n}=0
for some
n\geq m
.

By the same argument as in Step
6
, we have
{\rm NNef}(\omega_{m}+A_{m}/Z)\cap{\rm Nqklt}(X_{m},\omega_{m})=\emptyset
and
(\omega_{m}+A_{m})|_{{\rm Nqklt}(X_{m},\omega_{m})}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
We pick
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in(\lambda_{m},\lambda_{m-1})
such that
\omega_{m}+A_{m}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}A_{m}
is ample over
Z
.
We have

(1+\lambda^{\prime\prime})(\omega_{m}+A_{m})=\lambda^{\prime\prime}\left(\omega_{m}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(\omega_{m}+A_{m}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}A_{m})\right).

We put

A^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(\omega_{m}+A_{m}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}A_{m}).

By Remark
3.6
, the
(\omega_{m}+A_{m})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A_{m}

X_{m}\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow X_{i}\dashrightarrow\cdots

is also a sequence of steps of an
(\omega_{m}+A^{\prime\prime})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A^{\prime\prime}
.
We may apply Lemma
5.1
to the
(\omega_{m}+A^{\prime\prime})
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A^{\prime\prime}
.
By Lemma
5.1
, we have
\lambda_{n}=0
for some
n\geq m
.

By the same argument as in Step
9
in this proof, we have

(1+\frac{\lambda_{n-1}}{2})(\omega_{n}+A_{n})=\frac{\lambda_{n-1}}{2}\left(\omega_{n}+\frac{2}{\lambda_{n-1}}(\omega_{n}+A_{n}+\frac{\lambda_{n-1}}{2}A_{n})\right)

and
(\omega_{n}+A_{n})|_{{\rm Nqklt}(X_{n},\omega_{n})}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
Now
\lambda_{n-1}>0
and
\omega_{n}+A_{n}+\frac{\lambda_{n-1}}{2}A_{n}
is ample over
Z
by construction in Step
1
.
By applying Theorem
2.21
to
[T_{n},\omega_{n}]
and
\frac{2}{\lambda_{n-1}}(\omega_{n}+A_{n}+\frac{\lambda_{n-1}}{2}A_{n})
, we see that the divisor
\omega_{n}+A_{n}
of the resulting quasi-log scheme
[X_{n},\omega_{n}+A_{n}]
is semi-ample over
Z
.
We finish the proof.
∎

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
We put
(X_{1},B_{1}):=(X,\Delta+A)
.
Let
H
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
.
Then there exists a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
H

\textstyle{(X_{1},B_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},B_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{m},B_{m}),}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(X_{i},B_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})
is a step of a
(K_{X_{i}}+B_{i})
-MMP over
Z
, such that

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

\rho(X_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
, and

•

K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}
is semi-ample over
Z
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Proof.

We pick
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
A-\epsilon H
is
\pi
-semi-ample.
Then we can find a member
\Delta^{\prime}
of
|\Delta+A-\epsilon H/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta^{\prime})={\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
as closed subschemes of
X
.
By replacing
\Delta
,
A
, and
H
with
\Delta^{\prime}
,
\epsilon H
, and
\epsilon H
respectively, we may assume
A=H
.

By Corollary
3.12
, there exists a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A

\textstyle{(X_{1},B_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},B_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots,}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(X_{i},B_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})
is a step of a
(K_{X_{i}}+B_{i})
-MMP over
Z
, satisfying the following.

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

\rho(X_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
,

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{X_{i}}+B_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
and
\lambda:={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
, then the MMP terminates after finitely many steps or otherwise
\lambda\neq\lambda_{i}
for every
i\geq 1
, and

•

if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

If is sufficient to prove that
\lambda_{m}=0
for some
m
and
K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}
is semi-ample over
Z
.

By Theorem
5.2
, there exists a sequence of steps of an
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A

(X_{1},B_{1})=:(X^{\prime}_{1},B^{\prime}_{1})\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime}_{2},B^{\prime}_{2})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime}_{n},B^{\prime}_{n})

such that
K_{X^{\prime}_{n}}+B^{\prime}_{n}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
We put

\lambda^{\prime}_{j}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{X^{\prime}_{j}}+B^{\prime}_{j}+\mu A^{\prime}_{j}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
1\leq j\leq n
, where
A^{\prime}_{j}
is the birational transform of
A
on
X^{\prime}_{j}
.
Then we can find
l
such that
\lambda^{\prime}_{l}\leq\lambda<\lambda^{\prime}_{l-1}
.
Then we can find
m
such that
\lambda_{m}<\lambda_{m-1}\leq\lambda^{\prime}_{l-1}
.
Such
m
exists since the
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP
(X_{1},B_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},B_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots
terminates after finitely many steps or otherwise
\lambda\neq\lambda_{i}
for every
i\geq 1
.
Let
f_{1}\colon Y\to X_{1}
,
f_{m}\colon Y\to X_{m}
, and
f^{\prime}_{l}\colon Y\to X^{\prime}_{l}
be a common resolution of
X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{m}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}_{l}
.
We have

\lambda^{\prime}_{l}\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_{m}<\lambda_{m-1}\leq\lambda^{\prime}_{l-1}.

For any
t\in(\lambda_{m},\lambda_{m-1})
, we can write

f_{1}^{*}(K_{X_{1}}+B_{1}+tA_{1})=f_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}+tA_{m})+E^{(t)}

with an effective
f_{m}
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
E^{(t)}
on
Y
.
We can also write

f_{1}^{*}(K_{X_{1}}+B_{1}+tA_{1})=f^{\prime*}_{l}(K_{X^{\prime}_{l}}+B^{\prime}_{l}+tA^{\prime}_{l})+F^{(t)}

with an effective
f^{\prime}_{l}
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
F^{(t)}
on
Y
.
Then

f_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}+tA_{m})+E^{(t)}=f^{\prime*}_{l}(K_{X^{\prime}_{l}}+B^{\prime}_{l}+tA^{\prime}_{l})+F^{(t)}.

Since
f_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}+tA_{m})
and
f^{\prime*}_{l}(K_{X^{\prime}_{l}}+B^{\prime}_{l}+tA^{\prime}_{l})
are nef over
Z
, the negativity lemma implies
E^{(t)}=F^{(t)}
for any
t\in(\lambda_{m},\lambda_{m-1})
.
Then

f_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}+tA_{m})=f^{\prime*}_{l}(K_{X^{\prime}_{l}}+B^{\prime}_{l}+tA^{\prime}_{l})

for any
t\in(\lambda_{m},\lambda_{m-1})
, and therefore

f_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}+\lambda^{\prime}_{l}A_{m})=f^{\prime*}_{l}(K_{X^{\prime}_{l}}+B^{\prime}_{l}+\lambda^{\prime}_{l}A^{\prime}_{l}).

This shows that
K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}+\lambda^{\prime}_{l}A_{m}
is nef over
Z
.
By the choice of
l
and the definition of
\lambda_{m}
, we have
\lambda_{m}\leq\lambda^{\prime}_{l}\leq\lambda
.
This implies
\lambda_{m}=\lambda=0
because the
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP

(X_{1},B_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},B_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

terminates after finitely many steps or otherwise
\lambda\neq\lambda_{i}
for every
i\geq 1
.
Then
l=n
since
\lambda^{\prime}_{l}\leq\lambda=0
.
Now we have

f_{m}^{*}(K_{X_{m}}+B_{m})=f^{\prime*}_{n}(K_{X^{\prime}_{n}}+B^{\prime}_{n})

and
K_{X^{\prime}_{n}}+B^{\prime}_{n}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
Thus,
K_{X_{m}}+B_{m}
is semi-ample over
Z
.
We finish the proof.
∎

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.
Suppose in addition that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Let

(X,\Delta+A)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1}+A_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+A_{i})\dashrightarrow\cdots

be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A
such that if we put

\lambda_{i}:=\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$(K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+A_{i})+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.
Then
\lambda_{m}=0
for some
m
and
(X_{m},\Delta_{m}+A_{m})
is a good minimal model of
(X,\Delta+A)
over
Z
.

Proof.

The proof of Theorem
5.3
works without any change.
∎

5.2.
Proofs of corollaries

In this subsection we prove corollaries.

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair and let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of an
\mathbb{R}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then
{\rm Bs}|K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z|_{\mathbb{R}}\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

This immediately follows from Theorem
5.3
.
∎

i\geq 1
, then
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.12
, there exists a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
with scaling of
A

\textstyle{(X,\Delta)=:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots,}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
is a step of a
(K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i})
-MMP over
Z
, such that

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

\rho(X_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
,

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
and
\lambda:={\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}
, then the MMP terminates after finitely many steps or otherwise
\lambda\neq\lambda_{i}
for every
i\geq 1
, and

•

if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Then the MMP is a
(K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda A)
-over
Z
with scaling of
A
.
If
\lambda>0
, then we have
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda A/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
, and
(K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
is ample over
Z
because
(K_{X}+\Delta)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
is nef over
Z
and we may use
[
KM98
, Proof of Theorem 1.38]
and Nakai–Moishezon’s criterion for the ampleness.
This contradicts Theorem
5.4
, and therefore
\lambda=0
.
This MMP satisfies the conditions of Corollary
5.7
.
∎

Corollary 5.8

(cf.
[
G11
]
)
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective normal pair such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is pseudo-effective and the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.
Suppose that the numerical dimension
\kappa_{\sigma}(K_{X}+\Delta)
(see
[
N04
, V, 2.5. Definition]
) is zero.
Put
X_{1}=X
and
\Delta_{1}=\Delta
.
Then there exists a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP

\textstyle{(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{m},\Delta_{m}),}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
is a step of the
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, such that

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

\rho(X_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
, and

•

K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}\equiv 0
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are also
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.7
, there exists a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP with scaling of an ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
A

\textstyle{(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(X_{m},\Delta_{m}),}
\textstyle{W_{i}}

where
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\to W_{i}\leftarrow(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
is a step of the
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
Z
, such that

•

the non-isomorphic locus of the MMP is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
,

•

\rho(X_{i}/W_{i})=1
for every
\geq 1
, and

•

if we put

\lambda_{i}:={\rm inf}\{\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\,|\,\text{$K_{X_{i}}+\Delta_{i}+\mu A_{i}$ is nef over $Z$}\}

for each
i\geq 1
, then
{\rm lim}_{i\to\infty}\lambda_{i}=0
.

Moreover, if
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then all
X_{i}
in the MMP are
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.
Prime divisors contracted by the MMP are components of the negative part of the Nakayama–Zariski decomposition of
K_{X}+\Delta
.
Hence, there exists
m
such that
K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}
is the limit of movable divisors.
By the same argument as in
[
G11
, Proof of Theorem 5.1]
, we see that
K_{X_{m}}+\Delta_{m}\equiv 0
.
The MMP satisfies the properties of Corollary
5.8
.
∎

Corollary 5.9

(cf.
[
BBP13
]
,
[
TX23
]
)
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective normal pair such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is pseudo-effective and the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
is disjoint from
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
.
Then

{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta)=\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(K_{X}+\Delta).

Furthermore, every irreducible component of the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta
is uniruled.

Proof.

The argument in
[
BBP13
]
or
[
TX23
]
works with no changes because we may use Corollary
5.7
and
[
F21
, Theorem 1.12]
.
∎

Corollary 5.10
.

Let
\pi\colon X\to Z
be a projective morphism of normal quasi-projective varieties.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a normal pair such that
\Delta
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
.
Let
A
be a
\pi
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
such that
K_{X}+\Delta+A
is
\pi
-pseudo-effective.
Suppose that
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+A/Z)\cap{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)=\emptyset
and that
(K_{X}+\Delta+A)|_{{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)}
, which we think of a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle on
{\rm Nlc}(X,\Delta)
, is semi-ample over
Z
.
Then the sheaf of graded
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra

\underset{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}{\bigoplus}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(\lfloor m(K_{X}+\Delta+A)\rfloor)

is finitely generated.

Proof.

This immediately follows from Theorem
5.3
.
∎

6.
Examples

In this section we collect some examples.

The first example shows that a step of an MMP for normal pairs does not always exist and the abundance conjecture for normal pairs does not hold in general.

Example 6.1
.

Let
E
be an elliptic curve with a very ample divisor
H
and define

X:=\mathbb{P}_{E}(\mathcal{O}_{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{E}(-H))\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}E.

Let
S
be the unique section of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
.
Then
K_{X}+S+(S+f^{*}H)\sim 0
and
S+f^{*}H
is base point free.
The contraction

\pi\colon X\to Z

induced by
S+f^{*}H

is birational and we have
{\rm Ex}(\pi)=S
.
Thus
-S
is
\pi
-ample.
Let
D
be a non-torsion Cartier divisor on
E
of degree zero.
Since
f^{*}D+S+f^{*}H
is nef and big, we can write

f^{*}D+S+f^{*}H\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}A+B

such that
A
is an effective ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor and
B
is an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
.
Now we have

K_{X}+S+B+A\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}f^{*}D

and
D
is not semi-ample.

•

With notation as above, we consider
\pi\colon X\to Z
and the pair
(X,2S+B+A)
.
Since
-S
is
\pi
-ample,
\pi
is a
(K_{X}+2S+B+A)
-negative extremal contraction.
However, the divisor

\pi_{*}(K_{X}+2S+B+A)\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\pi_{*}f^{*}D

is not
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier since
rH
is not
\mathbb{R}
-linearly equivalent to
D
for any
r\in\mathbb{R}
(cf.
[
F17
, Proposition 7.2.8]
).
This example implies that we cannot always construct a step of an MMP even in the case of surfaces.

•

With notation as above, consider the pair
(X,S+B+A)
.
By construction,
K_{X}+S+B+A
is nef but not semi-ample.
This pair shows that the abundance conjecture does not always hold for normal pairs with polarizations.

The next example shows that the non-nef locus of the log canonical divisor of a normal pair is not necessarily Zariski closed.
In particular, minimal models of normal pairs do not always exist.

Example 6.2
.

For a blow up
V\to\mathbb{P}^{3}
at nine very general points and

X:=\mathbb{P}_{V}(\mathcal{O}_{V}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{V}(1))\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}V,

where
\mathcal{O}_{V}(1)
is very ample line bundle on
V
, Lesieutre
[
L14
, Theorem 1.1]
constructed a big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D
on
X
such that

\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D)=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{$A$: ample}\\
\text{$D+A$: $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier}\end{subarray}}{\rm Bs}|D+A|_{\mathbb{Q}}

is a countable union of curves.
By construction, there is an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta_{V}
on
V
such that
K_{V}+\Delta_{V}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}0
, and therefore
K_{X}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R}}0
for some effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta
on
X
.
We can write
D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}A+B
such that
A
is an effective ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor and
B
is an effective
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
.
Then

K_{X}+\Delta+B+A\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D.

Moreover, by
[
N04
, V, 1.3 Theorem]
, it follows that
\boldsymbol{\rm B}_{-}(D)
coincides with the non-nef locus of
D
.
Therefore, the pair
(X,\Delta+B+A)
satisfies the condition that the non-nef locus of
K_{X}+\Delta+B+A
is not Zariski closed.
If
(X,\Delta+B+A)
has a minimal model
(X^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
, taking a common resolution
g\colon Y\to X
and
g^{\prime}\colon Y\to X^{\prime}
of
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
, then we can write

g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+B+A)=g^{\prime*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime})+E

for some effective
g^{\prime}
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
E
on
Y
.
Since
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}
is nef, we have

{\rm NNef}(g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+B+A))={\rm NNef}(g^{\prime*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime})+E)={\rm Supp}\,E.

Then
{\rm NNef}(K_{X}+\Delta+B+A)=g({\rm NNef}(g^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+B+A)))=g({\rm Supp}\,E)
is Zariski closed, a contradiction.
Therefore,
(X,\Delta+B+A)
does not have a minimal model.
